| 293 | **Queue-mode rendering — fal's queue API for slow engines + real progress (owner diagnosis 2026-08-31: "the problem isn't the slowness, the problem is that it wasn't apparent anything was happening").** TLDR: the render proxy is synchronous (fal.run), so any engine slower than Fly's ~60s proxy idle cutoff can NEVER deliver — the 55s timeout makes that failure loud, but it permanently locks out slow engines (the Seedream 4-ref party edit ran >120s). fal's queue API (queue.fal.run: submit → request_id → poll status → fetch result) fixes both halves: no long-held connection (each poll is a fast request, immune to the idle cutoff) and REAL progress (queue position / in-progress status → the client status line shows actual state, not a hope). Build: server routes `/api/render-queue/submit|status|result` (same allowlist/entitlement/metering; meter on submit), client falFetch grows a queued variant used when the engine declares `slow:true` in RENDER_MODELS (table data, per the idiom), status line shows queue state + the v1.756 elapsed ticker. The 55s sync path stays for fast engines (nano ~14s) — no queue latency tax on the common case. | M | Any | ✅ **SHIPPED v1.757** (2026-08-31, same session as filing — owner green-light "throw that in now"). Server: `/api/render-queue/submit/*` (same allowlist/entitlement/cap, metered at submit) + `/api/render-queue/poll` (one status/result GET, HOST-PINNED to the queue base — the SSRF door stays shut); battery 54. Client: `falQueueRender` (api.js — server or BYOK route, 5-min give-up, status callback), Seedream's entry carries `slow:true`, doRender branches on it and the v1.756 ticker's base text now shows REAL state ("queued (#2)" / "rendering") with elapsed seconds; transport battery 37. Portrait paths deliberately stay sync (solo t2i fits the window). |

| 286 | **W2 same-scene overflow latch is unclearable by the summary its own refusal copy prescribes (joint review f59, verified against current identity.js 2026-08-29).** TLDR: when a big-cast scene overflows `SCENE_REF_ACTOR_CAP`=10 (or the negatives cap) WITHOUT a node change, `_sceneRefOverflow` stamps the latch with the ACTIVE scene serial, and `sceneRefsSummarySuccess` clears overflow only when `overflow.scene!==active.scene` — so every death in that scene fails closed (`w2DeathAuthorized` returns false even for fully-evidenced bound actors; `sceneRefBind`/`sceneRefExclude` also refuse, so the GM cannot even record the evidence to heal), while the refusal reason AND the prompt block both prescribe a structured summary that CANNOT clear it. The capacity-path refusal also suppresses `_w2ArmDeathValve`, so the `[NPC_DEATH_REPORTED:]` fork note never fires — the misleading summary prescription is the only guidance the GM gets. Real exits today, taught nowhere: any NODE change (incl. entering/leaving a sublocation — verifier) followed by a summary, or `[NPC_DEATH_REPORTED:]`. Verified: entry-13's bounded sealed-frame buffer did NOT touch this — it covers transitions UNDER a latch, not the same-scene latch itself; the pinned test covers only transitioned-overflow recovery. **Design fork needing an owner ruling (the fail-closed breadth was deliberate):** (a) a summary success under a same-scene latch honestly recovers capacity (clear + re-latch on the next over-cap event — the argument `sceneRefsSummarySuccess` already accepts for sealed frames); (b) narrow the latch so already-BOUND actors' deaths stay authorized and only new registrations refuse; (c) minimum: the same-scene refusal copy names the real exits. Missing test either way: summary while the latched scene is still active. **RULED 2026-08-29 (owner, via chip): option (a) + (c) — a successful structured summary under a same-scene latch clears the overflow (re-latching allowed on the next over-cap event), AND the refusal copy/prompt block name the real exits.** | M | Fable | ✅ **SHIPPED v1.749** (2026-08-29) — `sceneRefsSummarySuccess` clears overflow UNCONDITIONALLY (ordering keeps fail-closed honest: the clear runs at the END of `applySummaryExtract`, after `w2ValidateSummary`, so the clearing summary's own death claims still validate UNDER the latch — the #168R7 order pin stays green); the refusal reason (all 3 sites incl. the #213 registry row, census green) and the EVIDENCE CAPACITY WARNING prompt line now teach all three exits (summary / scene move incl. sublocation / `[NPC_DEATH_REPORTED:]`); the capacity path still deliberately never arms the death valve. 2 failing-first engine tests (same-scene clear + re-latch + bound-death-after-recovery; copy exits at both surfaces), suite 1851 green; 3 new sabotage clauses in dev/sabotage-w2.js, 3/3 proven at authoring, byte-identical restore. |

| 253 | **THE PAGE-HIDE FLUSH IS SIZE-BOUNDED — and the turns it can't flush are pushed at next launch (JP0-11, joint review 2026-08-27; Fable f68, verified ×2).** TLDR: closing or backgrounding the game was documented as never dropping the final turn — and that guarantee was dead for almost every real save, silently. Mechanism: the unload/page-hide flush rides `fetch(keepalive:true)`; the Fetch spec caps total in-flight keepalive request BODIES at 64 KiB, browsers reject anything larger, and the rejection landed in a `.catch(function(){})` — no console line, no badge, no toast, `_onSyncFail` never called. Not a mature-campaign edge case either: the PC's base64 portrait rides **inline** by design (`_stripNpcPortraits` strips only NPC avatars), so a 40–100KB data URL clears the cap from roughly turn 1. The loss is cross-device: the unflushed turns stay local, and a second device that then advances PAST them makes this device's next reconcile adopt the server copy wholesale (if it does NOT advance past them, the same silent flush failure instead surfaces as the loud CAS 409 — a different, visible face of one defect). Shipped: over `FLUSH_KEEPALIVE_MAX` (56KB, measured in BYTES — `payload.length` is a character count and under-reports the multi-byte `{__lz}` transcript) the doomed request is **not attempted**; the flush marks the campaign in `tnd_sync_dirty_v1` (`{campId: turn}`, `store` wrapper, cap 8) and says so in the console. The next `load()` **pushes before `_reconcileFromServer` may adopt**, deferring — never blocking — the reconcile; the CAS turn guard is untouched, so a genuine two-device conflict still pauses loudly. The marker clears ONLY on a confirmed 2xx of our own payload at a turn ≥ the marked one; one toast per page load. CLAUDE.md §16's false guarantee line was corrected in the same commit. | S | Fable | ✅ **SHIPPED v1.720** (2026-08-28) — 14 failing-first assertions (5 synchronous in the suite + 9 in the new standalone `dev/tests-jp011-flush-dirty.js`, which drives `load()`'s wholesale global rewrite); mutation proof `dev/sabotage-jp011-flush-dirty.js` **10/10**, byte-identical restore. Field watch: the boot toast is the visible proof the marker round-trips on a real device. |

| 231 | **THE ARC WALL — an emergent thread dies with the arc that bore it (owner observation + HARD-WALL ruling 2026-08-24, after finishing the first blueprint campaign).** TLDR: the owner finished Runelords and reported emergent quests as wildly uneven — “the candy heist was a lot of fun” vs “The Bells, and the closed eye, ‘Spire’ just dragged on and on” — ending with **“by the end of that campaign I just wanted it over, and that is the OPPOSITE of how a player should feel near the end”** (the north star: that feeling is a measurable failure, not a mood). **Two intuitive explanations were MEASURED AND FALSIFIED against the t2324 save:** duration is not the problem (Sugar War ran 161 turns from first mention and was the favourite), and epistemic-vs-terminal goals is not the problem (zero-box rates 3/13 vs 3/11; mystery quests like The Scarred Man 4/4 and The Glassworks 3/3 were fine). **Verifiability matters but is NOT sufficient — owner's decisive correction: “even if the closed eye line were verifiable, 18 threads is WAY too long… the closed eye dominated the main quest which can't be allowed to happen.”** **The real violation is PROPORTION:** authored spine = 3 acts / 10 arcs; the closed-eye thread = **18 quests, 1.8× the entire spine**, spanning **1,458 of 2,324 turns (63% of the campaign)**; 90% of all 51 quests were emergent. Mechanism: it began legitimately inside Act 1's “The Skinsaw Man” arc and then **outlived its parent by ~1000 turns across ~8 arc boundaries** with nothing ever asking whether it should still exist — while the giants/Jorgenfist thread (7 quests, 262 turns) was FINE precisely because it died with its arc. #191's stale-quest nudge is structurally blind to this: it fires on SILENCE (30 turns, no tag activity) and a hydra thread is loud — tags firing, quests closing on schedule, every indicator green. **Shipped fix — the arc wall:** an emergent quest is stamped `bornArc` at creation (`questIsEmergent` + `currentArcTitle`, helpers.js — one source for stamp and sweep so they can never disagree about what “spine” means); when `[ARC_COMPLETE:]` fires, live progeny of that arc are archived **`abandoned`** (the #229 status — the reopen guard blocks force-reactivation, a fresh `\|offered` may still bring it back if the story genuinely re-raises it), loudly (muts + toast + system message + console). `buildArcWallNudge` (NOTE_BUILDERS, combat-silent, per-arc cooldown) gives `ARC_WALL_WARN_LEAD`=15 turns of RUNWAY — naming the arc and every doomed thread so the GM can land them in fiction — but the wall falls either way: **no promotion path, by explicit owner ruling.** A thread that deserves campaign scale must be promoted to the SPINE (become an arc), which is the honest outcome — **and promotion is an AUTHORING-TIME act (f13 clarifier, 2026-08-29): it means editing the blueprint for the NEXT campaign; no tag, tool, or UI adds an arc to a running skeleton, by design under this ruling.** **Deliberately NARROW, three fail-safes:** spine-titled quests are never stamped (an arc can't sweep its own quest); UNSTAMPED quests are permanently immune (legacy saves — verified inert on the live t2324 save, both quests immune, `[ARC_COMPLETE:]` sweeps nothing); a PARALLEL act stamps nothing (an ambiguous parent would sweep innocents). | M | Fable | ✅ **SHIPPED v1.714** (2026-08-24) — 7 failing-first engine tests (suite 1678 green, latch registry + note-builder contracts re-censused clean); mutation proof `dev/sabotage-231-arc-wall.js` **8/8** — and sabotage EARNED ITS KEEP: the first run came back 4/5 + 0/1 with a MISSED clause exposing that nothing covered the parallel-act ambiguity guard, and a MISATTRIBUTED clause exposing an unreachable assertion; both fixed with real coverage. Field watch: the wall only binds quests created from v1.714 on — the first new campaign is the real test. |

Already-pending items skip the call and just reopen the confirm modal; refusals (already canon / pending / uncarried) return null. **Sibling ask, same session: Karzoug's greed-signet ring added to the STATIC item bible** (key `karzoug's greed-signet ring` + space/hyphen aliases) with canon derived verbatim from the t2334 Morwen identification scene; “unique” is encoded as the effect's leading word — the schema has no unique field and the BIBLE EDITOR CONTRACT refuses extra fields (TYPE-vs-INSTANCE). | M | Fable | ✅ **SHIPPED v1.710** (2026-08-24) — 3 failing-first engine tests (suite 1671 green; contract-canonical bible edit validated incl. the serializer's alias sort), browser-verified END TO END on the static server: button renders on canon-less rows only, click → proposal → sheet yields to the confirm modal → Accept → overlay written → button disappears → ITEM CANON injects; ring verified resolving+injecting from the exact carried line. Field watch: first real Define click (Cleaver is the natural candidate). |

| 228 | **The split re-affirm LOOP — the engine asked, the GM answered, and the answer wiped the question (field t2320-t2324, owner-ruled 2026-08-24 ⓒ).** TLDR: the player said “you three stay here, I'll scout ahead”; five turns later the save showed all three companions re-splitting to the party's own node EVERY turn, the guestbook had five phantom “arrivals” at a place nobody moved to, and the SPLIT AUDIT note was firing 12 turns out of 12. Root cause is a closed loop with the engine on BOTH ends: `buildSplitAudit`'s same-world waiver (api.js:514) makes a split inside the party's own world node due every turn regardless of age; the note tells the GM to re-affirm; the `[PARTY_SPLIT:]` handler re-MINTS `charSheet.splitLoc`, destroying the `.audited` cooldown that was the only brake — so the audit fires again next turn, forever. Each pass also stamped a fresh `guestbookStamp(...,“arrive”)` (filling `GB_TURN_CAP` with re-affirm noise — Frizwick's 34 real Eastern Ruins visits were folded into the `agg` by it) and re-stamped `lastSeenAt/Turn/Src`, re-witnessing #194 presence evidence for characters standing still. **Fix:** an identical re-affirm of an ALREADY-STAMPED split is a no-op — record, cooldown, guestbook and presence evidence all stand. Deliberately narrow: `_freshSplits` is STILL granted (owner ruling ⓑ — a same-node split is legitimate; without the grace the #133b co-location fold would dissolve “stay here” on the very next response), an UNSTAMPED legacy record still falls through and gets stamped (else the #133 legacy shape audits forever with no way to become fresh), and a changed location OR sublocation still writes (the audit note explicitly asks the GM to ADD a sublocation it omitted). **Owner rulings, same session:** (ⓐ) do NOT refuse an all-optional quest objective list — a fully optional quest is fine; (ⓑ) do NOT refuse a split whose target is the player's own node — “stay here” must keep working. **Honest limit:** this removes the loop's COST, not its trigger. The trigger was a missing `[SUBLOCATION:]` — Ammut walked throne room → archway → stair → bone-ring chamber with nothing filed, so the split target and the party's node stayed identical. The same-world waiver firing every turn on a legitimate same-node split is untouched residue. | S | Fable | ✅ **SHIPPED v1.707** (2026-08-24) — 3 engine tests written failing-first (1 RED before the fix, 2 pinning what must NOT change), suite 1660 green; mutation proof `dev/sabotage-228-split-reaffirm.js` 4/4 (branch dead / grace dropped / legacy guard dropped / sublocation dropped from the sameness test). Verified on the live t2324 save: SPLIT AUDIT firing 12-of-12 turns → 2-of-12 (the intended 10-turn cadence), guestbook and `lastSeenTurn` unchanged across 12 re-affirms, split still standing. |
| 227 | **The antiquity ratchet — cap "how old is old" in the blueprint with a named era ladder (owner ruling 2026-08-23: "it's just lazy. Anytime a new scene is introduced it's 'older' than the last").** TLDR: the GM signals that a new scene matters by making it older than the last one, so age only ever climbs ("older than Thassilon, older than the mountain, older than the oldest of the old things that predate the concept of old"), and the blueprint should declare a fixed ladder of named eras so a thing IS of an era instead of being "older". **Mechanism:** every new scene has to feel bigger than the last, and age is the one dial in the fiction with no stop on it — it costs no invention, contradicts nothing already written, and reads as gravitas, so the model turns that one every time. Same *safety* reflex as the banned ledger habit (DEFAULT_RULES "NEVER ADMINISTRATIVE", data.js:110): clerical detail is safe because it is verifiable, unbounded antiquity is safe because it is unfalsifiable. **Why a bare ceiling is not enough (owner ruled past it):** a wall at the anchor gets climbed in three scenes and then strained against ("nearly as old as Thassilon, and in some ways older"), or the laziness relocates to the next free dial — more cursed, more forbidden, deeper. Removing an escape hatch without supplying a destination just moves the vagueness. **Ruled shape — era LADDER, both consumers:** ① the blueprint declares 3-4 NAMED eras with the anchor on top (Runelords: Thassilon, fell ~10,000 years ago → the Chelish colonial layer → living memory and Sandpoint's founding), turning age from an unbounded axis into a closed ENUM: "older than Thassilon" has no rung to land on, so the GM must pick a rung, and picking is information; ② `campaign_generator.js` also GENERATES eras for freeform starts (the #59 two-consumer pattern), so the failure mode is not left live wherever no author wrote a ceiling; ③ a companion clause redirecting escalation to proximity and consequence (it is closer, it is awake, it knows your name, it costs something now) so the enum is a destination and not merely a wall. **Already half-authored:** samples/rise_of_the_runelords.blueprint names Thassilon 24 times and its premise already pins "fell ten thousand years ago" — the ceiling exists in prose and is enforced nowhere. **Placement:** campaign-CONSTANT text, so unlike buildSkeletonBlock (which carries arc lifecycle state and must stay volatile) this is a STABLE-half candidate beside buildNamingClause — cached at 0.1x on every re-read, in the region that carries authority; per-campaign variation is already precedented there by the tone and adult blocks. **Back-compat:** ""-clean when the field is absent, so the five sample blueprints, freeform campaigns, and every legacy save produce a byte-identical prompt. **Testability splits, and say so honestly:** the pure block builder is engine-testable (stable-half byte-identity, ""-clean-when-absent, legacy blueprints unchanged); whether the ratchet actually STOPS is playtest-audit judging only, alongside the existing prose-length dimension. **Touches:** blueprint schema + blueprint-designer.html, campaign_generator.js, api.js buildSysPrompt. **Possible sibling, NOT ruled:** a STYLE clause for escalation *in place* below the ceiling (an intensifier is not information — either spend one committing detail or say it plainly and move on), deliberately held back because "be specific about ancient things" naively applied turns the GM into a lore-dump engine whose inventions the memory tiers then file as canon. | M | Fable | **SHIPPED with a SECOND half the filed row did not have.** Mid-build the owner produced a live line — “I have waited longer than your kind has had a word for waiting” — and it is NPC DIALOGUE claiming no rung at all, so the ladder alone could never have caught it; they also placed the cause: house style burrowing through a prose-voice directive Sonnet is not fully holding. So an UNGATED anti-comparative-age clause went into **STYLE** (the volatile end-of-prompt slot, where voice authority lives per audit #2) rather than DEFAULT_RULES — a house-style intrusion is a voice failure, and it must fire for the freeform and legacy campaigns that have no ladder. **Also shipped:** `dev/set-deep-time.js`, because the ladder is write-once at campaign start and the campaign that produced the complaint is 2,100 turns past that — a human may set it once out of band (one deliberate cache invalidation); the GM still cannot, which is the whole distinction. ✅ **SHIPPED v1.706** (2026-08-23, designer v0.38). 9 failing-first engine tests + 3 designer contract pins; `dev/sabotage-deep-time.js` 13/13, which caught two real holes on its first run (an inert mutation, and zero coverage of the applyBlueprint write — deleting that line left the suite green). Designer round-trip driven live through `window.__bpdTest`. Runelords ladder authored: Thassilon → Chelish colonial → living memory. |

| 221 | **Spell name debranding — rename the capability bible's IP-shaped names, with tooling (owner request 2026-08-23).** TLDR: the owner is rewriting spell content to avoid potential copyright infringement, and some names have to change with it; doing that by hand is error-prone because a spell NAME is a storage key in six places, and a half-done rename fails SILENTLY (capabilityLookup returns null → the spell stops injecting its canon and `[SPELL_USED:]` stops billing mana). **Deliberately NOT part of #104** (owner call): that row is author-voice debranding; this is game content and has its own surfaces, tooling, and release gate. **Why the item-alias pattern (#157) is the WRONG shape here:** an alias keeps the old name resolving, which means the infringing string ships in the data files forever — the opposite of the point. Cheap, ships fast, field-watched like #108. ② escalation IF the rule under-delivers in the field: per-node `hours` canon (a `[LOCATION_HOURS:]` tag or LOCATION_DESC-adjacent) + a geo-block "closed at this hour" line — engine-backed, design-first, NOT part of ①. Stable half → one-time cache re-warm. **Field watch (the #108 pattern):** judge on the next few late-night scenes — clean acknowledgment of the hour closes ①; no behavior change escalates to ② (per-node hours canon + geo-block closed-line), which stays design-first. | 192 | **Blueprint class roster — custom classes + curated availability (the "Tinkerer for Iron Meridian" feature; authoring half shipped, engine consumption open).** TLDR: a blueprint can now DEFINE campaign-specific classes and CURATE which classes character creation offers (a steampunk sky-city without Druids/Rangers), authored in the Blueprint Designer; the engine normalizes/validates and carries the data but does not consume it yet. Schema: `customClasses[]` ({name, desc, hd, prime, statPriority, gear, abilities[{nm,ds}], skillSeeds, features[{lvl,nm,ds}]} — ability-based chassis only; spellcasting customs need capability-bible support) + `availableClasses[]` (**ABSENT = no restriction** — deliberately never expanded to a full list, so an old file can't silently exclude a future 10th base class; present = the checked roster, base + custom names). Designer v0.36: Custom Classes cards (ability/feature rows with breakout editors, CSV stat-priority/skill-seeds canonicalized to arrays AT the field boundary so fileOut always ships canonical shape) + Available Classes checkboxes fed by the shared `blueprintClassList()` (game.js) so designer and the future wizard read ONE roster; rename/delete of a custom class follows through the restriction live (no orphans); the page finally pays its 2026-07-29 seam debt (`window.__bpdTest`). Validation is LOUD per class: base-name collision, duplicate, pipe-in-name, hd ∈ {6,8,10,12}, prime + full 6-stat priority permutation, ≥1 complete ability, feature levels 2–20, skill seeds resolved against SKILLS, availability membership + non-empty. **NOT consumed yet — the follow-on engine milestone (drift surface, design-first):** wizard filtering, the classDef() overlay, applyBlueprint persistence into worldState, XP/archetype semantics for customs, capability packs for caster customs, and library-import portability of a custom-class character; the last two carry open design questions needing rulings before that build. | M | Fable | ◒ 🧪 **Ready to test** — authoring half shipped v1.637 / designer v0.36 (2026-08-15). 6 failing-condition engine tests (suite 1434 green), BLUEPRINT DESIGNER CONTRACT (run-tests.js, 5 clauses incl. the class_bible script tag + the shared-roster pin), sabotage `dev/sabotage-blueprint-classes.js` 8/8 caught + byte-identical restore; live-verified in-pane through the real page (collision/permutation/unknown-roster refusals, checkbox → restriction → absent-on-all-checked round trip, rename-follow). **Verification bycatch:** the designer's engine chain had rotted — api.js crashes at NOTE_BUILDERS without identity/clock/tag_table, so ALL designer LLM features (Generate/Review/Apply/Ask) were dead since v1.581 → fixed as designer v0.37 (chain restored). Engine consumption = the open remainder of this row. ✅ **ENGINE CONSUMPTION CORE SHIPPED v1.689 (2026-08-21, lane A):** the overlay lives at the ACCESSOR level so every downstream reader consumes customs with zero per-shape branching — `_customClassToDef` (helpers.js) adapts the designer-validated shape into the bible class shape (name-as-id; `archetypes:[]`, no spellTiers — the non-caster absence paths already handle both); `classDefs()` memo keys on the custom SOURCE REFERENCE (worldState.customClasses first, `pendingBlueprint` during creation — the handoff is seamless because startGame nulls the pending blueprint only after `applyBlueprint` persists COPIES into `worldState.customClasses`/`availableClasses`, which ride the sync blob free). `classAvailable()` gates the wizard grid (absent = unrestricted per the schema's own rule; matches id or display name); buildClsGrid filters + HTML-escapes custom text (#22 — file-authored data never raw into innerHTML) keeping original indices for pickCls. **Soft-lock closed at both L3 seams:** an archetype-less class skips `showArchetypeModal` (checkLevelUp) and `showCreationArchetype` falls through to the bump/spell/start continuation — an empty `wireClose:false` chooser would have locked the game. XP = the standard `classXpLevels()` curve; spell machinery no-ops via `SPELLS[cls]` absence. 6 failing-first tests incl. copy-persistence and the modal-soft-lock probe (1595 green). **REMAINING TAIL (pinned on owner rulings):** caster capability packs + library-import portability of a custom-class character. |
| 141 | **Suggestion validation pass — the in-call scene check (the driverless-cart phantom).** TLDR: a button offered "watch the driver's hands" at a cart the narration had explicitly left driverless — a generic ROLE NOUN no closed-vocabulary #126 gate rule can test (roster/spells/nodes are closed classes; "the driver" is open-class scene fact). | 6 | **Headquarters → THE VILLAGE (reframed 2026-08-24, owner design session — still in design, no build).** TLDR: the HQ is not a stash-room but a small persistent VILLAGE that exists OUTSIDE any campaign and attaches to new ones as a mini-skeleton frame — and every saved character has a house there: the character library, made spatial. **Owner framing (2026-08-24):** a piece of the map that exists within each campaign; the Runelords four can return there now that the campaign is done; the next adventure begins from there; characters store items/trophies and adjust loadout there; “sort of a mini-skeleton that attaches to new campaigns”; “a small village where each saved character has a house”; owner explicitly wants more thought before building. **Design state:** ① persistence home = a village store beside the character library (outside worldState, server-synced like it); ② houses are library-driven — saving a character mints/keeps their house; per-HOUSE stashes (your house, your stuff) + village COMMONS (trophy hall, tavern, notice board) for shared things; ③ the mini-skeleton attaches at campaign SEAMS — departure beat at start, optional homecoming interludes at act boundaries (the loadout/restock fiction, paced), return beat at the end — framing the authored spine, never fighting it, and dodging the geography problem (the village lives at the campaign's edges, travel is GM connective tissue); ④ killer consequence: cross-character continuity gets a PLACE — a new PC's first adventure can start by knocking on retired Ammut's door (library-import-as-NPC/companion already exists); ⑤ anti-drift unchanged from the original row: village state is authoritative data, HOME block injected when present, [HQ_STORE:]/[HQ_TAKE:]-class tags for narrated item moves. **⑥ THE VILLAGE HALL (owner: “A+ idea”, 2026-08-24) — where finished campaigns are DEPOSITED, not just ended:** the hall's walls accumulate each completed campaign as a physical memento — Runelords as a mural, a mounted map, the crown on a plinth — fed by the #219 story-compiler output and the trophy list; same village store, zero new mechanics, pure payoff. Finishing a campaign files it INTO the world your next character walks through. **Open forks (owner to chew):** attach-to-authored-blueprints vs generated-only (a creation-time toggle may suffice); companions' houses (saving Daeris/Frizwick/Morwen gives all four houses — per-character vs per-household); the hard v1 fence (houses auto-appear; upgrades/economy/village NPCs are phase 2 / Campaign Designer overlap); single village per account (prior v1 ruling: one HQ) presumed to carry over. Original 2026-07 sketch (worldState.hq per-campaign, [HQ_SET:], bench/residents) superseded by this framing; the party-bench sub-feature survives as phase-2 standalone. | L | Fable | ○ In design — village reframing captured 2026-08-24; awaiting owner's next pass, no build authorized |

| 77 | TTS: Input proof reading / nonesense filter: Fantasy Character names often auto complete, or spellcheck incorrectly, this is particularly bad during car mode when the user can't actually see the input was.  We need to implement a filter to check incoming input for nonsense (Frizwick's name becoming Physics, or "Show Corynn the symbol" becoming "Shalelu Korunn symbol"<br>Until we can reliably convert nonesense into a directive, we need a verification pass to make sure what was dictated is user intent.  This should be an option in the file menu to turn off or on.  So, the driver speaks their intent... the filter runs on it, the GM reads back the intent as recorded and asks for confirmation.  This needs to be played a bit to assess how irritating it is. **Also owns the multiplayer voice-misattribution case (Q5/D13, 2026-07-20):** with several people dictating in a car, a second player speaking during another's spotlight is silently filed as the spotlight PC's action. The read-back-and-confirm pass here is the backstop — the spotlight player sees/hears words they didn't say and declines. As written this row targets garbled CONTENT; misattribution rides the same confirmation gate as a side effect, so explicit speaker-identity handling, if ever wanted, is its home. | M | Fable | ○ Design sketched 2026-08-03 in [DOC/Research/DOC_whisper_stt.html](DOC/Research/DOC_whisper_stt.html) §5: confidence-GATED read-back (Whisper verbose_json avg_logprob/no_speech_prob + far sttCorrectNames edits + out-of-roster nouns trigger it; clean transcripts auto-send), read-back via the existing TTS ladder + spoken yes/no/again (parseCarCommand grammar), File ▸ Admin toggle, Car Mode default ON. Sequenced AFTER the #113 §4 upgrades land and are measured — the prompt-bias fix should kill most of this row's garble class at the source. Build note: the confirm gate must never touch sessionLog/transcript until confirmed. <br>**⛨ FABLE REVIEW 2026-08-06 (user widened scope: general nonsense filter, not just STT) — full findings + layered design in [DOC/Research/DOC_nonsense_filter.html](DOC/Research/DOC_nonsense_filter.html).** TLDR: the research law is RESTRICTION (shortlists/verify-before-correct/confidence bands — an LLM corrector fed full context measures WORSE than none); our sttCorrectNames is the validated shortlist pattern but, probe-measured and hand-verified, it also CORRUPTS clean speech ("there is"→Daeris, "sets"→Zethran; 14–18/30 synthetic sentences; no stoplist fix exists — the FP class is inseparable from the designed catches) and in Car Mode those auto-send with zero glance; the confidence signal the gate needs is FREE on the shipped model (`include:["logprobs"]` on gpt-4o-mini-transcribe — §5's verbose_json fields are whisper-1-only, re-point the design) and NOTHING captures any confidence today on either path. Build order ruled by the review: L0 capture confidence + persist a correction/measurement channel → L1+L2 gate-and-read-back as ONE feature (yes/no vocab doesn't exist yet; "again" collides with the #78 menu re-read; confirm hook must sit ABOVE the <3-char gate and busy-park) → L4 one GM DEFAULT_RULES line (unintelligible → ask in-fiction; drift-surface, own commit) → measure → L3 Haiku proofread (~$0.0004/utt, restricted-shortlist only) ONLY if measurements demand it. Typed path confirmed to have zero inspection; the structural garble class ("Shalelu Korunn symbol") is provably beyond token-aligned substitution — L3 territory. <br>🧪 **Ready to test — BUILT v1.548 (Layers 0–2, user go 2026-08-06) — awaiting FIELD validation (the row's own "needs to be played a bit" gate).** L0: `include[]=logprobs` rides every gpt-4o transcription request (whisper-1 fallback runs confidence-less by design), native per-chunk `confidence` read (0 = no-signal, the Edge-Canary class), `sttLogEvent` ring (`tnd_stt_log_v1`, cap 100, counts+reasons never transcripts — read via `sttLogAll()`). L1-gate: `sttCorrectNames` gains a collector (3rd arg, omitted = byte-identical, regression-pinned); corrections feed `sttSuspicion` (helpers.js — reasons: low-confidence <0.66 / far-correction sc≥10 / common-bigram (the "there is"→Daeris FP class) / multiple-corrections / unknown-name; null confidence NEVER flags alone — the native path must not confirm everything). L2: suspicion on the auto-send path → Car Mode speaks "I heard: … — send it?" + spoken **yes/no/redo** (`parseConfirmCommand`, helpers.js — same anchored false-positive discipline as parseCarCommand, battery-tested incl. "no time to lose"/"yes and I draw my sword"; "repeat" re-reads; 2 unrecognized answers fail safe to park-in-field); non-car auto-send holds + amber-flags the field; File ▸ Admin ▸ 🛡 Confirm unclear voice input (default ON); confirm interceptor sits ABOVE carVoiceCommand/busy-park/<3-char gate (the #78 ordering lesson — a spoken "no" is 2 chars); nothing touches sessionLog/transcript until confirmed (pending text lives in `_confirmPending`, field cleared); cloud confirm answers stay push-to-talk (the round-2 #25 hallucination rule — a hallucinated non-answer can't send, parseConfirmCommand refuses it). Tests: 11 engine tests red-first→green (1047 total); 3-clause #77 CONFIRM GATE source contract in run-tests.js (interceptor order / logprobs request / sends pend.text never the field), all 3 sabotage-proven. **L4 SHIPPED v1.549** (own drift-surface commit): UNINTELLIGIBLE INPUT rule in DEFAULT_RULES beside the PLAYER-ACTIONS-ARE-INTENT rule it counterweights — garble → a character asks in-fiction, no state tags beyond [TIME_ADVANCE:]; the NARROW guard ("an unusual but coherent action is NOT garble") is test-pinned because without it the rule licenses refusing weird-but-valid actions. L3 stays evidence-gated on the sttLogAll record. |
| 78 | **Car Mode: suggested actions read aloud as 1/2/3, pickable and re-readable by voice.** (= CAR_MODE.md **Phase 2**; Phases 1 and 3 — the loop, overlay, auto-listen, mediaSession — already shipped.) CAR_MODE.md names Web-Speech-in-a-moving-car as the one risk that could change the design (ties to #19). **The spec's premise was STALE and that WAS the work:** CAR_MODE.md assumed "`speakResponse()` reads the raw `*You could A; B; or C.*` line", but **#14/v1.110 removed that line from GM prose entirely** — suggestions now come from a SECOND async call (`generateActions`) that resolves *after* narration starts, so on a short turn narration drains before the options exist. Doc corrected in place. <br><br>**Built:** two pure, engine-tested pieces in helpers.js — `buildOptionsSpeech(acts)` ("Option 1: … Option 2: …", punctuated via #88 so `splitSentences` gives clean pause boundaries) and `parseCarCommand(text,count)` → `{kind:"pick",n}` / `repeat` / `repeatAll` / null. Car Mode side (ui-carmode.js): `_carActions()` reads the live `.qa[data-action]` per the doc's single-source rule (placeholder buttons carry no `data-action`, so "" *is* the not-ready signal), `_carOptionsStep()` inserted into `_carAutoMic` **before** the mic, and `carVoiceCommand(text)` as a new cross-lane hook mirroring `carNotify`'s contract style. stt.js calls it from `_applySendPolicy` **above both gates** — the busy branch would park a command as an action, and the rank-8 `<3`-char gate would eat a spoken "1"/"2" *and clear the field*. <br><br>**User rulings (2026-07-27):** ① narration→options→mic, holding the mic up to `CAR_OPT_WAIT_MS`=3s (300ms polls) then a spoken "No suggestions — just say what you do"; ② read every turn automatically; ③ "repeat"/"again"/"options" re-reads the MENU, "repeat everything"/⏮ replays the SCENE (`_carDoReplay`). <br><br>**⚠ The false-positive rule is the load-bearing design decision:** a command must be the WHOLE utterance (anchored `^…$` after filler-stripping), never a substring — a bare `/second\|repeat/` match would silently eat real turns ("I attack the second guard", "repeat the ritual"). An out-of-range number ("four" of 3) deliberately falls through as free-form. <br><br>**Live-found bug fixed along the way:** `sttCorrectNames` runs BEFORE the interceptor and `STT_COMMON` protected one/two/three/first/last but NOT second/third/option/repeat — a roster holding "Theros" rewrote a spoken **"third"→"Theros"** and the pick vanished. Also a pre-existing wart for ordinary play ("take the third door"). Fixed by extending the existing protection list; name-snapping verified intact. <br><br>**Verified:** 851 green (6 new: speech shape, pick vocabulary, the false-positive battery, repeat-vs-repeatAll, out-of-range, and the name-correction regression) + **live in-browser through the real functions** — 1st `_carAutoMic` speaks the menu / 2nd falls through to the mic; "option two" sends "Ask about the letter"; "I attack the second guard" NOT consumed; hold→poll→read when options arrive late; give-up fallback spoken; "repeat" with no options warns; a consumed command always leaves `#action-input` empty (and while `busy` parks the RESOLVED action text, not the word "three"); zero console errors. **Not covered:** real-device mic accuracy in a moving car (CAR_MODE.md's standing risk, ties to #19). |

| 219 | Story compiler — weave the campaign into a cohesive short story the player can keep and read later. PDF final, HTML fallback. | L | Opus | Planning doc written ([STORY_COMPILER.md](DOC/Research/STORY_COMPILER.md)) — chunked compile, standalone HTML first, ~5.5h build estimate. **DECISIONS (2026-06-15):** ① **Weave from VERBATIM PROSE, not chapter summaries** — the keepsake must preserve the actual told story (real dialogue / GM prose), not re-inflated summaries (this supersedes the doc's "memory.chapters is the spine"). ② Architecture: complete append-only **transcript = the flesh** (source prose), `memory.chapters` + `storyBeats` + `keyDecisions` = the **skeleton** (arc shape, pacing, and which transcript stretches to pull verbatim). ③ **Hard dependency — blocked on #25:** needs a complete/ordered/durable transcript that doesn't exist yet; the auto-export `.txt` files can't serve (desktop-only trigger, DOM-snapshot truncated by reload, scattered per-device). Build transcript capture FIRST. ④ Cost: skeleton must *select* transcript stretches, not feed all ~1200 turns. Chunking/voice/output sections of the doc still valid; only the input source changed. **Build parked** (user's call). <br>**ARCHITECTURE REVISION (2026-07-09) — pipeline of composable POST-PROCESSES, not a monolithic compile.** The transcript dependency (#25/#12) is now DONE, and **Export Narrative shipped v1.229** (`buildNarrativeHtml`, File ▸ Save/Load ▸ 📜 Export Narrative) — transcript → styled HTML with player actions as `.act` blocks and GM prose as `.gm` blocks. That structured, free, deterministic export is **stage 1** and the enabler for everything after it: because the export tags which text is player vs GM in the markup, later passes parse it back into `(action, narration)` pairs and run on the **saved file** — no live campaign/transcript needed, reusable on any exported chronicle. **Stage 2+ = optional, composable LLM passes, each producing a SEPARATE doc (never overwrite the raw chronicle; it stays the honest source of truth):** <br>• **Fold player actions** — weave each player action into the following narration so it reads as continuous prose. The core rule (from play): the drop-vs-fold decision is **per-seam, not global** — if the following narration already conveys the action, DROP it; if it assumes the action without showing it (leaving an incongruous hole), FOLD a brief clause in; preserve the GM's actual prose/dialogue, smooth seams only, invent nothing. LLM judgment per transition, not string surgery. <br>• **Person conversion (2nd/1st → 3rd)** — recast the immersive second-person "you" (+ first-person player actions) into a third-person novelization ("Vyrindra…/she…", named from the sheet + gender). **LLM, not regex** — the trap is dialogue: narration-"you" must convert but an NPC *saying* "you" must stay. Pairs naturally with a **present→past** tense shift (novel feel). A **taste choice**, not a default — some want immersive 2nd-person, some want a novel about their character. <br>**Both passes still need:** chunking (fold/convert a chapter at a time, reassemble — cost + context limits) and the per-seam/per-line model judgment; the post-process framing just isolates all that difficulty behind an opt-in. **Load-bearing constraint:** the export must keep its block structure MACHINE-READABLE (`.act`/`.gm` classes) — flattening it to undifferentiated prose would break every downstream pass. Order matters (fold → then person). ✅ **v1 SHIPPED v1.685 (2026-08-21, lane-D Opus agent build, Fable-reviewed + integrated): `story_compiler.html`** — root satellite (opened directly, network-first in sw.js), verbatim-prose keepsake per decisions ①②③: three data sources in the map_viewer precedence order (`window.__storyTest` seam → `.tnd`/JSON import or drag-drop → live localStorage, read-only comment-pinned), chapters segmented from `memory.archive.chapters`+`memory.chapters` turn windows + an always-present unsummarized tail, blocks emitted as `<section class="chapter" data-turn-start/end>` holding `<div class="act"/"gm" data-turn>` (machine-readable, `data-tnd-story` marker for the later fold/person passes), `bk`/`rf`/`rc` entries excluded but COUNTED in sidebar + colophon (never silent), export = self-contained HTML with light+dark palettes and a print stylesheet (print-to-PDF = the v1 PDF story). Real-save proof: the t2050 Runelords export (4.8MB) → 4053 passages across 324 chapters in 51ms; file-input path driven in a real browser. `dev/tests-5-story-compiler.js` 38 assertions (drives the page's OWN inline script in a vm — the shipped page cannot rot behind a green test) + `dev/sabotage-story-compiler.js` 8/8, both in run-standalone-suites (12 suites). **Deferred as designed:** person/tense fold (decision ④, opt-in later pass), era-based PART divisions, File-menu entry (open directly, the blueprint-designer precedent). **RENUMBERED #5 → #219 (2026-08-23):** the completed section already held a different row 5 (Campaign designer) and the tracker lint verifies moved rows BY NUMBER, so filing this one under 5 paired it with the wrong row. Prose citations updated (CLAUDE.md, DOC/RAG_MEMORY.md); `dev/tests-5-story-compiler.js` / `dev/sabotage-story-compiler.js` and two code comments keep the original number — their section labels are load-bearing sabotage `mustFail` anchors. |

Field watch: final-act quests carry their goal as an unchecked objective from activation; side errands arrive "(optional)". **⚠ First measurement (lane-F sonnet-5 50-turn run, AUDIT_playtest_v1689_sonnet5.md): creation-time compliance 0/2** — both quests were created |active with NO same-response end-condition [QUEST_STEP:]; the #129 zero-objective nudge + step activity healed both within a few turns (final state correct). | 173 | **LOCATION VISITS HAVE NO ACTOR PROVENANCE — Frizwick inherited the party's Jorgenfist memory after rejoining (field, t1728).** TLDR: on the return approach Frizwick said, “Last time we saw it, it looked like a wound in the mountain,” but she was not there: t1590 sent her alone to warn Hemlock in Sandpoint; t1594 names only Ammut, Morwen, and Daeris continuing into the mountains; t1661 has Frizwick embrace the three survivors; t1662 is when they TELL her the Jorgenfist story. The save's Era 4 summary preserves that distinction exactly, so this is not missing campaign knowledge — the model collectivized history once the four were together again. **Mechanism:** the location map has no representation that can answer “which characters were here on the previous visit?” `fileLocation` records only global camera turns/counts (`memory.locations[loc].visited`, node `firstVisit`/`lastVisit`/`visits`); `mapNpcLocation` adds a name to the node's timeless, additive `npcs` set and overwrites one mutable `lastSeenAt`; `[PARTY_SPLIT:]` keeps only the CURRENT `splitLoc`, which rejoin deletes. `buildGeoBlock` injects current geography, current split threads, and NPCs whose latest location is elsewhere — neither `node.npcs` nor historical attendance reaches the prompt. Thus correct prose memory competed with the recent “the four of you are returning” frame and lost, producing a false shared eyewitness claim. **Class:** actor provenance for shared history — the historical half of #137's “membership ≠ presence” invariant. Every split/rejoin can turn one subgroup's past into the reunited party's “we,” so this is a class, not a Frizwick wording patch. **Owner-ratified data shape (2026-08-12; review-before-code):** every node gets a plain dictionary `guestbook`, keyed by canonical character name, with one uniform value shape `{turns:[...],resident:boolean}`. A filed arrival records the hero and living unsplit party members at their effective physical node after same-response split/rejoin state settles; a contemporaneous NPC-location write records that NPC too. `resident:true` is an explicit, reversible “routinely based here” association for proprietors such as Ameiko — never evidence that the NPC is physically present now. Turns are deduplicated and bounded per character. NPC merge/alias and location merge/split/repair paths must re-key, union, dedupe, OR resident flags, and allocate split history explicitly; map viewer must expose both visits and residents. The volatile GEO block projects only relevant current/prior attendance plus “usual base” residents with the non-presence warning. **Failure-first replay:** split Frizwick to Sandpoint → file the three-person ridge/Jorgenfist visit → rejoin → return with four; Ammut/Morwen/Daeris must have both turns, Frizwick only the return. Resident-only Ameiko has no fabricated visit turn and is never asserted present. **Known evidence limits:** `[NPC:]` is not an exhaustive scene roster and can itself mis-stamp a remotely mentioned NPC, so ordinary NPC visits are only as reliable as contemporaneous location writes; this save also labels Jorgenfist `KNOWN OF (not yet visited)` because the 57-turn infiltration had zero location tags (#168). | M | Fable | Filed 2026-08-12 from the t1728 live save; owner finalized the guestbook dictionary + explicit residency contract. Three read-only evidence briefs completed and parked in review entry 15; no code changed. **FABLE ADJUDICATION ✅ 2026-08-14 (build authorized — owner: "implement now", solo, not waiting for the August batch):** design confirmed build-ready with these PINNED amendments — ① the cap is PER CHARACTER (8 turns each, older folding into `{first,last,count}` aggregates), not per node (that phrasing was the pre-amendment shape); ② negative assertions are RECORD-based — "no recorded visit", never "has never been here" — and the GM instruction treats unrecorded as UNKNOWN, not as never (attendance inherits LOCATION-tag compliance; the motivating save's own Jorgenfist infiltration went 57 turns untagged); ③ the snapshot writer runs at a POST-HANDLER seam after same-response split/rejoin settles (brief A: `[LOCATION:]` precedes `[PARTY_SPLIT:]` in table order regardless of textual order); ④ repair-executor integration is FIRST-CLASS scope (brief B: locMerge deletes / locSplit drops / NPC merge orphans today — fold=union+dedupe+OR-resident, split allocates explicitly, alias/merge re-keys); ⑤ prompt projection carries no ledger-speak (attendance reads as memory); ⑥ historical-save BACKFILL is a follow-on receipted tool (map_cleanup family), NOT this build; ⑦ `node.npcs` redefines as display-only "ever associated" — nothing authoritative reads it. | 209 | **Eyelines scatter even when the written gazes converge — body orientation is the enforceable floor (owner read of the five-way, 2026-08-21).** TLDR: the five-way's WRITTEN prompt aimed 3 of 4 gazes at the stairs (Frizwick's rear-glance was the prompt-writer's sanctioned exception) and the renders still scattered — Nano's Daeris gazing at nothing, gpt-image-2 with not a single character looking up the stairs, Seedream "looking all over the place, visual discord" (owner). Gaze WORDS are the weakest lever an image prompt has; models obey TORSOS far more reliably than pupils. ✅ **SHIPPED v1.691 same day:** the v1.677 EYE-LINES directive in `buildSceneRenderRequest` now demands ① every pose clause END with an anchored facing phrase that NAMES the focal point ("eyes locked on the stair-summit" — concrete craft vocabulary, the t2084 lesson), ② BODY ORIENTATION IS THE FLOOR — even a turned head keeps torso/stance angled at the focus, ③ the one sanctioned look-elsewhere exception keeps its body squared to the focus. 3 assertions added to the #165 battery (each target string unique to the directive); the comparison page gained the verbatim prompt (the criterion it originally omitted — owner callout) + the per-engine eyeline table. Field watch: judge on the next few party renders whether convergence improves; escalation if prompt-side levers cap out = post-render eyeline QA is not worth a second image call — accept the model ceiling. | S | Any | ✅ Shipped v1.691 (2026-08-21). **#209b SHIPPED v1.692 same day — the A/B found the working levers (owner: "figure out what we can add to reinforce body positioning… then we're on our way"):** two candidate structures rendered on Nano against the control ($0.16, images + verdicts on the comparison page): **V2 staging-geometry + camera-relative view angles WON decisively** — the focal point stated DEEPER IN FRAME than every figure with the camera low behind the party, plus per-character view angles ("seen in three-quarter rear view", "seen from behind at the flank") — the previously-astray Daeris converged completely; V3's formation-vector opener moved torsos but not gazes (partial). The directive now teaches both validated levers on top of v1.691's facing-phrase + body-floor; the geometry invariant generalizes (a speaker-facing scene puts the party between camera and speaker — bodies still aim at the focus). 2 assertions added to the #165 battery. **#209c SHIPPED v1.693 (the synthesis landed — two levers #209b lacked):** ① ANCHOR-NOUN CONSISTENCY — encoders concentrate a concept in 1–2 tokens; pronouns bind to nothing, synonyms mint a second concept (the five-way's own prompt mixed staircase/stairs/stairway/steps/terraces) — the directive now demands ONE focal-point name repeated verbatim in every facing phrase; ② REFERENCE-POSE NEUTRALIZATION — vendor-documented: Gemini-family reference images carry pose/composition signal, so four front-facing bust portraits are four "face the camera" votes against the staging — `buildSeedLegend` now reassigns references to identity ONLY (pose/orientation/gaze follow the written description). 2 more #165/#166 assertions; the ranked five-lever synthesis (with shipped-state per lever + the GenSpace model-ceiling caveat) is on the comparison page. **#209d SHIPPED v1.694 (the practitioner sweep — graded evidence, reproducible-example sources):** the directive's own "nobody looks at the camera" was the research's MOST-CONVERGED failure phrasing — a negation (fails in every model family: DALL-E/FLUX/Nano vendor-documented) that also injects the token that summons the lens (the Grok arm's protagonist glared into it) — replaced with positive candid framing ("absorbed in the scene, unaware of being observed") + the writer is now TAUGHT the never-negate rule; and MOTION VERBS aimed at the named focal point are preferred over static facing words ("striding toward the X" orients a body by construction — the community's rear-view idiom is motion phrasing). 4 assertions incl. the negation-absence pin. Booru finding worth keeping: where captions separate body/head/gaze into distinct labels all three are controllable — NL models only keep the camera axis, which is why every shipped lever is camera/body-side. **#209e SHIPPED v1.695 (the gaze deep-research landed — 99 agents, 3-vote adversarial verification per claim):** two levers survived for our one-call prose-model case — ① DUAL-CHANNEL gaze clauses (head and eyes as separate statements per character, "head turned toward the X, eyes fixed on the X" — TextGaze: separately-describable axes; the only single-prompt mitigation for the weakest instruction class) and ② the EYE-CONTACT TRAP taught to the writer (booru ontology: eye_contact = strictly MUTUAL gaze, machine-enforced — in a group scene it turns characters toward EACH OTHER; reserved for deliberate two-character beats). Honest ceiling recorded on the page: gaze is a relation clause, ~1-in-5 fail at the measured top end — techniques raise hit rates, nothing guarantees eyelines in a one-call API. Calibration value: two popular claims REFUTED 0-3 (bare `looking at viewer` sufficing; the "gaze words are under-captioned" community lore). 2 assertions added. Escalation instrument if the field watch warrants: an in-house gaze-compliance A/B grid (the #209b method). Field watch continues on live party renders **#209f (v1.702, owner art direction 2026-08-22, the Storval Stairs pair):** FACES ARE OPTIONAL joins the writer directive — of four figures in the Nano test render the ONE that read correctly had her face completely hidden (and the Seedream re-render's sole stray gaze was a RENDERED face: Frizwick's eyes cross-frame while her torso held — the #209e ~1-in-5 gaze-failure floor, live). A hidden face cannot mis-aim its eyes, so the licence is also a gaze-reliability lever: identity carries through hair, silhouette, build, and gear; view angles serve the scene's geometry, never face visibility. The multiSeed reference legend quietly biased the writer toward showing every referenced face — explicitly released. Clause-pinned (2 checks beside the #209b pins). **CLOSED 2026-08-22 (owner: “finish up 209”) — the field watch adjudicated on the Storval Stairs pair:** both renders ran the identical complete directive; Seedream — the five-way’s WORST eyeline engine — converged 3-of-4 with the 4th torso-held (exactly the #209e ~1-in-5 measured ceiling), while Nano scattered on the same prompt. Same stack, opposite outcomes = the residual variance is PER-ENGINE, and the prompt-side quiver is complete by the research’s own conclusion (nothing guarantees eyelines in a one-call API). Per the row’s escalation rule: model ceiling ACCEPTED; engine variance is #208 engine-choice territory. | 218 | **Sabotage newline rot — multi-line find strings could never match CRLF files (harness class, found 2026-08-22 while proving #213).** TLDR: `sabotage.prove` matched clause `find` strings by exact `indexOf`; every source file is CRLF on disk, so any clause whose find contained a bare LF escape was structurally unmatchable — it printed `NOT APPLIED` and proved nothing, forever. 39 clauses across 14 sabotage files were candidates; 5 confirmed dead in live runs, two of them drift-surface guards (`combat-close propagation bypasses the scene ledger` in sabotage-w2 — the B3 kill-stamp bypass; `band distance degrades to always-far` in sabotage-phase — the #158 in-band silencer). The class survived because sabotage isn't in CI — the loud NOT-APPLIED verdicts scrolled past in manual runs. **Fix (harness, one boundary):** the matcher normalizes the CLAUSE to the file's newline convention — never the file (restoration is byte-identity) — and the replacement lands in the same convention so a mutation can't mint bare-LF islands into a CRLF file. Truly-absent finds still hard-fail NOT APPLIED (pinned). **Proof:** 2 failing-first meta-tests in `tests-sabotage-meta.js` (suite 5→7; rides CI via run-standalone-suites) + a clause in `sabotage-standalone-suites.js` that deletes the normalizer and demands the meta suite red (8/8; `dev/sabotage.js` joins its synthetic copy set; the scratch-isolation meta test skips LOUDLY in the no-git synthetic tree, still covered by the real-repo pass). **Wake-up audit:** sabotage-w2 + sabotage-phase re-run under the fixed harness (results in row status); the other 12 candidate files re-run as a follow-up batch — any newly-woken clause verdicting MISSED is a guard that never worked and gets fixed as its own finding. | S | Any (dev tooling) | ✅ Harness fix + meta proof + standalone clause SHIPPED v1.701 (2026-08-22, on Fable). Wake-up audit ①: sabotage-w2 66/66 + sabotage-phase 16/16 under the fixed harness — ALL FIVE formerly-dead clauses (incl. both drift-surface guards) now apply AND catch; zero MISSED, so the guards were correctly written all along and only the matcher was dead. Audit ② COMPLETE: 10 of 12 batch files fully clean — every newline candidate woke and caught. The 2 stragglers were a SECOND rot class the fixed harness now exposes honestly: STALE FIND TARGETS (code grew past the anchor text — principalNudged split the identity clause's pair; the TTS ladder gained gemini AND its contract error was reworded, so that clause's old mutation would not even red today). Both re-anchored v1.702 and proven catching. Net across the whole sweep: 5 newline-dead + 2 stale-dead guards revived, zero MISSED anywhere — every dormant guard was correctly written; only its proof was dead. |
| 217 | **Schedule near-duplicate dedupe — the triple-booked bracelet deadline (field, live t2097, 2026-08-22).** TLDR: the live clock carried THREE deadlines for one errand — "Return to Wyla Ashvane's shop to collect the four cold-warded bracelets" (7.4d) / "Collect the completed ember-rune warding bracelets" (6.8d) / "…finish tempering … in three days" (2.9d) — all injected into UPCOMING every turn, and the contradiction reached the fiction (Wyla arguing six days at t2087 while an entry said three). **Root cause:** `scheduleAdd` deduped by EXACT lowercase label only; its sibling store `fileFutureEvent` got stemmed-fingerprint near-dup dedupe as #29① for precisely this re-phrasing failure, and the tooth never landed on the clock schedule — even though #150 extracted `feNearDup` specifically so schedule code could share thresholds. **Shipped, both halves through the ONE shared fingerprint (no rival heuristic):** ① write-time — `scheduleAdd` folds a near-dup label into the existing entry, keeping the original label and adopting the NEWEST deadline (last-write-wins, exactly the exact-match semantics; the GM's latest statement of a deal is the freshest information); ② load-time — `scheduleDedupSweep` (clock.js, called from `migrateWorldState`) collapses twins an existing save already carries: born-DESC greedy grouping so the freshest-born entry wins by construction, complete pre-images archived to `clock.repairs`, idempotent, never touches `clock.min` (the #146 never-auto-heal rule guards the scalar/anchors; this removes redundant rows, reversibly). **Verified against the REAL save, not a synthetic twin:** replaying the actual t2097 export collapses 3→1 keeping sch3 — the three-day deal the fiction was actually arguing about — with both stale phrasings archived and re-run a no-op; the shipped fingerprint separates the control pair ("Meet Wyla at the shop" / "Ambush the caravan"). Judgment call recorded: last-write-wins on write + freshest-born on sweep were chosen without an owner ruling (the owner greenlit the build without answering the merge-policy question) — both match existing exact-match/fileFutureEvent semantics and the live fiction; re-rule if the field disagrees. |
| 216 | **[TIME_CHECK:] — the GM reads the clock before it writes (owner design 2026-08-22, the t2175 sundown-at-midday).** TLDR: sonnet-5 narrated nightfall and camped the party in full dark while the clock read 11:57 AM, and #158's prose recognizer — precision-tuned on the 328-turn audit — was structurally blind to it (nightfall rendered as pure imagery: "day bleeding out of the sky"; `clockPhaseAssertion` returned null on both failing turns and only fired on the correction). Widening the recognizer's grammar would trade in the false alarms that audit exists to prevent, so the fix is UPSTREAM of recognition: the owner's read-then-write proposal, made structural. **Shipped:** the GM opens every response with `[TIME_CHECK:phase]` — the #141 forced-checking-space shape (committing to a phase before writing prose anchors the prose) — and detection becomes a deterministic BAND COMPARISON instead of prose recognition: `clockCheckDeclared` (clock.js) resolves the label via the one `TIME_PHASES` vocabulary, compares via `clockPhaseBandDist`, and an off-band declaration ≥`PHASE_MISMATCH_MIN` arms the SAME #158 one-shot `phaseMismatch` → `buildPhaseMismatchNudge` (one latch, one nudge, no #151 census change). **Read-only by construction, and it is the design's load-bearing wall:** TIME_CHECK never moves the clock — `[TIME:]` reconciles and `[TIME_ADVANCE:]` charges; a declaration that could advance time would let the GM teleport the clock by assertion, the exact back door #73 closed. Table position BEFORE the TIME family = the declaration is judged against the pre-advance clock the GM actually read. Unmappable labels warn loudly and arm nothing. Doc line joins the clock family in the STABLE half (deliberate frozen-hash re-baseline, +582 chars doc / +11 strip; one cache re-warm on next live turn). Against the field failure: `[TIME_CHECK:sundown]` at 11:57 AM arms the reconcile nudge on the SAME turn instead of two turns of wrong-phase narration and a player question. | S | Fable | ✅ **SHIPPED v1.700** (2026-08-22, built ON Fable — drift review recorded here per the amended decree). 6 engine tests (2 defining reds first; 4 guard pins proven by mutation) + 3 sabotage clauses (teleportation write / dead arm / dead gate — all caught); diff-replay on 2 corpora: zero handler errors, historical responses unaffected. **Field watch (the #207 pattern):** compliance is free to measure — `tagLog` records tag names per response; judge emission rate + whether anchoring reduces phase drift over the next live sessions. Escalation if under-emitted: a one-shot missing-check note (the #20 channel lesson), not a stronger doc line. Deliberately NOT in TAG_REINFORCE (the clock family isn't there today, and the failing model is Claude, which never sees reinforce). |
| 214 | **The narration killed it, the tracker did not — the Grey-Hided Skulker desync (field, owner-reported 2026-08-22).** TLDR: the prose closed a fight ("the last of the grey things finally goes down") while Skulker C stood at 8/32 in the combat panel, because the response emitted no combat tag at all — not the killing `[ENEMY_HP:]`, not `[ENEMY_SLAIN:]`, not `[COMBAT_END:]`. **Owner ruling:** the GM saying "sorry, it's still alive" is a worse outcome than reconciling — remove the remaining HP and call it dead. Best case is a GM that follows the rules, but that cannot be the only lever (#60b: a prohibition without a channel does not change behavior; the gpt-4o finding: tag discipline is a separable capability). **The design boundary that makes this safe, and it is the whole design: prose wins for `combat.foes[]`, evidence still wins for roster NPCs.** W2 fails closed on identity because a wrong corpse is unrecoverable canon; a rolled foe has no identity stakes and its HP exists to serve the narration. Both levers are deterministic — **no prose scan anywhere** (the instrument #175b rejected as non-deterministic). **① `[COMBAT_END:` + a victory-shaped outcome word resolves the survivors** (tag_table.js). Previously a victory close with living foes nulled the tracker and discarded them silently — never marked slain, never propagated. They are now zeroed and marked slain *before* `propagateSlainFoes`, so a **rostered** foe still routes through the same scene-evidence gate as any other named death (pinned by a test: an unwitnessed rostered foe is NOT killed by the outcome word). fled/truce/disengaged/defeat resolve nothing — those mean the foe stopped fighting, not that it died. **② `buildCombatStaleNudge` asks when an open encounter goes `COMBAT_STALE_TURNS`=2 turns with no combat tag** (api.js, first in NOTE_BUILDERS, latch `combatStalePing`). It measures SILENCE — engine-observed tag activity stamped at the post-handler seam — never prose. Deliberately NOT combat-silent like its siblings: it is the one note whose entire subject is an open fight. Names each living foe and its HP, teaches the two honest exits (`[ENEMY_SLAIN:]` / `[COMBAT_END:]`) and the still-fighting case (`[ENEMY_HP:]`). | S | Fable | ✅ **SHIPPED v1.699** (2026-08-22). 6 failing-first engine tests + 5 sabotage clauses. ⚠ **Semantics changed for an existing shape:** three pre-existing tests were already closing a victory over living foes and now resolve them — no assertion broke, and the death-gate test pins that this cannot become a licence to kill named NPCs. **Field watch:** lever ② fires on the turn after the desync, so the next occurrence should self-correct in one turn; if the GM ignores the note, the escalation is a forced fork (the #127 pattern), not another prompt line. |
| 215 | **A withheld reward becomes a decision, not a loss — the claim modal (owner ruling 2026-08-22, amends #213).** TLDR: #213 made the shelve notice honest ("the withheld reward will not arrive — award it by hand with the Sync button"), and the owner ruled the fix wrong in its last step: **"going into Sync isn't an option — Sync is too much of an immersion breaker."** Open a modal and ask the player instead. One deliberate #213 re-baseline recorded in place: the shelve toast no longer says the reward "will not arrive" — it says it is waiting on the player’s call. The surviving contract is unchanged and is the one that matters: a costly shelve must say what it cost, a costless one must invent nothing. |
| 213 | **The withhold toasts say WHY, and tell the truth about whether the reward is coming back (owner request 2026-08-22).** TLDR: when W2 refuses a death and strips the quest completion + XP/gold that rode with it, the player watched a reward get narrated and then not arrive — and the toast said "an unresolved identity dispute", which names an internal and explains nothing. Owner ruling the same day, in two steps: ① the diagnostic identity toasts are dev chatter and get stripped before release (**Clear for Release** row), but ② **the two reward-denial toasts SHIP** — "they signal a bug the player shouldn’t have to suffer from" — and ③ they must carry the ACTUAL reason. **What the investigation found before any code:** the reason already existed at both sites and was being dropped on the floor (`refusedVictim` was a local one line above a toast that never named the victim; the standing-dispute reason sat on `c.reason`, which [identity.js:1229](identity.js:1229) already had precedent for printing) — and, the finding that shaped the fix, **nothing ever replays a withheld reward**: `_w2RefuseLog` is provenance only, payout depends entirely on the GM voluntarily re-emitting, and after `IDENTITY_CONFLICT_STALE_ATTEMPTS`=5 unanswered deliveries the conflict shelves itself, the withhold stops, and that XP/gold is simply gone. So "held until it’s sorted" would have been a lie in exactly the case where the player got hurt. **Shipped (owner picked "reason + honest status"; actual recovery deliberately NOT built — paying out rewards the engine refused is a new canon write path and wants its own design pass):** `W2_REFUSAL_REASONS` (the shipped-reason registry) + `W2_REFUSAL_COPY` (one ordered table, reason → player sentence) + pure `w2RefusalCopy`/`w2WithheldSummary` in identity.js — a DISPLAY layer only, never read by a parser or a prompt; the technical reason is untouched on the record, in the console, and in the GM nudge. Site A now reads "⚠ 600 XP, 100 gold, Giantbane withheld — the GM killed someone the scene never showed was there (Mokmurian). Asking the GM to put it right."; site B names the quest and its cause; and the SHELVE notice — the moment the withhold silently becomes permanent — says "the withheld reward (600 XP, 100 gold) will not arrive. Award it by hand with the Sync button if it was earned." Honest status needs to know the cost, so `_w2StampWithheld` records the stripped tokens on the conflict record (`c.withheld`, bounded by `W2_WITHHELD_CAP`=12, deduped) — a dispute that cost nothing never invents a loss. That ledger is also the groundwork if the recovery half is ever built. | S | Fable | ✅ **SHIPPED v1.698** (2026-08-22). 8 failing-first engine tests (each red on v1.697 before the fix; the suite went 1605 → 1610) + 10 `dev/sabotage-w2.js` mutation clauses. **The coverage guard earned itself twice during the build:** the new REFUSAL COPY CONTRACT (`dev/run-tests.js`, scans by reason SHAPE — call-site parsing both missed real reasons and mis-captured ternary fragments) caught two reasons shipping with no player sentence, "registered combat foe lacks a prior positive scene binding" and "claim id was reused with different metadata", both of which my own hand enumeration had missed. ⚠ **Deferred, and it is the real harm:** the withheld reward is still never recovered — the toast is now honest about that rather than fixing it. | 146 | **DRIFT HARDENING: clock repairs become idempotent transactions; impossible timelines get caught at load.** TLDR: the −1160 repair landed twice (−2320, transcript-proven); repairs get receipts riding the save, and load warns on born>now or regressed stamps. <br><br>**Solution:** ① `clockRepair(repairId, expectedMin, delta)` (clock.js): applies ONLY when `clock.min===expectedMin` AND `repairId` is absent from `worldState.clock.repairs[]` — the receipt list rides the save/sync blob, so a second device sees the first application (cross-device blindness IS the double-fire vector; a device-local receipt would not have prevented this); mismatch or duplicate → loud toast + console, zero mutation. ② `migrateWorldState` invariant check: any schedule with `born > clock.min` or `due < born` → console.warn + one-shot toast naming the anomaly; NEVER auto-heal (transcript stamps are decree-immutable — quarantine-and-ask only). ③ THIS campaign — **adjudication RULED 2026-08-08 (user): the intended timeline is ONE −1160 repair, so restore +1160 once** (true min = current+1160; both schedule anchors then sit legitimately in the past, the Sable window returns to ~18h). After ① ships, run `clockRepair("142-double-decrement", <current min>, 1160)` on ONE device as the build's closing step — the ruling authorizes the run; execution is part of finishing this row, not a separate ask. Red-first: refuse-on-mismatch, refuse-on-duplicate, invariant warn fires on the t1549 fixture, silent on healthy saves. <br><br>**Evidence (audit round):** the interim #142 repair was a raw console decrement (`clock.min-=1160`) with no expected-before value, repair id, idempotency, or invariant check. The latest t1549 save proves it ran twice or had an exactly equivalent second edit: GM clock stamps fall from 8820 at t1525 to 6500 at t1526, precisely **−2320 = 2×1160**, while turn numbers remain monotonic and normal engine code only increments `clock.min`. Current `clock.min=6680`, but both surviving schedules have `born` in the future (6915 and 7490), which violates the clock contract and extends the Sable deadline by 810 minutes relative to its creation duration. `migrateWorldState` only shape-heals the clock; it does not check transcript-clock monotonicity or `born <= now <= due`. Scope is honest: this is a repair/migration safety defect, not a normal-turn regression. Candidate direction: preconditioned/idempotent repair transactions plus load-time clock/schedule invariant diagnostics; long term, derive/replay corrections from a ledger rather than editing the scalar. | M | Fable | ✅ **CLOSED 2026-08-21 — the live repair was ALREADY APPLIED (receipt: t1550, 6690→7850) and the guard proved itself in the field:** the owner re-ran `clockRepair("142-double-decrement", clockNow(), 1160)` on the live device and it REFUSED on the receipt match with the loud console line — exactly the double-fire protection this row was built for, demonstrated against a real second attempt 550+ turns later. Current save verified: receipt present, `clockTimelineAnomalies` = none, 3 healthy schedule entries. 🧪 **BUILT v1.566** (2026-08-08, drift pass order 3). Shipped: `clockRepair(repairId,expectedMin,delta)` (clock.js) — refuses on receipt-id match (receipts ride `worldState.clock.repairs[]` in the sync blob, so the second device sees the first application — the double-fire vector) and on expected-state mismatch, both loud, zero mutation on refusal; `clockTimelineAnomalies()` (pure) + migrateWorldState wiring warns on EVERY load while born>now or due<born stands — never auto-heals. 5 red-first tests → green (1094); **full arc proven on a scratch copy of the real t1549 save**: both anomalies warn at load → `clockRepair("142-double-decrement", 6680, 1160)` applies 6680→7840 with receipt → the second application REFUSES → anomaly list empty. **▶ USER ACTION (ruled + authorized): on ONE device, desktop console:** `clockRepair("142-double-decrement", clockNow(), 1160)` — note the deficit is constant (−1160) even though min keeps advancing with play, so the receipt (not expectedMin) is the load-bearing guard for this particular repair; expectedMin earns its keep on future repairs prescribed with a written-down value. Consolidated 2026-08-08: Sol R3 — Fable conceded its earlier rejection (the transcript ck stamps prove the double application; re-verified independently) — [SOL](DOC/Research/Drift_risks_SOL.html) §R3 · [FABLE](DOC/Research/Drift_risks_FABLE.html) comments. Do NOT auto-repair this campaign before the instrument exists. |
| 114 | **Lazy-load heavy optional surfaces — first-launch cost on low-end phones.** TLDR: index.html eagerly loads 30 scripts (~1.5 MiB) in strict manual order; repeat loads are free (SW cache) but first-launch parse/main-thread cost grows with every release (review finding 6). The SW precaches the whole app shell at first load, so deferral saves ZERO network bytes — parse/execute only — and the realistic ceiling is ~63.6KB (ui-carmode 28,674 + stt 34,945) ≈ **2.7% of eager parse**, sitting on the driving-safety Car Mode path and the mic (stt's `loadSettings` is what reveals `#mic-btn` at boot — deferring it hides the mic entirely). Loader-stub complexity is not worth 2.7%. Feasibility map recorded should the calculus ever change: ui-portrait deferrable as-is (zero boot refs); ui-carmode needs 4 bare refs (ui-boot.js:228-231) wrapped in closures; stt needs a mic-button visibility strategy + post-load loadSettings; char-creation is the first-run boot screen — effectively not deferrable. Reopen only with a measured low-end-device parse profile showing those 64KB matter. |

| 198 | **gpt-5.6 hidden reasoning starves the output cap — "Response hit the length limit" toasts, ~100-token narrations, and one fully EMPTY committed turn (field, story turn 2002).** TLDR: gpt-5.x spends its hidden reasoning tokens from the SAME `max_completion_tokens` budget the adapter passes (1500 for turns, 200 for the suggestions call), and the openai entry sends no reasoning control and has no headroom scale — so visible prose gets whatever reasoning leaves over, sometimes nothing.
