Three defects: **ⓐ provider config** — send `reasoning_effort:"low"` for `/^gpt-5/` gameplay calls (the exact sibling of gemini's `thinkingLevel:"low"` ruling #22; "minimal" exists on gpt-5 but verify live — the gemini "minimal rejects with 400" lesson) + give openai gpt-5.x tokScale-style headroom so reasoning+prose fit under the cap (the cap is runaway insurance, not a style lever — v1.540). **ⓑ empty-response silent commit (no-silent-failures violation, drift surface)** — ALL THREE parseResponse fns return `""` for an empty-string payload (`typeof content==="string"` passes; PROVEN by direct call 2026-08-20 on all three), so an empty turn commits (turn++, empty sessionLog assistant, zero tags) instead of throwing "Empty response" into the loud retry path; fix = whitespace-trim check per provider, engine test per provider fixture. **ⓒ reroll-after-empty transcript hole** — rerollLast's replace guard (game.js:1862) requires the LAST transcript entry to be a GM one; the owner's sonnet-5 reroll of the empty turn replaced sessionLog+display but story turn 2002's narration is permanently absent from the transcript (last entry was the player line, so the replace was skipped). ⓑ removes the trigger; residual fix = push a fresh GM entry when the guard finds no GM tail. Bycatch: the suggestions call's 200-token budget on gpt-5.x is reasoning-starved by construction — each one trips `finish_reason:"length"` and fires the #132 toast too (api.js:2250 has no kind gate), which is why the toasts felt frequent; the buttons then die silently. **LIVE PROBE 2026-08-20 (owner-keyed, 9 Responses-API calls on the exact 33k-token t2004 prompt, callGM-faithful; raw record `testRuns/probe198/results.json`): the reasoning is NOT useful on this engine.** Reasoning summaries were unavailable (org-verification gate), so the judgment is behavioral A/B: at medium (the implicit default) a story turn spends 486–516 of ~700 output tokens on hidden reasoning; a COMBAT turn spent 1034 (variance 218–1034 on the identical prompt — the field's empty turn is the high-side draw under the 1500 cap); the suggestion-call shape (200 cap) came back `incomplete` with **200/200 tokens ALL reasoning and empty text — every actions call on gpt-5.x is a guaranteed length-toast + dead buttons**. What medium buys over low: nothing measured — low kept every tag discipline, ANSWERED the [SCENE_CAST:] ask both medium runs ignored, and played the better combat round (incremental, foe stays up), while medium's most ambitious product was a full W2 death envelope that is engine-ILLEGAL (same-response SCENE_REF evidence; one-shot-killed a 26-HP fanatic + completed the quest). `'minimal'` is REJECTED by gpt-5.6-sol (menu: none/low/medium/high/xhigh/max — the gemini "minimal rejects" lesson repeated, probe-verified); `none` works (0 reasoning, rich prose, fastest, one mildly-loose [XP:100] at n=1). Design of record: `reasoning_effort:"low"` for `/^gpt-5/` (the certified gemini `thinkingLevel:"low"` pattern, not `none` — keep a floor) + openai `tokScale:4` (actions 800 / turns 6000 — low's ~131-token reasoning always fits) + teeth ⓑⓒ. Note: this changes the sweep-certified arm — 50-turn Korrag re-certification is the follow-up per the menu rule. | S–M | Fable | ✅ **v1.666, 2026-08-20 (owner-ruled: ship low + headroom now, sweep later).** ⓐ gpt-5.x buildBody pins `reasoning_effort:"low"` (chat-completions acceptance probe-verified live; the #29b luna rung inherits through the same conditional) + openai `tokScale:4` (turns 6000 / actions 800 / summarize 8000). ⓑ ALL THREE parseResponse fns throw "Empty response" on ""/whitespace — the silent empty-commit path is closed (anthropic keeps the opus-5 thinking-block tolerance: first NON-EMPTY non-thinking block wins). ⓒ rerollLast pushes a fresh GM transcript entry when the replace guard finds no GM tail (heals rerolls of already-holed turns; new holes impossible via ⓑ). 3 engine tests written failing first + `dev/sabotage-198.js` 5/5 caught, byte-identical restore; full suite 1525 green incl. the #29 transport battery (rung rebuild carries the pin). **Certification: settled by the owner 2026-08-20** — the completed GPT-ladder sweep ([SWEEP_gpt_ladder_v1662.html](audits/SWEEP_gpt_ladder_v1662.html): sol/terra/luna all measured on the 50-turn standard campaign, menu unmoved) stands as the family's certification; a dedicated sol-at-low re-run is waived. The probe plausibly root-causes sol's v1640 blemish (3/50 zero-tag turns = tags cut at the reasoning-starved 1500 cap), so the shipped low+headroom config targets that arm's measured weakness; the #17 drift-health zero-tag indicator and the #132 truncation crumbs are the standing field watch if low ever regresses tag discipline |

Honest residuals: a custom-named player on a named-protagonist blueprint gets one benign note; a principal named in prose but never `[NPC:]`-registered still counts as off-stage (the note teaches the register-now exit); once-mentioned entities (a lone "Chained King") stay below the ≥2 bar by design — a single mention is a reveal, not a principal |

Re-roll clears/sets the rf mark to follow the current text. 8 engine tests (each written failing first; the RAG fixture probe-verified to SERVE pre-fix) + `dev/sabotage-refusal.js` 6/6 caught, byte-identical restores — enabled by a new `also:` option in sabotage.js proveScratch (co-changed working files ride into the scratch clone; the multi-file sibling of the #196 working-suite fix). | 128 | **NPC identity fragmentation — one person, many memory entries.** TLDR: the same character accumulates separate memory entries under name variants — the live Runelords save has 62 memory entries for 37 actual NPCs, with Sheriff Hemlock existing four times ("Sheriff Belor Hemlock" / "Sheriff Hemlock" / "Belor Hemlock" / "Hemlock"), so his history is split four ways: recall pulls from whichever fragment a scene happens to match, and every fragment looks thinner than the person really is. The existing tools (`[NPC_ALIAS:]`, `[NPC_MERGE:]`, the #57 merge-confirm nudge) are the right shape but under-fire — they wait for the GM or the summarize extractor to notice a duplicate. **Near term:** a deterministic variant scan — when one NPC name is contained in another ("Hemlock" ⊂ "Sheriff Belor Hemlock"), queue the pair through the existing #57 GM-confirmed merge channel so duplicates drain continuously; never auto-merge (twins, titles, and shared surnames are real people). **Long term:** stop using display names as the storage key and give every NPC a permanent internal ID — the indexing approach the user suggested back when (recorded in Architecture decisions ▸ "Stable entity IDs", 2026-06); that ruling deferred IDs until something forces them, and this field evidence is the strongest pressure yet — networked multiplayer / the server-canonical model remains the natural landing spot. **Ratified target shape (user design, 2026-08-03): a pointer table** — name keys are POINTERS to a stable numeric ID that owns the data (`"hemlock"→12345`, `"sheriff_hemlock"→12345`, `12345→{…}`), so a merge is one pointer write, a rename is one attribute flip, two NPCs can share a display name, and write-time stamps (RAG entity index) never rot. Recorded so it isn't re-litigated at migration time; the near-term variant scan is required under either storage scheme (the pointer table records "same person" cheaply but can't DECIDE it). ⚠ Drift surface (memory tiers + alias resolution) — Fable tier, design review before code. | M | Fable | ✅ **COMPLETE (v1.528, 2026-08-03; closed at the 2026-08-20 split)** — the deterministic variant scan was this row's deliverable: (2026-08-03): `npcVariantTokens`/`npcVariantPairs`/`scanNpcNameVariants` (memory.js) run every summarize, feeding the existing #57 queue via the extracted shared `_queueMergeHint` (once-ever latch + dedupe, one implementation for both producers). Deliberately does NOT reuse `npcCoreTokens` — that stripper drops role nouns (man/woman/stranger), safe only under resolve-time's exactly-one-candidate guard; at proposal time those words are identity-bearing. Ambiguity rules: a bare surname matching two different fuller names proposes nothing (the Perdrath-sisters case); a party member is always the canonical side. | 188 | **RAG retrieval is location-blind to the player's QUESTION — a memory ask naming a place retrieves nothing unless the party is standing there (field, t1788+: the wine-cellar confabulation).** TLDR: Ammut asked Frizwick if she remembered the merchant's wine cellar; the GM was served no excerpts and confabulated smugglers, in character and confident. Mechanism (verified in memory.js): `ragQueryEntities` builds input weights via `ragScanNames` — known NPC NAMES only; `q.loc` is the CURRENT world node. | 172 | **Narration stuck in THIRD person — the exit correction was time-boxed, and single-player had no person directive at the end of the prompt at all.** TLDR: the GM is supposed to write vivid second person; the owner's live campaign was narrating the hero by name instead. Two independent causes, both confirmed by execution against the owner's own save. **RC-A (proven, the latent defect):** leaving hot-seat multiplayer sets `worldState.mpEnded`, which drives the D12 second-person reinforcement — and `commitGmTurn` retired it on a TURN COUNTER, three turns after the demote, whether or not the GM had ever switched back. The proof is in the transcript rather than the state (a snapshot cannot see turn 816): hot-seat rounds render a `Name:` prefix per player, and scanning `worldState.transcript` for player entries with ≥2 such segments gives t809–t816 (Ammut+Morwen) and t915 (Ammut+Frizwick). Overlaid on the per-response second-person count: multiplayer ran t809–816, third person correctly began at t813, the exit note fired t817–819, the GM did not comply, the counter cleared `mpEnded`, and the campaign then narrated Ammut in the third person for TEN MORE TURNS (t820–829) with no correction left anywhere, recovering at t830 only by luck. The corpus even contains the controlled A/B: at t915 the GM complied on the first note and the drift lasted ONE turn. Complied → 1 bad turn; missed the window → 13. **RC-B (structural, and the likelier cause of the report itself):** in ordinary single-player the post-STYLE slot was the EMPTY STRING, so the only second-person instruction in the whole prompt sat in the CACHED STABLE half ~100k characters earlier — while the prose voice occupied the end-of-prompt authority position this file documents as load-bearing. Measured across 10,067 real GM responses: campaigns running the **`howard`** voice are 2.7–5.3% second person (third person from their THIRD response, before any momentum exists) against 98–100% for `abercrombie` — and the owner's campaign `proseAuthor` is `howard` in the t1667 export (it was `muir`, then `wells`, earlier). A voice that narrates in third person by nature had nothing at the authority end of the prompt to answer to. | M | Fable | ✅ **SHIPPED v1.605 (2026-08-12).** ① **Retirement is COMPLIANCE-boxed, never time-boxed** — `personDriftDetect` (api.js) clears `mpEnded` the moment a response actually narrates in second person, so a GM that complies retires it a turn EARLIER than the old counter and one that does not keeps being asked (the #167 lesson). ② **The empty slot is filled**: single-player now always ends with a short `PERSON: second person — 'you' is <name>` line, deliberately terse so it holds the person without competing with the prose voice. ③ **A cause-agnostic drift detector**, because the corpus shows drift that no multiplayer explains: `PERSON_DRIFT_MIN`=2 consecutive responses that narrate the hero by name with no second person outside dialogue arm `buildPersonDriftNudge` on the ENGINE-NOTE channel (the D12 round-3 channel — it rides the user message, the newest tokens, after the third-person prose it must overrule) plus the post-STYLE slot; one compliant response silences both. **Predicate measured, not guessed:** 10,043 judged responses, and the flagged set has a **ZERO** false-positive rate — the load-bearing clause is requiring the hero's name in the NARRATION, which suppresses exactly the 59-of-172 class that is pure dialogue, combat readouts and atmosphere (without it the FP rate is 34%). Flags run ≥54 narration words, median 208. Refinements that came out of the measurement: dialogue spans stripped but emphasis spans deliberately kept (a `*you*` is real narration), ABSTAIN on unbalanced straight quotes (the #93 lesson, same week — with odd parity the strip cannot be trusted either way), count once per `worldState.turn` so a re-roll REPLACES its verdict instead of inflating the run, and a gap >`PERSON_DRIFT_GAP`=3 restarts the run rather than letting the note claim "the last N responses" falsely. ④ **The silent surface is now visible**: `playerCount()>1` was discoverable ONLY by opening each companion's sheet one at a time, so a forgotten hot-seat promotion narrated the whole campaign in third person with no indicator anywhere — the party HUD now carries a `⚑ N players · third-person narration · <names>` chip that opens the offending sheet, where the PC/NPC toggle undoes it. **Verification:** 1348 green, 10 new tests incl. the failure condition (mpEnded must survive 6 non-compliant turns) and the cache guard (stable half byte-identical; the person line sits AFTER STYLE or it loses the position fight); a 9-mutation sabotage battery in an isolated tree, every mutation tripping a #172 test; a NARRATION-PERSON source contract in run-tests.js that fails the build if `game.js` ever retires `mpEnded` on a turn count again (sabotage-verified by re-adding the old line); and a live browser run at v1.605 confirming the tail line, the arm-on-two/silence-on-one behaviour, and the chip rendering. **Post-ship field confirmation:** the t1723 export reproduces RC-B exactly: one player, `proseAuthor=howard`, no `mpEnded`, and no narration directive in the retained user messages. Perspective first slips at t1697; after one mixed t1698 response, t1699–t1723 contain zero second-person narration (23 hero-named third-person responses plus three atmospheric responses). The save was exported at 12:19 but its affected transcript rows are stamped v1.594–v1.604, so the still-open browser session had not begun using v1.605; this is pre-fix evidence, not a v1.605 recurrence. **✅ LIVE-CONFIRMED 2026-08-12:** after reloading onto v1.605, the owner's next live turn returned to second-person narration. |

| 159 | **TTS: interword hyphens read as pauses — "half-buried" spoke as "half … buried" (field report 2026-08-10).** TLDR: hyphenated compounds (half-buried, cargo-culting) paused mid-word on the Piper-class tiers; the hyphen now becomes a space before the engine ever sees it, so compounds read as adjacent words while the comma/period rhythm the user likes stays untouched. **Mechanism (grounded on the t1593 corpus):** the GM writes compounds with plain ASCII hyphens — 1011 instances across 1.8MB of prose, in 636 of 1592 GM entries (40% of all narration), and ZERO em/en dashes anywhere — but `normalizeForTTS` only ever handled `--` and em/en dashes, and the unit splitter never breaks at hyphens, so the compound reached Piper intact and espeak-ng phonemized the hyphen as an audible pause INSIDE the synthesized wav — the one place the scheduled inter-unit gaps (comma 0.15s / full stop 0.44s) can't reach. **Fix (tts.js):** one lookahead rule in `normalizeForTTS` — `([A-Za-z])-(?=[A-Za-z])` → letter+space; the unconsumed trailing letter means chained compounds ("three-and-a-half") strip every link. Scope deliberately letters-only: digit ranges ("3-4"), spaced breaks (" - "), and the `--`/em-dash dashRepl rules untouched; unconditional across engines (OS voices speak both forms identically, so native is neutral and the paths can't drift). | S | Any | ✅ **SHIPPED v1.585** (2026-08-10). 2 failing-first tests + 2 pins (scope boundaries; B14 unit-count invariance — persisted speaker maps key on counts) → 1199 ALL GREEN; real-data check through the shipped pipeline: 5 real hyphen-bearing t1593 entries → 232 units, zero surviving interword hyphens, zero unit-count changes. piper_test.html's spike-era normalize copy left as-is (historical, pre-harvest, already drifted). |

⑥ nudge phrasing per review: "if this described the CURRENT phase, emit [TIME:…]; if it was only a reference, do nothing" (never "correct the prose" — already read); latch in NOTE_LATCH_FIELDS; ⑦ tests carry the review's REAL false-positive sentences (multiple phases in order, Morningstar Inn, negation, plans, history, figurative, visions) + a full-corpus precision audit over the 328 clock-stamped t1593 GM turns as the ENABLE GATE (reviewer's rough literal scan: 10 alerts ≥4h, mixed true/false — the shipped detector must keep the true "Morning comes…"@4:10pm class and drop the rest; noisy detectors teach everyone to ignore them). Red-first: due audit + dead provider → the audit fires again next turn; successful turn → normal cooldown; TT/silent paths untouched. <br><br>**Evidence:** Sol R6, Fable-verified (buildRelationshipAudit stamps at compose time; game.js builds notes before callGM; the catch restores nothing) — a due split-audit probe burned its stamp with no delivery, and t1547's ignored split audit consumed its cooldown anyway. decide design-or-oversight, then either validate-with-warn or record the ruling in a comment. ② **`combatFoeByName` containment picks the first of multiple matching foes with no warn** (tag_table.js:151; "Guard" over {Guard Captain, Guard Sergeant}) — the bare-tag path warns on exactly this ambiguity, the named path doesn't. ③ **Silent-miss cluster:** unknown `[SKILL_SUCCESS:]` id, player `[SPELL_USED:]` miss (companion twin warns — asymmetry intent unrecorded), all `COMPANION_*` unknown-name misses, `[SPELL_DEF:]` junk tier→0 + silent re-definition drop — candidate no-silent-failures violations; triage each to warn-or-documented-exemption. ④ **`[NPC_FORGET:]` has no archive** while its sibling `[NPC_SUPERSEDE:]` archives (and both retire EVERY substring-matching fact — a broad needle over-reaches; supersede is recoverable, forget is not). ⑤ **Receipts without mutations** (Sol R4, live at t1530): `[ITEM_LOST:none]` pushed the muts line "-none" though `removeInventoryItem` returned false — the handler reported success it never verified; sweep every handler that pushes a muts line without checking its helper's return value (the tagLog ring already pairs tags with muts lines, so a dev-mode verifier is a cheap diff over an existing structure). **Rebuttal-round rulings, baked in:** the ④ fix is the ARCHIVE (a tombstone in memory.archive the fiction can't see but the operator can recover) — NOT match-narrowing, because Oubliate's breadth is the spell's contract; | 93 | **B14 splitter edges, post-#96 rescope: an unclosed quote can voice NARRATION as a character (from the entry-4 Fable review, brief F; rescoped 2026-07-27).** TLDR: of the three original edge behaviors, #96's segment-claiming deriver killed one and UPGRADED another. **① THE SURVIVING CORE — unbalanced-quote parity inversion:** a stray unclosed quote flips `splitSentences`' dialogue/narration `spk` labels for the rest of the paragraph, and since v1.451 the consequence is worse than mislabeling: `deriveSpeakerMapFromTags` assigns voices per-unit mechanically, so inverted NARRATION units get the segment's CHARACTER voice — narration read aloud in Ameiko's voice (the old model post-pass usually omitted such lines; the deterministic deriver won't). The safe direction (dialogue→narrator) is unchanged. Candidate fix unchanged: reset `_inDlg` at a paragraph boundary only when quote parity CLOSED at that boundary (the continued-speech convention — unclosed quotes DO continue across paragraphs — must keep working, B14c-tested). **③ minor tail:** a lone `"` fragment can reach synthesis as a junk audio blip (the fold loop's `k > 0` bound skips index-0 punctuation-only pieces) — forward-fold them. **② RETIRED (2026-07-27):** the adjacent-paragraph span MERGE is dead as a live concern — the v1.451 deriver iterates units and never calls `speakerSpans` (grep: zero live callers; engine tests keep it as a harness helper), and merged spans split correctly across `[SAY:]` segments (field-fixture-tested). Deleting the now-dead `speakerSpans` + retiring its tests can ride the eventual fix commit. ⚠ Any splitter change alters unit counts → stored speaker maps degrade via the `sp.n` fuse (mono-voice replay, the designed behavior — v1.423 precedent). Low-frequency, no text loss. **①b NEW from the entry-7 Fable review (2026-07-27, brief E): a SECOND cross-assignment class with well-formed quotes** — `_sayNorm` strips quote marks from segment text, so the deriver is quote-blind inside a segment: narration in speaker A's segment that happens to contain the text of speaker B's later tagged line captures B's line into A's voice (probe W1: `Frizwick will only say ten out of ten, as always.` inside Daeris's segment stole Frizwick's `"Ten out of ten,"`). Needs quote-aware matching (require the key's hit to start inside a quoted run of the raw segment) — same session as ①, same test bed. Sharpened ① evidence: a `[SAY:]` tag CONVERTS a parity fault from silent-flat (no tags → map null) into audible wrong-voice, a following tag bounds the over-reach but hands the leftover narration to the WRONG second voice, and a paragraph break confines the damage (`_inQ` resets per paragraph); a stray CLOSING quote (probe Y8) is unsafe the same way. Splitter note: the parity counter counts `["”]` but not `“`, so curly quotes orphan each closer into its own unit — theoretical today (0 curly in 13,505 GM quote chars, brief D census), record only. Untested shapes list (2a/2b/2g/multi-tag 2c/tag-inside-quote 2h) in the entry-7 verdict. | M | Sonnet | Filed 2026-07-24 from the Fable review of entry 4; rescoped 2026-07-27 after the #96 cutover (user asked "do we even need this?" — answer: ② no, ① more than before, ③ barely); ①b + sharpened evidence from the entry-7 review (v1.462 — the Z2/Z3/Z4 normalization half was fixed THERE, this row owns the two cross-assignment classes); full observed outputs in both review records → **Assigned to Sol (Codex) 2026-08-03** — brief in [DOC/todos_completed/SOL_TASKS.md](DOC/todos_completed/SOL_TASKS.md). <br><br>✅ **SHIPPED v1.603 (2026-08-12).** TLDR: narration can no longer be read aloud in a character's voice, a tagged line can no longer be stolen by another speaker, and lone quote marks no longer reach synthesis as audio blips. **① (tts.js `splitSentences`):** a paragraph whose quote glyphs do not balance resolves in the SAFE direction — every unit in it narrates. It rewrites LABELS ONLY, so unit count and unit text are untouched and no persisted map trips the `sp.n` fuse on this account. Three exemptions, all sabotage-proven: the unterminated run STARTS the paragraph; the next paragraph opens on a quote (the continued-speech typography, B14c-pinned); or the paragraph is the response's LAST and ends with no terminal punctuation. **That third exemption is measured, not theorised** — a census of 23,858 real GM paragraphs (13 campaign exports + 6 harness corpora) found odd parity 27 times (0.113%; only 3 in 18,704 paragraphs of current-model prose), and in ALL 27 the unmatched glyph opened a character still SPEAKING when the response hit its output-token cap (23 ended with no terminal punctuation, several mid-word). The narration-after-a-stray-opener shape has ZERO field instances, so without the truncation exemption this rule's entire realized effect would have been flattening ~24 genuine cut-off speeches while preventing nothing. **①b (game.js `deriveSpeakerMapFromTags`):** each segment now carries a quoted-run MASK beside its quote-stripped text, and a key must LAND inside a quoted run. Quote state carries ACROSS segment boundaries — a `[SAY:]` tag does not reset parity, and `"[SAY:X]Ten out of ten,"` puts the opener in the PREVIOUS segment, so restarting per segment would narrate that correctly-authored shape flat forever AND nag the GM through `buildSayComplianceNudge` for prose it cannot improve — and RESETS at paragraph breaks, mirroring `splitSentences`' per-paragraph `_inQ`. **It caught a real one:** replaying the 23 `[SAY:]`-tagged responses held in the saves, t1667 had Frizwick's `"There,"` spoken in **Daeris's** voice — narration inside Daeris's segment (`...stays there, small, deliberate`) matched the key first and the forward-only cursor never reached Frizwick's segment. Now correct. **③:** punctuation-only units fold into a neighbour (backward, and the paragraph's FIRST unit forward, which the old `k > 0` bound could not reach) — text is moved, never dropped; a wholly-punctuation paragraph has no neighbour and is left exactly as it was. **② `speakerSpans` DELETED** (dead since the v1.451 rework); its surviving invariant is asserted directly on units in the B14b test. **Verification:** 1333 green, 14 new tests all written failing-first (6 red before the fix); a 14-mutation sabotage battery in which every guarded branch trips a check and both sources restore byte-identically — it also caught two branches the first pass left unproven (the forward fold, the paragraph reset) and four stale needles as no-op mutations. **Blast radius, measured old-vs-new over the 3,331-entry t1667 campaign (2.08M chars of GM prose):** junk units 87 → 13 (the 13 are punctuation-only paragraphs with no neighbour); 58 entries (1.74%) change unit count; 5 entries change labels, ALL of them flattening, ZERO gaining a dialogue label; 24 of 611 stored speaker maps degrade to mono-voice replay via the designed `sp.n` fuse (the v1.423 precedent); compliance-nudge firing unchanged (4 → 4). **Residuals, honestly:** curly quotes are absent from the real corpus (0 of 23,085 glyphs), so every curly branch is pinned by test only and has no field evidence behind it; the clause regex still drops a leading `,` when a piece opens on one (inaudible, a separate class, not fixed here); and the defect shape surviving inside a TRUNCATED final paragraph is deliberately unprotected, because there the truncation reading wins. <br><br>⚠️ **v1.603 SHIPPED A REGRESSION; CORRECTED IN v1.604 (2026-08-12).** TLDR: the first cut built its quoted-run mask from the RAW response while the units it must match come from the CLEAN text, and that asymmetry could hand a tagged line to the WRONG CHARACTER — the exact harm this row exists to remove. Found by an adversarial review pass (3 census agents over 23,858 real paragraphs + 3 critique agents + an adjudicator that re-executed all 17 findings in isolated processes), reproduced by hand against v1.602 before any of it was believed. **The three v1.603 defects, all now fixed and sabotage-pinned:** ① `cleanTxt` CREATES paragraph breaks the raw does not have, by stripping a tag that sat alone on its own line; `splitSentences` reset its quote state there and the mask did not, so the correct in-quote hit was rejected and the forward-only cursor walked into a LATER speaker's segment — `[SAY:Daeris]"First part.\n[SAY:Frizwick]\n"Second part," he says.\n\n[SAY:Cyd]"Second part," she echoes.` gave Frizwick's own line to **Cyd**. ② a quote glyph inside ANY stripped tag payload (`[NPC_NOTE:…she keeps saying "ten out of ten]`) toggled the mask, so every later key landed unmasked and the WHOLE map went null — the entire response narrating flat. ③ flattening the whole paragraph killed correctly-tagged balanced quotes that merely shared a paragraph with a stray glyph. **The v1.604 rule is role-based, not parity-based:** `quoteFault()` classifies each glyph by typography (text-left + space-right closes; space-left + text-right opens; ambiguous falls back to parity) and returns the FIRST unmatched glyph, and only the quote runs at or after that fault are demoted — so an earlier closed quotation keeps its voice. The ambiguity fallback is load-bearing and was found by measurement: `normalizeForTTS` rewrites `...` to an ellipsis PLUS A SPACE, so a real closer in `And you..."` ends up with whitespace on both sides; without the fallback the rule misfired on 41 units across 22 corpus documents, with it, 4. **The next-paragraph exemption is RETIRED** — one ordinary quoted paragraph appended after a faulty one disarmed the whole rule (restoring the wrong-voice bug), it applied at 27% of paragraph positions, and the shape it protected has zero occurrences in the corpus; the genuine continued-speech fixture is protected by the opens-paragraph exemption, verified by ablation. **Deriver fixes:** cleanTxt's own regexes strip tags before the scan (silently — reusing its catch-all `console.warn` would be permanent console noise), a `tail` seam on the shared state detects a break that STRADDLES the `[SAY:]` cut (stripping alone does NOT fix that case — the tag creating the break is the SAY tag itself, which is in no slice; this was built and measured, not assumed), and a FLATTENED unit now consumes the forward-only cursor while taking no voice, so its text cannot be re-claimed by a later speaker's identical tagged line. Folds are voice-aware in layers — a hard gate at the piece level, preference-order at the paragraph level (a hard gate there resurrects the lone-quote blip on `"...," she said.`). **Measured v1.602 → v1.604 across 3,902 real GM documents:** junk units 164 → 26, B14c straddlers 1 → **0** (better than the pre-#93 baseline, which had one), dialogue units 20,872 → 20,720, 15 demotions across 100 changed documents, and **ZERO narration→dialogue promotions** — the safe direction holds absolutely. 1338 green; an 18-mutation sabotage battery, each mutation in its OWN PROCESS because the deriver's helpers are file-scope globals and in-process reloading silently contaminates every snapshot (that trap bit both me and one review agent). Sabotage also caught a worthless check of mine: the cursor-consumption guard is only exercised when the duplicate text sits INSIDE the flattened run, because an earlier closed quote advances the cursor by itself — the test now pins the shape that actually proves it. **Residual, deliberately left:** truncated speech whose cap happened to land on a period (`…Before the centuries.`) still flattens after its first closed quote — widening the truncation exemption to ANY trailing fault in a final paragraph would drop the corpus label changes to zero, but retires that protection for most GM dialogue, which sits in final paragraphs. **Filed separately, NOT #93:** `sayTagCoverage`/`paragraphGaps` precision defects (an `/i`-flagged attribution regex matching scare-quoted narration; `paragraphGaps` as an unconditional floor on `missing`; `qm` searched on raw text while `plain` is tag-stripped) make `buildSayComplianceNudge` fire on responses whose speaker map is 100% correct — identical on v1.602 and v1.604, so #93 neither caused nor worsens them, and folding a detector redesign into a regression fix would be wrong. |

Engine tests: bidirectional coverage guard (sabotage-proven), ladder shape, both injection halves, ''-clean at zero. **Mechanics model was Fable's call (user unavailable at the fork): bonus+auto-success over advantage tiers/DC shift — deliberately data-only, a rebalance is a 6-line edit in SKILL_LEVEL_MECHANICS.** bible_editor support deferred (BIBLE_TYPES entry when needed). | M | Fable | ✅ Complete 2026-08-06 (v1.545). **v1.546 follow-up (doc rot):** the STATE TAGS `[SKILL_SUCCESS:]` exact-ids doc line was hand-maintained and had rotted — Explosives (a real SKILLS Craft entry with a skills-bible def) never entered it, so the GM was never told it could award the skill. The id list now DERIVES from SKILLS in tag_table.js (data.js loads first; load-time constant, so the cache byte-identity contract holds), pinned by a bidirectional engine guard (doc ids ⇆ SKILLS, proven red-first on the missing id); frozen doc hash re-baselined (+12 = ", Explosives"). |
| 72c | **#72 COMPLETED RECORD — class bible: origin, spec, editor, authored classes, and the C1–C7 engine wiring.** TLDR: the finished half of #72, moved here 2026-08-03 so the open row stays legible — nothing in this row is open work. <br><br>**ORIGIN + DESIGN (the original row):** **Player levelling has holes — audit and design a real progression, per class.** TLDR: levelling up mostly gives HP and an occasional one-line passive; casters get NO new spells after level 3, two levels give nothing at all, and the XP table dead-ends at 10. **Traced 2026-07-18 from "what happened when Ammut hit level 9?" — the answer was: +HP and one passive (Blindsense), nothing else.** Findings: **①** `checkLevelUp` never touches `character.spells` — spells are granted at creation (capped tier 3 by `SPELL_PICK_LIMITS`) and at the level-3 archetype pick, and never again; `initSpells` only backfills an EMPTY list, so it is not a progression path. A level 9 Arcane Trickster holds the same book they had at level 3 plus GM ad-hoc grants, and `SPELLS` has a Necromancer tier 4 no player can ever reach by progression. **②** The curve is lumpy: L2/5/7/9 = one class feature, L3 = archetype, L4/8 = stat bump, **L6 and L10 = HP only** — and L10, the last level in the game, is one of the empty ones. **③** `getLvl` hard-clamps at 10 (64,000 XP); past that XP accrues and buys nothing (Ammut sits at 51,100 on a t833 campaign, so the ceiling is close). Decide: soft-cap with prestige-style rewards · extend the table · or deliberately end campaigns there. **④** Cosmetic: features are stored `nm:"Lv9"`, so the sheet lists an ability literally named "Lv9" instead of "Blindsense". **THE WORK:** go class by class (9 classes) and design a full 1-10 progression — features, spell/slot growth, and what fills the dead levels — then land it in a **`class_bible.js`**. **Naming checked (user asked whether `class` is a protected word):** `class_bible`/`CLASS_BIBLE` are perfectly legal — only the BARE identifier `class` is reserved, and this codebase already ships `CLASS_FEATURES` as a global. The real risk is CONCEPTUAL collision, not syntax. **SCOPE RULED 2026-07-18 (user): a single definitive source — `CLSS` is MIGRATED THEN DELETED, not kept as an alias** ("leaving both alive is how you get a third source six months from now"). data.js has **seven** class-keyed tables, 52 refs, almost all in char-creation.js + game.js: `CLSS` (30 refs — hd/prime/gear/desc) · `CLASS_FEATURES` (4) · `ABILS` (2, starting abilities) · `ARCHETYPES` (6) · `ARCH_SPELLS` (3) · `STAT_PRIORITY` (3) · `SPELLS` (4). **The bible absorbs all of them EXCEPT `SPELLS`** — spell MECHANICS already have a canonical home in `capability_bible.js`, and folding them in would re-fragment exactly what #10 unified. Instead the class bible lists spell NAMES per tier and the capability bible owns what they do — the pattern `racial_caps` already uses for ancestries. **The class bible holds progression and references, not mechanics.** **SEQUENCING (do not big-bang this — it drives creation AND `checkLevelUp`):** ① introduce **`classDef(id)` as THE lookup first**, collapsing ~30 hand-rolled `for(i…) if(CLSS[i].id===c.cls)` loops (there is no such helper today — the same smell `wsNpcByName` fixed for NPCs) into one function; pure refactor, independently valuable, testable in isolation. ② THEN swap the backing store to `CLASS_BIBLE` — now a one-line change *inside that helper*, because nothing else touches the table directly. ③ Delete `CLSS`. **Invariant test either way: an existing character's derived values must not move** — load a live save before/after and assert identical hit die, features, archetype list, and stat priority; a silent shift there corrupts saves.<br><br>**THE SHIPPING LOG (the original status cell):** Filed 2026-07-18. Design-first: the progression tables are a balance question, not a coding one. ⛨ Touches the level-up write path + the char sheet — engine-test the single-player invariants and existing saves (a mid-campaign character must not retroactively gain or lose anything). **Spec at R2, ALL FORKS RULED (user, 2026-07-27): [DOC/Research/DOC_class_bible.html](DOC/Research/DOC_class_bible.html).** R1 same day carried the measured inventory (85/85 class spells already resolve in the capability bible; archetypes confirmed pure flavor — one `{nm,desc}` ability at L3, nothing after). Rulings: **C2 = PICKS** at tier-unlock levels (reuse the creation picker; full casters T2@5 T3@7 T4@9 T5@11 T6@15, content caps at T6, tier system stays open-ended beyond) · **C4/C4b = EXTEND the table to L20** (the full classic arc, overriding the soft-cap rec; draft XP curve to ~355k@20; `STAT_BUMP_LEVELS`→`[4,8,12,16,19]`) · **C5 = creation-only familiarity seeds** (`skillSeeds`, 2-3 signature skills at Familiar; existing saves untouched). The 1–20 grid: class 2/5/7/9/11/13/15/17 · archetype 3/6/10/14/18 + CAPSTONE@20 · bumps 4/8/12/16/19 — every level grants something. Honest authoring bill at L20 scope: ~250–260 entries (36 class features + 162 archetype rows + ~55 T5/T6 spells), shippable per-class. **Iteration 2 SHIPPED v1.464 (redefined by the user: skeleton + editor first, user fills data):** `class_bible.js` generated from the live engine tables (9 classes · 27 archetypes · 234 level slots, 36 seeded with REAL feature names — the `nm:"Lv9"` bug structurally dead — 198 fill-phase blanks · XP 1–20) + **`bible_editor.html`**, the dev-only authoring satellite (SEPARATE from read-only bible_study by user call — bibles may become player-facing). Editor: per-name resolve badges, fixed-6-attribute capability define form, localStorage draft, exports regenerated file + paste-ready capability additions. Guards: BIBLE EDITOR CONTRACT (serializer↔file byte-pin, sabotage-proven both directions) + coverage guard (every bible spell name must resolve in capability_bible — additions merge in the SAME commit) + 6 structural engine tests (871 green). NOT engine-wired: legacy tables remain truth until C6. **v1.467 — EDITOR v2: open a bible, save back over it** (user: a big spell fill exported to ~/Downloads instead of the project dir). File System Access API — verified live on `file://` — gives a real handle, so **Save overwrites the original in place** behind a confirm; a lapsed handle parks behind a **Reconnect** button re-granted inside a click (the #30 permission lesson); no-FSA browsers get a file input + download fallback, stated loudly. **Bible types are now a REGISTRY** (`BIBLE_TYPES`: detect/parse/serialize/render — the item bible later is ONE entry, not new branches): **class_bible** (regenerated wholesale) + **capability_bible** (hand-commented → untouched entries re-emit as their ORIGINAL SOURCE LINES, comments preserved in place, so a save is a minimal diff — 175 entries, 18 comments, verified). New capability view: filterable list, per-entry edit through the same 6-attribute form, add/delete. Draft shape v2, and **v1 class-only drafts are MIGRATED, never discarded** (a fill session must survive the upgrade). Contract extended and sabotage-proven 4/4 — ⚠ the first version MISSED two mutations because it only exercised the unedited path, leaving `emit()` (the code that writes your edits) untested; an all-dirty round-trip clause now pins it. Live testing also caught the capability view not refreshing the dirty flag / edited count (renderCap bypassed the shell refresh; markDirty now updates both synchronously). 874 green. <br><br>**v1.466 — FIRST ARCHETYPE AUTHORED: Arcane Trickster, end to end** (user approved 2026-07-28: "I like it, might lead to some fun situations"). Six features + six paired capability entries in one commit: **L3 The Old Switcheroo** (Mage Hand swaps two objects, both holders keep believing) · **L6 It Was Their Idea** (planted suggestion remembered as the target's own) · **L10 Seventy-Two Shapes** (take any form you've seen, holds until dropped) · **L14 Never Caught** (first seizure each scene simply doesn't happen) · **L18 The Story Runs Ahead** (a rumour is true by the time it arrives) · **L20 Turnabout** (CAPSTONE — exchange place, bonds, condition and blame with another creature). Spine verb = **the SWAP**: object → idea → face → blame → story → fate. Register is Loki/Anansi/Wukong per the user's redirect ("trickster" = mischief and chaos, NOT the shadow-operative first draft — that material is now earmarked for Thief/Assassin). ⚠ **A rejected capstone produced the enforceability ceiling** (below): the first draft erased the character from a past event, which the user flagged as dangerous to RAG — correctly. This is the **TEMPLATE** the other 26 archetypes follow; 42/234 slots filled. <br><br>**2026-07-28 — the ENFORCEABILITY CEILING, the second authoring test (spec Part II-a):** a feature's blast radius must not exceed the engine's vocabulary for enforcing it. **Generative is cheap, subtractive is expensive** — creating facts is native; unwriting them has almost no vocabulary (`worldState.transcript` is immutable by decree, `[RETCON:]` de-indexes exactly TWO entries, and an event also lives in chapters/eventHistory/lore/keyDecisions/npcs.knowledge/npcs.events/npcGraph/quests/storyBeats/coreMemories-across-every-sheet). RAG would re-serve the original scene non-deterministically → silent drift, reading as a broken ability. Survivable form: scope "they forget" to what people KNOW (current-state blocks outrank RAG excerpts by design), never to the record. Literal event erasure = a real `[EVENT_ERASE:]` feature (turn-RANGE rc mark + witness fan-out + summary-tier scrub), Fable-tier, its own session — **not filed as a row, deliberately; raise it if the fiction ever demands it.** Both this and the narratability test render in the editor's Archetypes header. <br><br>**2026-07-28 — the CONCEPT behind the rubric (spec Part II-a):** class answers "what can you do", archetype answers "who are you when you do it" — class levels are training, archetype levels are *becoming*. Load-bearing for THIS engine: features are injected as canon every turn, so an archetype level is **a sentence the GM permanently knows about the character**, not a sheet number — therefore features must be **narratable, not numeric** ("+2 to hit" is invisible to an LLM GM; "you can always tell when someone is lying to you about money" gets reached for unprompted). Placement test = **blast radius**: yourself (L3–6) → the scene (L10–14) → the world (L18) → the story (L20). Both the narratability test and the radius ladder now render in the editor's Archetypes header, where the 162 slots actually get filled. <br><br>**v1.465 — archetype levels DEFINED (user: "define archetype levels for me"):** ① the **rubric** — each of the six archetype rows has a stated job (L3 IDENTITY · L6 REFINEMENT · L10 REACH · L14 MASTERY · L18 APEX · L20 CAPSTONE), shown in the editor at the point of authoring so 162 slots aren't 162 arbitrary calls (class rows deliberately have none — the class is the chassis, the archetype is the character); ② **C7, the third-caster gap CLOSED** — Arcane Trickster + Eldritch Knight had spell benches but NO unlock schedule (spellTiers was generated only for the 6 full/half-caster classes), so AT's T4/Oubliate was unreachable under the C2 picks ruling. Tiers now key to the archetype's own rows: **T1@3 · T2@10 · T3@14 · T4@18**; EK gains blank T3/T4 for the fill phase. Editor gained per-archetype tier controls (the schedule was un-tunable before) + the rubric line. Engine-tested (872 green): both caster archetypes carry a schedule, every unlock level must BE one of that archetype's rows, every unlockable tier has a spells array. **v1.472 — C6 ① SHIPPED: classDef()/classDefs() are THE class lookup.** TLDR: every hand-rolled CLSS scan in the engine now routes through two helpers, so the ② store swap is an edit inside them and nowhere else. helpers.js gains classDefs() (the list) + classDef(id) (exact canonical-id match first, then a trimmed case-insensitive fallback — the normalizeCompanionSheet model-output path, byte-identical behavior there; null on miss so every caller keeps its own fallback: getMHP 8, companionBaselineHp hd 10, unknown-class hpGain 3). Rewired: checkLevelUp, checkCompanionLevelUp, buildCompanionSheetPrompt, companionBaselineHp, normalizeCompanionSheet (game.js) · buildClsGrid, pickCls + gear preview, both cls/anc scans, the AI-assist ctx (char-creation.js) · getMHP (helpers.js). Grep proves CLSS is read ONLY inside classDefs(). 5 new engine tests written FIRST (879 green): classDefs↔CLSS identity, all-9 exact resolve to the same objects, CI+trim fallback, the null contract, and the normalizeCompanionSheet canonicalization pinned before the change. Wizard verified live in the browser (9-cell grid, pick → selection + gear preview, zero console errors). **v1.474 — SECOND ARCHETYPE AUTHORED: the Assassin, end to end** (user-directed slate 2026-07-28 — L6/L10 user-authored, L14/L18 picked from candidates, ledger framing rejected and memorialized). Spine verb = **the ENDING**: **L3 The First Blow Is the Last** (strike an unsuspecting target — auto-crit, fight often over before it begins) · **L6 Master Poisoner** (any food/weapon lethally poisoned instantly, creatively) · **L10 Sure Shot** (1/combat, an impossible ranged shot hits critical — ricochets, bank shots, needle-threading; if you can see it you can kill it) · **L14 A Natural Death** (kills wear whatever face you choose; shapes what the world BELIEVES, never the record — the ceiling-survivable form) · **L18 Angel of Death** (per-day, nothing bars your approach to a target **within 150ft** — the user's end-run guard baked into the range attribute and pinned by test) · **L20 The Inevitable End** (CAPSTONE, 1/campaign: name a target, their death becomes when-not-if; the world supplies opportunity, never the kill). All six MUNDANE (isMagical:false, category martial — no caster tradition draws them; an enemy martial could). Six paired capability entries + six class rows in ONE commit per the coverage discipline; red-first template test (six rows, all resolve, all martial, capstone + 150ft pinned); 885 green; editor live-verified (Rogue view, six green resolve badges). **52/234 slots filled.** The recovered shadow-operative material (Never the Suspect, Memory Thief) was ultimately CUT here too (L10 went to Sure Shot, L14 not-death-oriented) — it remains available for the Thief, whose slate is drafted and awaiting the user's notes. **v1.475 — THIRD ARCHETYPE: the Thief (slate approved as drafted 2026-07-28) — ROGUE COMPLETE, all three archetypes authored.** Spine verb = **the TAKE** (object → lock → place → person → world → the impossible): **L3 Legerdemain** · **L6 No Lock Holds** · **L10 The Casing Eye** · **L14 The Shirt Off Your Back** · **L18 Word of the Score** (passive — rumor of every score finds you) · **L20 The Impossible Theft** (CAPSTONE, 1/campaign: steal a god's flame, a house's luck — GENERATIVE by construction, 'creates a possession, never unwrites the past', the ceiling clause test-pinned in the effect text). All martial/non-magical like the Assassin. 886 green; editor live-verified. **58/234 slots filled.** The recovered Never the Suspect / Memory Thief material is now formally UNUSED — three Rogue archetypes shipped without it; keep or retire at will. **v1.476 — WARRIOR ⅔ AUTHORED: Champion + Battle Master** (slates approved 2026-07-28; A Call to Arms user-authored). **Champion**, spine = **the FEAT** (Achilles/Heracles, mundane): L3 The Telling Blow · L6 Peak of the Body · L10 Last One Standing · L14 One Against Many · L18 Strength of Legend · L20 The Challenge (CAPSTONE 1/campaign — call any creature by name to single combat; the circle admits no outside power). **Battle Master**, spine = **the READ**: L3 The Read · L6 Wordless Command · L10 The Turning Move (1/combat) · L14 The Battlefield Is a Board · L18 **A Call to Arms** (conscript **5d10 locals, each level 2d6**, armed to station — **one army at a time, raising anew disbands the old**, the anti-fishing limit; dice + limit clause test-pinned) · L20 The Plan Holds (CAPSTONE 1/campaign — steps succeed as described; 'the world may complicate the aftermath, never the steps' test-pinned; NOTE: the first capstone draft was a retroactive this-was-the-plan-all-along reveal, self-rejected as a ceiling violation — retroactive fact-creation about the past is the event-erasure class). All 12 mundane martial. 887 green; editor live-verified. **70/234 slots filled.** **v1.477 — the Eldritch Knight: WARRIOR COMPLETE (2nd full class).** Spine = **the BOND** (blade and spell one thing), and unlike its siblings it is **MAGICAL/arcane** — an enemy arcane caster can draw these (the category gate). L3 Weapon Bond · L6 Spellblade · **L10 Sundering Stroke** · **L14 Soul Steal** · L18 The Standing Ward · L20 The Blade Keeps Faith (CAPSTONE — death refuses the bond; 'what happened still happened, you simply are not dead of it', ceiling-clean). **Two user calls shaped it:** ① **Soul Steal** replaced the drafted Warded Edge — a Stormbringer beat, user-authored: the blade drinks the soul of what it cuts, taking one ability for **4 hours**, **three held at once**, nothing new taken while full (a TRANSFER — the victim lacks it while you hold it; present-state, ceiling-clean, and the 4h window rides the #73 clock). All three limits ride in the injected canon and are test-pinned, because the limits ARE the ability's shape. ② **L10/L14 SWAPPED** (user: 'seem like they should switch places' — agreed: cut magic at REACH, drink beings at MASTERY; it also moves the strongest non-capstone feature in the book off L10 to sit with its true peers). Order test-pinned so it can't drift back. Also added: a **half-authored guard** — every archetype must have 6 rows filled or 0, never in between (a partial archetype reads to the GM as a character with gaps). 889 green; editor live-verified (six green badges, swapped order confirmed in the DOM). **76/234 slots filled — Rogue and Warrior both COMPLETE.** **v1.482 — the WARRIOR CLASS LADDER authored to a user-specified slate** (2026-07-28; the class rows, distinct from the archetype spines). L2 Action Surge (unchanged, "good as is") · **L5 Indomitable** (moved L7→L5) · **L7 Resilience** (recover 1 HP per level per hour — a Warrior who walks off a battlefield is whole by nightfall, no healer) · **L9 Taunt** (foes attack YOU, not your companions) · **L11 Double Attack** · **L13 Counter Attack** (d20 per incoming melee attack; under your level it is countered outright and you answer). Two deliberate displacements: **Extra Attack and Indomitable(x2) LEAVE the ladder** (the attack upgrade returns at L11 as Double Attack). L15/L17 stay blank — not specified; a blank is a fill-phase slot, not an error. All six are mundane martial, and all six **close six of the ~30 seeded class features that carried prose but no injectable canon** — the GM now gets real mechanics, not a one-line label. ⚠ Two judgment calls flagged rather than buried: **Taunt** had no stated duration/limit and an unbounded forced-target is unenforceable, so it ships 1/combat · 3 rounds · WIS save for named foes (a test pins that it stays bounded); **Counter Attack** is implemented exactly as specified including its scaling — **60% of incoming melee countered at L13, 95% at L20**, very strong and auto-growing, flagged for a later balance pass if it plays hot. 895 green. The Lv9-naming canary legitimately broke (its fixture WAS Warrior L9) — repointed to Primal L9, a class not yet re-authored; the general no-Lv<N> scan that is the actual guard is untouched. Live-verified in the editor. **78/234 slots filled.** ⚠ NOT live in game: class_bible is not engine-wired until C6-②, so data.js CLASS_FEATURES remains truth for play — expected fill-phase divergence, documented in the file header. **v1.483 — Warrior L15 + the Counter Attack rebalance** (user call same day, on the flagged scaling). **Counter Attack now halves damage instead of negating it**, still buying the free answering strike — a TRADE (eat part of the blow to open the attacker up) rather than the flat immunity it became at high level, where the counter fires on 95% of incoming melee at L20. User's own framing: "that way a warrior can open up a target to sacrifice a little damage to deal a lot." The trade rides in the effect text so the GM narrates it as a choice; the test pins BOTH halves (roll-under-level AND damage halved, not negated). **L15 Iron Constitution** (user-specified): every CON save auto-succeeds except on a natural 1 — **the exception IS the feature** (an auto-pass with no failure case gives the GM nothing to narrate), test-pinned in the injected canon. 895 green, editor-verified. **79/234 slots.** **L17 remains OPEN** — the last Warrior class row, pending the user's pick from the offered options (Unstoppable / Triple Attack / Killing Ground). **v1.484 — WARRIOR COMPLETE: L5 Stunning Blow + L17 Unstoppable** (user picks, same session). **L17 Unstoppable** (chosen from three offered): while you still have hit points nothing takes you out of the fight — no stun, paralysis, restraint, prone, disarm, grapple, or forced movement, mundane or magical. It finishes the sentence Iron Constitution starts (L15: the body can't be broken down · L17: it can't be stopped) — *"You can be killed. You cannot be handled."* The **while-you-have-HP bound is test-pinned**; without it the immunity would absurdly persist while downed. **L5 Stunning Blow replaces Indomitable** (user: "what I don't really love is Indomitable"): forgo a hit's damage to stun instead, CON save negates, and **any creature can be caught by it only ONCE** — a foe who has felt it never falls for the same blow twice. That per-target limit reads as character rather than bookkeeping and makes the feature a decision, not a rotation; all three parts (trade · save · once-per-target) are test-pinned because all three ARE the shape of the feature. Nice emergent rhyme: the Warrior learns to stun at L5 and becomes unstunnable at L17. **Indomitable stays IN capability_bible** though it left the ladder — legacy CLASS_FEATURES still grants it at L7 in live play until C6-②, and the coverage guard only runs one way (a bible NAME must resolve; not every entry must be used). **WARRIOR is the first class complete in both halves** — all 8 class rows (2/5/7/9/11/13/15/17) AND all three archetypes (Champion · Battle Master · Eldritch Knight). 895 green, editor-verified, **80/234 slots filled**. **Next: the remaining 7 classes (Sorcerer/Ranger/Primal/Paladin/Cleric/Druid/Necromancer — Rogue's class rows are already 8/8 from the earlier seed, its archetypes done); then C6 ②③ store swap + CLSS delete (Fable, save-invariant tested).** **2026-07-31 — CLERIC ROUGHED OUT (draft, awaiting the user's review).** TLDR: all 22 blank Cleric slots are filled with narratable prose and the four seeded class rows re-authored, so the class reads end to end — but **no paired capability entries yet**, deliberately: the names may move under review, and the coverage discipline says they land in the approval commit. Class ladder (chassis = divine authority): L2 Channel Divinity (*the god answers the priest, not the request*) · L5 Destroy Undead · L7 Divine Strike · L9 Divine Intervention (d100 under level; answered or not, silent for seven days) · **L11 Consecrated Ground** (one at a time, the Call-to-Arms limit shape) · **L13 The Blessing Holds** (boons run to your next rest, not to the end of the fight) · **L15 Death Waits** (1/rest, and never the same creature twice in a day) · **L17 Smite** (user-authored 2026-07-31, replacing the moved The Word: one blow equal to 95% of the target's remaining hit points, delivered in the deity's own idiom — thunderclap, ghostly kraken tentacles — GM's choice of manifestation. Two bounds added by Opus and flagged: **1/day**, and **95% of REMAINING hp — never a killing blow**, so the god brings a foe to death's door and the kill still belongs to the party). Domains, each with a spine verb: **Life = the MENDING** (domain review 2026-07-31, user rulings applied: Disciple of Life · **Cleanse** (up from L10) · **Preserve Life** (down to L10) · **Restore Limb** at L14 (new, replacing The Wound Does Not Take — regrows anything short of a head) · **Battlefield Miracle** at L18 (renamed from No One Dies Today, restricted to ALLIES — enemies enjoy no such protection) · **The Return**, capstone 1/campaign, **cost clause removed** (user: it threw the capstone out of whack with its peers)) · **War = the FIGHT — user-approved as drafted** (War Priest · Guided Strike · The Line Holds · Consecrated Arms · The God of Battle Attends · **Decide the Battle**) · **SUBJUGATION = the YOKE** (review applied: **The Yoke** up to L6, **The Knee** down to L10; L14 renamed **The Missive**) (user ruling 2026-07-31: the drafted Trickery Domain duplicated the Arcane Trickster's spine — 4 of 6 rows on the same beats — so it was cut same-day and replaced; heal / kill / OWN, three verbs with no shared surface): **The Knee** (your voice presses wills — never puppets bodies) · **The Oath** (willingly-spoken promises bind) · **The Yoke** (bind the defeated into awake, resentful service, one at a time) · **Nothing Refuses You** (compulsion without presence, one standing command abroad) · **The Word** (moved from class L17 — it IS the domain identity; one command in the god's voice, everyone who hears obeys) · **The Law** (capstone 1/campaign — one decree the land itself enforces until a god's own authority overturns it). **Necromancer boundary, ruled by the user:** Possess Thrall is full possession (the necromancer INSIDE the thrall, its eyes and mouth); Subjugation only SUPERSEDES the target's will — awake, aware, no connection between cleric and target. Baked into the L10 prose so the two never converge. The rename shipped as the v1.506 id-alignment commit (trickery→subjugation rode `ARCHETYPE_ID_RENAMES`). **2026-07-31 — SORCERER ROUGHED OUT (draft, same review-first shape as the Cleric: no capability entries until approval).** User slate: **Bayaz / Elric / Raistlin / Doctor Strange — magic has a price and the body is the ledger.** Class ladder 8/8 (seeded rows re-authored + 4 new): L2 Font of Magic · L5 Metamagic · L7 Sorcerous Restoration · L9 Overchannel (max effect, d6/tier self-damage unhealable until sleep) · **L11 Arcane Sight** · **L13 Split Weave** (two concentrations) · **L15 Body of Magic** · **L17 Witnessed Once, Worked Once** (1/day cast any spell ever seen cast). Domains-of-the-arcane, spine verbs: **Draconic Bloodline = the BLOOD** (Old Blood · Elemental Vein · Presence of the Wyrm · Wings · Wyrmform 1/day · **The Progenitor's Due** — capstone 1/campaign, the ancestor dragon answers in the flesh) · **Wild Magic = the SURGE** (Tides of Chaos · Bend Luck · Controlled Chaos — the surge offers two, you pick · Chaos Feeds You · Unraveling Presence · **Rewrite the Odds** — capstone, declare one chance outcome) · **Shadow Magic = the DARK** (Eyes of the Dark · Hound of Ill Omen · Shadow Walk · The Shadow Half · The Dark Between · **Sovereign of the Night** — capstone, dusk-to-dawn the night is yours). skillSeeds proposed Arcana + Lore. T4–6 spell benches stay blank (no arcane entries at those tiers yet — new authoring). **124/234 slots.** Spell bench gained only names that already resolve: T1 Command/Sanctuary/Detect Magic · T2 Augury/Zone of Truth · T4 Divination; **T5/T6 stay blank — no divine capability entry exists at those tiers yet** (they are new authoring, not a fill). skillSeeds proposed as Lore + First Aid. 939 green; the file was re-serialized through the editor's own serializer, so the BIBLE EDITOR CONTRACT byte-pin holds. **102/234 slots.** <br><br>**C6 COMPLETE (2026-08-03, v1.533 ② + v1.534 ③).** The bible IS the engine store: classDefs()/classDef() swapped to CLASS_BIBLE (castStat ported first — 8 classes, canonical re-serialize), every sibling-table consumer routed through the accessor family (classFeaturesAt/archFeaturesAt/classXpLevels), level-ups grant NAMED class + archetype rows for player and companions, getLvl's hidden 10-cap removed (L11 gate 85000, L20=355000), class_bible.js joined index.html + the SW app shell and left the network-first regex, and the six legacy tables (CLSS/ABILS/ARCHETYPES/CLASS_FEATURES/XP_LEVELS/STAT_PRIORITY) are DELETED from data.js. The spec's invariant is a frozen-literal engine test (hd/prime/castStat/statPriority captured from the live legacy tables minutes before the swap + XP 1-10 verbatim) and was proven against the live t1385 save: Ammut keeps level/hd/mana, next gate 85000, no retroactive grants. <br><br>**C2 COMPLETE (2026-08-03, v1.535).** Spell growth per the ruled spec: spellUnlocksCrossed() (pure, helpers.js) scans class + committed-archetype spellTiers over the crossed interval (half-open — no retroactive grants ever); the player gets a forced-choice tier picker (showSpellUnlockModal, stat-bump house style) queued after archetype/bumps and re-surfaced before the next turn; companions auto-pick silently; C7 third casters (AT/EK) key to the archetype schedule incl. catch-up at the pick; fill-phase blank benches (EK T3/T4) skip loudly; SPELL_UNLOCK_PICKS is the template-iteration count constant. Real-browser proof: Cleric 10→11 raised the actual modal, empty-confirm warned, over-select refused, Flame Strike landed as T5 and manaMax derived 6 (#110 interplay). 1008 green. Ammut's frozen-spells era ends at 85,000 XP. Remaining: fill-phase slots, half-caster tier drafts, per-class pick counts. | — | — | Record row (2026-08-03). Open work lives in #72. |
| 134 | **Missing-interior-description nudge — lived-in rooms get their canon pinned.** TLDR: the SECONDARY hardening from the t1431 multiplying-beds finding (sibling of #133, which carries the primary stale-splitLoc mechanism; full forensics in [Fable analysis](DOC/Research/t1431_drift_analysis_Fable.html) + [Sol analysis](DOC/Research/t1431_drift_analysis_Sol.html)): interior detail has exactly ONE pin, the write-once `[LOCATION_DESC:]`, and the GM filed it for 4/50 (8%) of the live save's sub-locations — an undescribed room's furniture lives only in prose, evaporates within ~2 summarize cycles, and gets re-imagined from genre priors on return. Fix: `buildLocationDescNudge` (api.js, NOTE_BUILDERS) — once the party is SETTLED at a node with `description=null` (past the arrival turn, deliberately: arrival responses are crowded and write-once means a rushed description is pinned forever), an engine note demands `[LOCATION_DESC:]` with PHYSICAL facts and countable furnishings ("facts, not mood"). Latch stamps on fire (`worldState.locDescNudged`); re-fires every `LOC_DESC_NUDGE_COOLDOWN`=10 turns while still null (#29 rot lesson); a filed description ends it permanently; silent in combat; fires only where the party actually dwells. ⚠ Drift surface (engine-note channel) — Fable, test-first. Ship-time confidence, recorded honestly: HIGH that descriptions get filed (the #20/#129 channel is field-proven), MODERATE that furniture drift fully stops (depends on the GM recording countable facts; and per #133 the t1431 incident had a stronger co-cause this nudge does not touch). | S | Fable | ✅ **v1.541**, 2026-08-04. Test-first: 3 engine tests failing → green (1023 total). Field verification: next lived-in undescribed room produces a description within ~2 turns of settling. |

Render-verified against the live t1431 state via seeded localhost preview — all three split members display correctly, present members keep vitals. | 132 | **Output-truncation detection — a response cut mid-tag must be loud, not a silent mutation loss plus raw text on screen.** TLDR: the engine has ZERO truncation detection — no provider adapter reads `stop_reason`/`finish_reason` — so when the 1000-token output cap cuts a GM response mid-tag, the trailing fragment renders RAW to the player (`cleanTxt`'s strip regexes require the closing `]`), the mutation is silently lost (all TAG_TABLE handlers match complete tags), and no warn fires anywhere (`__tagUnknownScan` needs a colon). Field case: Runelords t1410 (B21 evidence) ended `…was listening. [SCH` — the GM's demanded `[SCHEDULE_RESOLVED:…]` was cut and lost; harmless there only because the sweep retired the entry anyway, but a truncated `[QUEST:x\|completed]` would silently drop the completion AND leak raw text. Direction (B21 findings sketch ③): each provider's `parseResponse` also surfaces its finish reason; on length-truncation `callGM`/the commit path warns loudly (console + toast + #16 crumb) and strips the trailing unterminated `\[[A-Z_]*:?[^\]]*$` fragment from the DISPLAYED text only (transcript keeps the raw truth). ⚠ Drift surface (cleanTxt + frozen strip hashes, provider adapters, response-commit path) — Fable tier, design review before code; a greedy trailing-fragment strip could eat legitimate bracketed prose, so the regex must anchor to end-of-response. | S | Fable | ✅ **v1.538**, 2026-08-03. `parseFinish` on all 5 provider adapters (OpenAI-shaped share `OPENAI_FINISH`); callGM warns loud (console + toast + #16 crumb) on a length-cap finish; cleanTxt drops a trailing unterminated ≥3-caps tag fragment (end-anchored; complete tags and lowercase bracket prose untouched; sessionLog keeps the raw). Test-first: 2 tests failing → green (1016). |

Unmappable free text stays flavor (stored, clock untouched). Loud muts line on every reconcile. NOT taken (recorded): deriving/retiring `world.time` injection (GM keeps its narrative channel); editing the `[TIME:]` STATE-TAGS doc line (hash-pinned, behavior is engine-side); Sync-modal manual time edits still don't touch the clock (deliberate override). The field save's standing "dawn"-vs-3:15pm desync heals on the GM's next mappable `[TIME:]` write. Test-first: 4 engine tests incl. the verbatim field numbers (clock 6315 + `[TIME:dawn]` → 7200) → green, 1020 total. |

No data migration, no pruning — label only. Verified against the real save: the boundary lands at exactly index 111 on Ammut's 130-beat array (the Sandpoint-burns beat is the first native one). Both notes: one per turn (stalest), silent in combat, offered quests exempt (not accepted goals). The live save's stale entry heals on its first turn after deploy — the exact field numbers (due 357, clock 6005) are an engine test. Prompt + cache split untouched (notes ride the user message; stable-half purity suite green). | M | Fable | ✅ **v1.526**, 2026-08-02. Fixed expire-before-escalate: `buildScheduleEscalation` and the clock block's HAPPENING NOW now skip past-expiry entries (full record: DOC/BUGS.md B21). |

| 113 | **Whisper STT exploration — where should speech-to-text transcription actually run?** TLDR: today's STT is two paths (stt.js): native Web Speech API where it exists (Chrome/Edge desktop + Android; NOT Firefox, NOT iOS Safari), and a cloud fallback that records the utterance and sends it to OpenAI `whisper-1` **on the player's own OpenAI key** — the path that makes iPhone Safari / Car Mode work at all, and the key-ownership property that was decisive in declining LiveKit. Exploration (findings doc first, NO code): ① **Local WASM Whisper** (whisper.cpp / transformers.js) — the Piper #41 pattern: vendored, offline, $0, model cached in OPFS; questions: tiny/base model size (~40-150MB) vs fantasy-noun accuracy, cold-start + per-utterance latency on a phone, and WASM memory pressure on iOS Safari (the r8/jetsam monotonic-resources lesson — soak test, not one input). ② **Self-hosted Whisper on the tnd-tts Fly app** — the #90 M1 pattern: same warm-daemon/volume machinery, auth via the game session token, removes the OpenAI-key requirement for connected players; questions: per-utterance round-trip vs the 15s MediaRecorder cap, cost on our box, and whether the STT ladder should mirror `TTS_LADDER` (native → server → cloud-on-player-key → none). ③ **Status quo** as the control. Decision criteria: the iOS Safari / Car Mode path is the critical consumer (#78 Phase 2 wants spoken 1/2/3 action picking), accuracy on fantasy proper nouns (measure with and without the `sttCorrectNames` backstop — relates #77), and who pays. Output: a DOC/ findings page + a go/no-go per option; any build work becomes its own row. | M | Opus | ✅ **Exploration complete 2026-08-03** — findings: [DOC/Research/DOC_whisper_stt.html](DOC/Research/DOC_whisper_stt.html). Verdicts: local WASM **NO-GO** (least accuracy, worst latency, second iOS wasm memory ratchet — the B9 class); self-hosted on Fly **DEFER** to the subscription proxy era; status quo + four upgrades **GO** (send Whisper's prompt-bias param with roster/location names — we never have; model swap whisper-1 → gpt-4o-mini-transcribe with fallback; WebAudio silence endpointing replacing the 15s wall; mic-path constraints audit + narrowband-BT telemetry into #16 crumbs). Key car finding: the dominant factor is likely the Bluetooth hands-free mic (telephone-band capture) — engine-independent, measure-then-mitigate. **§4 upgrades SHIPPED v1.536 (user go 2026-08-03):** prompt-bias on every request (sttBiasPrompt, helpers — engine-tested; party/roster/places/quests, deduped, 800-char cap at a name boundary), gpt-4o-mini-transcribe primary with a LOUD whisper-1 retry + #16 crumb, WebAudio silence endpointing (1.5s quiet after speech ends the take; 45s hard cap replaces the 15s wall; noisy-cabin floor degrades to cap-only, never a cut-off; singleton ctx per the monotonic-resources rule), and mic getSettings telemetry (stt-mic crumb + one-per-session narrowband toast in Car Mode). 5-clause STT UPGRADES CONTRACT sabotage-proven 5/5. Honest limit: the mic itself can't be harness-driven — the next real car session is the field test; watch the stt-mic crumbs' sampleRate. Local-WASM/self-host verdicts stand as before. |
| 110 | **Mana pool — replace one-cast-per-spell with a spend-by-tier casting economy.** TLDR: today every known spell is castable exactly once per rest (`used:true`), so a L15 Cleric can heal the party once a day; a mana pool makes casting a resource decision — spam Healing Word or spend big on Spirit Guardians. **Design ruled with the user 2026-07-31 (grew out of the Cleric review):** ① **base mana = the SUM of the known bench's tiers** — self-balancing, scales automatically with picks and tier unlocks, no separate curve table; a player who wants the old behavior can still cast each spell once, but mixing is now legal. ② **Cantrips cost 0** — tier 0 stays at-will, exactly what `cost:"at-will"` already means in the capability bible. ③ **Stat modifier keyed to each class's PRIME CASTING STAT** (a data field per class, not per tradition; user accepted — correction 2026-07-31: this game's Sorcerer prime is INT, not CHA as argued in-session, but prime-stat keying stays strictly more robust than tradition keying either way): **+10% pool per point over 16**. ④ Casting cost = the spell's **capability-bible tier** — canonical, already injected to the GM. ⑤ Opens design room for **mana potions** (user callout). **Second-ring rulings (user, one-at-a-time pass 2026-07-31):** ⑥ **refill = full on Rest only**, nothing between — Sorcerer L7 Sorcerous Restoration is the ONE exception (draft: +25% pool on a short breather; | 106 | **In-game time runs far too slowly, and the clock silently loses most of what the narration describes.** TLDR: 216 turns of Day 1 play advanced the campaign clock 1043 minutes while the narration actually described ~2332 — the clock is running at under half the story's pace, and a day takes ~200 turns to pass. **Measured 2026-07-30** from the live save (`Ammut_t1265.tnd`, Rise of the Runelords) — full line-item evidence in [timeline_day1.html](timeline_day1.html) (216 rows, per-turn duration reconstructed from the narration under the GM's own reference scale, then compared against the real stored clock). ⚠ Day 1 of this campaign is **turns 1050–1265, not the campaign's first day** — the clock shipped at v1.389 (≈t917) and `migrateWorldState` starts an old save's clock at Day 0 on load ([state.js:254](state.js:254)), so the counter began mid-campaign; the t1049 `[REST:long]` rolled it to the 1440 dawn boundary that opens Day 1. **TWO INDEPENDENT CAUSES, both confirmed:** ① **the reference scale prices actions, not scenes** — [tag_table.js:68](tag_table.js:68) gives the GM "conversation 1-5 min", and conversation is **100 of 216 turns** (46% of play), so ~4 min/turn means a 16-hour waking day needs ~200 turns to fill. The scale times the words spoken; the walk over, the waiting, and the aftermath are free. ② **a turn with no `[TIME_ADVANCE:]` advances ZERO minutes, silently** — the handler opens `if(!ts.length)return;` with no warn and no floor, and `clockAdvance` has exactly one caller, so nothing backstops it. The visible symptom in the live save: the clock reads Day 1 17h23m (≈11pm) while `world.time` says "early morning" and the t1263 prose has "Sandpoint waking up around you both" — **the story slept and the clock did not**; the 5 turns classed as narrative time-skips alone carry 555 narrated minutes the clock had no way to receive. **② is the bigger and cheaper lever, and it is a no-silent-failures violation.** Candidate remedies (none ratified): a loud warn (and/or a minimum-1-minute floor) when a story turn advances nothing; a scene-level framing in the reference text ("charge the whole scene, including approach and aftermath"); rescaled category values; an explicit narrative time-skip tag for between-scene gaps. **⚠ Drift surface — the reference scale lives in the STATE TAGS doc block, byte-frozen by the suite (strip/doc hashes), and the tag vocabulary + prompt docs are named surface. Time of day is the projection already fixed by #89 and written into clock.js's header — `(clock%1440)+6h`, dawn=6am — computed every render, never stored, so it cannot desync from the counter; past midnight the calendar hour wraps while the adventuring Day does not (dawn-to-dawn). Transcript entries gained absolute **`.ck`** so a rebuilt past turn shows the time it actually happened (distinct from `.ta` on purpose: `.ta` is what a turn CHARGED and is not derivable across gaps/imports, `.ck` is WHEN it was); entries older than the field render the bare turn number rather than a guess — verified against that failure condition, not just the happy path.
