Pure relabel — no migration, and `.ck` stamps written under the old label still render correctly. 4 sabotage clauses proven, including both half-renumbered states (player-only and GM-only). ⚠ Note when reading older material: [timeline_day1.html](timeline_day1.html) and the turn range cited above were written under the OLD labels, so that stretch (t1050–t1265) now displays in-game as **Day 2**; the report carries a banner saying so. ⚠ **Interaction worth watching:** this makes the pacing defect VISIBLE to the player — the stored clock reads ~11pm while the prose says "early morning", and that contradiction is now on screen every turn. Good for diagnosis, but it raises the priority of choosing a remedy. **Next session can measure this directly from any save instead of reconstructing it** — re-run the timeline off `.ta` once a few dozen turns have accumulated, then pick the remedy against real numbers. ⛨ **Fable review passed v1.501 (queue entry 8, 2026-07-30):** cache contract runtime-proven (stable half byte-identical across 17 mutation classes incl. clock+schedule moves), memo reasoning proven at runtime, all four v1.498 sabotage clauses independently re-derived and re-proven. Suite hardens shipped: the cache-invariant test now mutates the clock (it never had), the clock-face test catches AM faces (was pm-gated), a real-`commitGmTurn` `.ta` wiring test (the old test's title claimed the turn path but its body called `applyMuts` — the #107 wiring-hole class), and a warm-memo round-trip test. **⚠ MEASUREMENT STILL BLOCKED: zero post-v1.496 `.ta` data exists on any disk export** — play a few dozen turns on ≥v1.496 and export the save, then re-run the timeline off `.ta`. Accepted residue: a tags-only GM response (prose empty after stripping) writes no transcript entry, so a clock move can go unreceipted — pathological input; synthesizing an entry would violate transcript sanctity. ✅ **CLOSED 2026-07-31 (user field verdict: "working well" — closed together with #73).** The scene-pricing rewrite (v1.499) is field-approved by play feel; the formal .ta re-measurement was superseded by that verdict (the instrumentation stays in every save if anyone ever wants the numbers). **Cause ② (a tagless turn advances zero, silently) closes as NOT NEEDED at observed behavior** — the every-turn instruction + scene pricing are holding in the field; if pacing ever stalls again, reopen with the warn/floor remedy and measure off .ta first. |
| 73 | **Campaign clock — the engine has no concept of a DAY, so any in-fiction deadline is pure hallucination.** TLDR: the Runelords campaign has a solstice countdown the story treats as load-bearing, and the GM invents a different number every time it is asked, because nothing anywhere stores one. Evidence (2026-07-19, live Table Talk): asked "how many days to the solstice", the GM answered **11**, then **8**, then **94** — three confident answers, no stored fact behind any of them — and it emitted `[CALENDAR:11 days to winter solstice]` and `[DAYS_TO_SOLSTICE:94]`, **invented tags for a system that does not exist** (grep for solstice/CALENDAR/DAYS_TO across tag_table/state/api/globals returns zero hits). So the GM believes it is recording the countdown; nothing is listening. The only clock today is free-text `[TIME:]` ("dusk", "midday") with no advancement — CLAUDE.md §7 already notes this. **Design questions for the fresh session:** what advances a day (GM tag? travel? rest? an explicit `[DAY_PASSES:n]`?); where it lives (`worldState.world` beside time/weather is the obvious home); whether countdowns are FIRST-CLASS (a named deadline with a target day, so "days remaining" is COMPUTED not remembered) or just a day counter the GM does arithmetic against — computed is far safer given the evidence; how travel interacts (the trigger case was a 3-day ride, a day on site, a 3-day ride back); what the GM is shown each turn (one "Day 94 · 11 days to winter solstice" line in the volatile half would do it); and whether existing saves get a starting day or opt in. ⛨ Drift surface — new tag + buildSysPrompt injection + migration; the anti-hallucination win IS the point, so the injected line must be authoritative and the GM must never be asked to remember a number it can compute. | M | Fable | ✅ **CORE SHIPPED v1.389 (2026-07-20).** Design converged with the user (both designed independently, then compared — full spec: [DOC/Research/DOC_clock.html](DOC/Research/DOC_clock.html), decision log C1–C6). **Built (drift surface → Fable pre-review logged in todo_checkWithFable.md #3):** new `clock.js` (`worldState.clock={min,schedule}` — ONE scalar, minutes since epoch, day/hr/min DERIVED not stored; GM does ZERO arithmetic, only emits duration estimates); tags `[TIME_ADVANCE:N]` (unit-suffixed, monotonic, clamp ≥1, sums per response), `[SCHEDULE:label\|when]` (stores ABSOLUTE dueMin, countdown COMPUTED every turn — the anti-hallucination heart), `[SCHEDULE_RESOLVED:]`, `[SCHEDULE_CANCEL:]`; `buildClockBlock()` ONE shared builder injected volatile-half (api.js) AND called by Table Talk `ttStateBlock` (the #76↔#73 coupling — solstice now answerable in TT from data). **Jump-safety (C3, load-bearing):** firing is THRESHOLD (`now≥due`), never exact-minute — a 1h deadline slept past by a 6h rest fires on waking (live-verified: "Watch change" +1h, rest 6h, fired narrated "5 hours ago"). **C2:** firing surfaces DUE events to the GM (HAPPENING NOW); GM narrates + emits the consequent tag — engine tracks time, GM stays sole mutator. **DEFERRED to fast-follow** (the display/date-projection layer, both touch the Sync modal): **C4** clock-authoritative time-of-day + retiring `[TIME:]` (v1 keeps `[TIME:]`/`world.time` as narrative flavor; clock is elapsed-time, no conflict since scheduling runs on `clock.min`), and **C5** the named in-world calendar (user: "it's gold" — months + epoch date + fixed-date deadlines like "21st of Deepfrost"; keep fixed month lengths, no leap logic). **Verified:** 745 engine assertions (+14 clock tests incl. the jump-safety failure-case, all-crossed-in-one-jump, STABLE-HALF PURITY cache-killer guard, migration); frozen strip/doc hashes re-baselined; live browser end-to-end, zero console errors. #76's no-date-branch assertion still holds — TT reads the shared builder, so the coupling delivered as promised. ✅ **CLOSED 2026-07-31 (user field verdict: "the campaign clock is now working well").** The core (v1.389) plus the #106 arc (.ta receipt v1.496, player-facing clock v1.497, 1-based days v1.498, scene pricing v1.499) is the shipped, field-approved system. The deferred fast-follows C4 (clock-authoritative time-of-day / retire free-text [TIME:]) and C5 (named in-world calendar) close WITH the row: C4 was substantially overtaken by the #106b player-facing time-of-day projection, and C5 is flavor a future need can re-file fresh. |
| 92 | **Compress the transcript inside the cloud-sync payload — the 2 MB toast fired in the field.** TLDR: the sync POST ships `worldState` with the transcript as a PLAIN array, so a mature campaign uploads its whole history every synced turn — the sentinel toast ("⚠ Cloud sync upload is 2.0 MB — mature campaign; mention it to the dev", storage-adapter.js `_syncSizeWarned`) fired for real on the Runelords save at t1130 (2026-07-24). No data risk (server body cap is 8 MB, so 25% used), but the payload grows forever and the per-turn upload cost on cellular is the real bill. **Fix = "optimize the copy, never the source" (sanctioned by the transcript decree):** send the `serializeWorldState()` compressed form (`transcript` → `{__lz:…}`) in `syncToServer` — the server stores TEXT + `json_extract('$.turn')` which the compressed form doesn't touch. ⚠ **CORRECTED by the 2026-07-24 evidence pass (brief E): the server-ADOPT path consumes the pulled blob RAW (`worldState = data.worldState`, storage-adapter) — it never goes through `parseWorldState`, so the "read side is already tolerant" claim is FALSE for exactly the path that matters.** The pull hops (reconcile adopt + campaign-picker fetch) must inflate the compressed form too, or shipping this poisons live state. Also bundle: the transcript-memo enforcement decision (entry-4 ★ verdict — generalized stamp accessor vs. revision counter; the `rc`-adjacency and hardcoded-array fragilities are recorded there). Verify the WHOLE round trip: pull path (`load`/`fillPortraitsFromBlob`), campaign-list derivation (server reads `world_state` for char/level/location — those fields stay plain), `.tnd` export/import unaffected, CAS guard, and a device on an OLD version pulling a compressed blob (parseWorldState tolerance predates this, but PROVE it with the real failure case). Expected: ~2.0 MB → well under 1 MB; the 8 MB horizon moves out by thousands of turns. ⚠ Save-data transport — a compressor bug here IS data loss; engine-test the round trip against a real mature blob. | S-M | Fable | ✅ **SHIPPED v1.504** (2026-07-30, Fable). **The wire format IS the disk format:** `compressWorldStateSnapshot()` (state.js) is the ONE producer — extracted from `serializeWorldState` (now a pure veneer, byte-identical output, SHARED memo so a saveCore-then-sync turn pays one LZ pass) — and both POST paths (`_syncNow` payload + `pushCampaignState`) route through it. **The adopt-hop poison the row flagged is closed:** `inflateWorldStateSnapshot()` (the object-form twin of `parseWorldState`, which is now a veneer over it) runs FIRST in the reconcile before any consumer touches `data.worldState`; the campaign-pull hops needed no change (their slot writes pass {__lz} through and `parseWorldState` inflates on read — verified by test). **Measured on the real 2,527-entry Ammut blob: worldState wire 1.93 MB → 1.09 MB (transcript alone 1.72 → 0.87 MB), full deep-equal round trip.** The UTF-16 form costs ~3 bytes/char in UTF-8 — a base64 wire would shave ~17% more but introduces a SECOND format old clients cannot read, so one-format-everywhere won (mixed-version safety beats the extra bytes). **Old-client proof (the row demanded it):** a pre-#92 device adopting the compressed blob raw suffers only session-transient breakage — its own `serializeWorldState` passthrough stores {__lz} intact and its `parseWorldState` (v1.227+) inflates on next load, ZERO permanent loss — engine-tested as the OLD-CLIENT SAFETY test. 6 tests + a 3-clause SYNC COMPRESSION CONTRACT in run-tests.js (the async reconcile is source-pinned); 6/6 sabotage clauses proven on a scratch copy. The transcript-memo enforcement bundle (entry-4 ★) deliberately NOT folded in — no new fragility was introduced (the helper shares the existing memo), so that decision stays its own item. |

| 105 | **Locations are frozen in their first-visit state — a place the party materially changed is still served to the GM as intact.** TLDR: the party destroyed a large part of the sea cave, and ~700 turns later a new quest re-offered it as though nothing had happened, because nothing in the engine ever tells the GM what a location looks like *now*. **Promoted from [DOC/BUGS.md](DOC/BUGS.md) B17 (field report 2026-07-23, v1.427, Rise of the Runelords t1059) — already root-caused there; the full seven-leg investigation stays in the bug row, this row owns the build.** **The mechanism:** `[LOCATION_DESC:]` is write-once by design (anti-drift, CLAUDE.md §9) and `buildGeoBlock` describes the CURRENT node only — so there is NO channel at all that serves a remote location's history to the GM. The field built for exactly this is dead code: `fileLocation(loc,note,turn)` writes `memory.locations[].notes[]`, but its only caller passes a hardcoded empty string (tag_table.js:211) and nothing reads it for injection. Map nodes have no changed/damaged field; `[LOCATION_ITEM:]` covers named items only. The recency windows had scrolled past (STORY SO FAR = last 8 chapters, RECENT DECISIONS = last 5), and RAG's location bonus (`if(q.loc&&en.e.l===q.loc)sc+=2`, memory.js:543) only fires when the party is physically AT the location. **The structural trap:** the prompt is frozen BEFORE generation, but the GM decides to reference the cave INSIDE its own output — so every mention-triggered mechanism (the `memoryNpcDetail` last-6-messages pattern) keys off already-said text and cannot help. A fix must be ALWAYS-PRESENT (compact roll-up) or next-turn-reactive, never predictive. **Fix sketch (direction only, not ratified):** a durable per-node state-change log distinct from the immutable description — `stateNotes[]` on the map node (capped 3-5, lore/futureEvents cap discipline), written by a new dedicated tag (`[LOCATION_STATE:note]`, sibling of `[LOCATION_ITEM:]`), surfaced as a compact always-present one-line-per-changed-location roll-up near memoryTOC's VISITED lines. **⚠ Drift surface — Fable-tier by decree, design review BEFORE any code.** The write-once rule exists for a measured reason and must not be reopened. Silent-failure classes a careless fix invites: unbounded notes → volatile-half bloat; a mutate-instead-of-append tag reopens description drift; wiring through RAG's location bonus inherits the exact gate that caused the bug; any leak into the STABLE half kills prompt caching campaign-wide. | M | Fable | ✅ **SHIPPED v1.503** (2026-07-30, Fable). **Design fork decided with the user: append-only state notes + roll-up** (GM-rewrite of descriptions rejected — reopens the write-once rule). As built: `[LOCATION_STATE:what changed]` (one TAG_TABLE entry — parse+strip+doc land together, ordered AFTER LOCATION so a move+note in one response lands on the NEW node) → `fileLocationState` (memory.js) appends `{n,t}` to the map node's `stateNotes[]` — node created if never filed (a silently-vanished note would be the feature failing its own defect), near-duplicate REFRESH with richer-text-wins (the #29 lesson), cap `LOC_STATE_CAP`=3 with loud oldest-eviction. TWO read surfaces: `buildGeoBlock` serves the current node's (and sublocation's) changes beside the frozen first-visit description with an explicit this-OVERRIDES instruction, and `buildChangedLocationsBlock` (api.js) is the ALWAYS-PRESENT remote roll-up — most-recent-first, cap `CHANGED_LOC_MAX`=10 with a visible "+N more" line, volatile half only, ""-clean when nothing changed — which defeats the structural trap (the GM references a place INSIDE its own output, so mention-triggered injection can never help). Write-once `[LOCATION_DESC:]` untouched. 9 engine tests red-first (923→932); frozen strip (+15) and doc (+478) hashes re-baselined deliberately with a clause guard on the doc line; **7/7 sabotage clauses proven on a scratch copy** (handler-delete/phantom, dedupe, cap, current-node exclusion, stable-leak, materiality gate, strip entry). ⚠ Forward-looking only: the sea cave's destruction predates the tag — nudge the GM once in Story ("record the current state of the sea cave") or the record starts at the next material change. |
| 94 | **Test Piper's "high" voices — the quality upgrade we may already own.** TLDR: Kokoro (#91) died on cost, but the engine already shipping has a better-sounding tier we've never evaluated. `PIPER_VOICES` already lists four **high**-quality models — `en_US-libritts-high` (904 speakers, **already downloaded on the server volume**), `en_US-lessac-high`, `en_US-ryan-high`, `en_GB-cori-high` — against the **medium** models in use today. Same engine, same warm-daemon machinery, same endpoint, same client: switching is a *voice pick*, not an architecture change. **The only open question is CPU cost**, and the headroom is enormous: Piper medium measures RTF 0.16 on the live box, so a high model could be **6× slower and still clear the ≥1× realtime bar** (where Kokoro needed to get 2× *faster* and failed at 2.1). **The work:** point `tts/bench-kokoro.mjs`'s corpus at the piper daemon instead (or a small sibling script — the 30-unit corpus and the RTF/percentile reporting are reusable as-is), measure medium vs high on identical text, then **listen** — the ear check is the actual deliverable, since the whole point is "does it sound meaningfully better." ⚠ Run it when the box is otherwise idle: the #91 run proved sustained CPU exhausts the `shared-cpu-1x` burst balance, and a benchmark competing with live narration corrupts both. If high lands under ~0.6 RTF and sounds better, it ships as a narrator/character voice preference with **zero code change** (and #91 closes as "quality upgrade delivered by a different route"); if it's slower than that, the per-character casting question becomes "which voices are worth the CPU." ✅ **FIELD DATA ALREADY ANSWERS THE SPEED HALF (2026-07-24, no benchmark needed):** the live session was found to be *already synthesizing with* `en_US-libritts-high` (a warm daemon at rate 1.05), so the server's own logs measure it directly — **high ≈ RTF 0.98–1.04 vs medium ≈ 0.16–0.21**, i.e. **~6× the CPU cost, landing at break-even** (1s of CPU per 1s of speech). It WORKS on the shipped box — but with essentially zero margin, where medium has 6×. ⚠ Read those log lines correctly: piper reports **cumulative** infer/audio per daemon, so each line is a running average for that voice's whole session, not one sentence (the monotonically rising "20s audio" figures are totals — no single unit is anywhere near the client's 10s per-unit timeout). **What remains is therefore a JUDGMENT call, not a measurement:** ① does high sound meaningfully better to the user's ear (the actual deliverable); ② is break-even margin acceptable as a *default* — the exposure is concurrent narration (multiplayer/second device) or a long unbroken read pushing past 1.0, where the client's lookahead drains and units start missing the timeout; ③ per-character casting economics — at 6× cost, which characters are worth a high voice. Safest shape if the ear says yes: **high for the narrator (or for one or two signature characters), medium elsewhere** — the per-character voice system already supports exactly that mix, with no code change. ⚠ Also note the #91 burst lesson applies at the margin: sustained RTF 1.0 pegs the core for the whole read, which is the load pattern that exhausts the shared-CPU balance; between-turn idle is what refills it. | S | Any | Filed 2026-07-24 from the #91 benchmark's most useful side finding; **speed half answered same day by field logs** — remaining work is an ear check + a default-voice decision ✅ **Closed 2026-07-30 — evaluated and confirmed good (user: checked and rechecked).** The ear check happened and the outcome stands; the shipped voice set and default are keepers. → **Assigned to Sol (Codex) 2026-08-03** — brief in [DOC/todos_completed/SOL_TASKS.md](DOC/todos_completed/SOL_TASKS.md). |

Authored narrator voice now rides the blueprint end to end: `narratorVoice` on the schema (`normalizeBlueprint` coerces non-strings to ""), exported by `buildBlueprintFromGame` from the campaign pin, applied by `applyBlueprint` under the SAME E20 rule as `proseAuthor` (only a non-empty authored voice pins, so a narration-indifferent blueprint can never wipe the player's own narrator), with an unknown-id toast via `TTS.voiceKnown`. TLDR: after each turn, a cheap LLM call says who speaks which sentence, so dialogue narrates in each character's own voice. **Measured facts that shaped it:** `speakResponse` fires at game.js:832 BEFORE `generateActions` at :833, so narration waits for nothing today — "run it concurrently with the suggestion call" would ADD a wait, not hide one; time from `speak()` to first audible sample is **5,920ms** warm (voice cached, WASM compiled); `_speakPiper` already calls `mod.predict` PER UNIT (tts.js:1067) under `PIPER_MAX_AHEAD_SEC`=25 backpressure, so per-unit voice is a one-line change, not a rearchitecture. **Ratified:** ① **Blocking with a fuse** (user call, against the no-block recommendation): narration waits for the map, hard ceiling ~4s, then speaks regardless — an unbounded wait would turn one hung request into a silent turn. ② **Contract = unit INDICES, not text spans** (supersedes the original `{speaker,text}` shape): the model gets the numbered `splitSentences(clean,null,true)` units — the exact units the synth loop will use — and returns `{index: speakerName}`; index binding removes the substring-matching failure mode, where a paraphrased span silently mis-voices. ③ **Only characters with an explicitly assigned `voiceId`**; the prompt carries just those names, and the call is SKIPPED entirely when none exist or the response has no quote marks — non-users pay nothing. ④ **Tiny standalone prompt** on the active provider (no world state, no cache reuse — mechanical attribution, not creative work); new `kind:"speakers"` bucket in `recordUsage` so the cost is attributable in the Usage modal. ⑤ **PERSISTED PER TURN ON THE TRANSCRIPT ENTRY** as `sp:{n,s}` — additive field stamped by `logTranscript`, exactly the shape of RAG's existing `e:{n,l,q}` ("additive fields — no schema bump", §8b), so it does NOT engage the transcript-is-sacred decree (that bars trimming/capping/restructuring, i.e. loss). This makes the ALREADY-SHIPPED per-message 🔊 replay button (ui-shell.js:191, attached on transcript rebuild at ui-boot.js:322) re-speak ANY past turn as first heard, on any synced device — and it collapses the `worldState.lastNarration` copy: one home, no desync to engineer around. Footprint accepted by the user without measurement: only dialogue-bearing entries grow, ~50–70 raw chars on a ~1,200-char entry (~5%, less post-LZ since names repeat). ⑥ **Staleness guard (the trap):** indices bind to splitter output, so a future `MAX_UNIT`/pause-tier change would silently re-index every stored map = confidently WRONG voices on old turns. `n` = unit count at write time; if `splitSentences(text).length!==n` at replay, drop the whole map and narrate flat. ⑦ Story turns only — Table Talk is the single-voice help desk. Resolution via `resolveNpcName` → `TTS.characterVoiceId`. Output post-process, no applyMuts/memory/prompt contact → Sonnet tier. <br><br>**AS BUILT — 9 tests, all written failing-first, 764 green.** `speakerCastList`/`speakerPassNeeded`/ `buildSpeakerPrompt`/`parseSpeakerMap`/`speakerVoiceMap`/`assignSpeakers`/`narrateWithSpeakers` in game.js (beside generateActions, its sibling post-turn hook); `stampTranscriptSpeakers` in state.js; per-unit voice + a `_voiceReady` memo in `_speakPiper`; `speak(text,voiceId,voices)` / `speakResponse(clean,voices)` / `requestSpeak(text,voices)`; `sp` threaded through addMsg and the transcript rebuild; `speakers` added to the Usage-modal ordering. **THE TRAP FOUND WHILE BUILDING — the compression memo:** `serializeWorldState` memoizes the LZ-compressed transcript on (length, last-entry ref, last-entry `.x`), and the post-pass stamps `sp` onto an ALREADY-WRITTEN entry 1-4s later, changing none of those keys — so without an explicit `invalidateTranscriptMemo` every speaker map would be silently dropped at the localStorage boundary (reload → flat narration, no error anywhere, feature looks broken). `stampTranscriptSpeakers` owns the invalidation and its test primes the memo BEFORE stamping so the guard can actually fail. **One judgement call worth knowing:** the pass is skipped while audio is muted (`isOn()` false and not Car Mode) — a muted player is a non-user and shouldn't pay per turn — with the consequence that turns narrated while muted keep no map and replay flat. **Live-verified end to end:** usage kind `speakers` + noHistory, cast in the prompt, out-of-range index AND unknown name both dropped, map stamped and surviving a serialize/parse round trip, correct voiceIds handed to TTS; the fuse (hung request → narration starts at 4,575ms with a null map, warned, entry left unstamped); the rebuilt-old-turn 🔊 button resolving names LIVE (alba → rebind → ryan → unbind → narrator); and a real two-voice read that downloaded the second voice mid-passage with zero synth errors. Annotated for Fable in todo_checkWithFable.md #4 (user request — TTS is not drift surface but wanted double-checked). <br><br>**Build order:** voice-picker first (unblocks the catalog) → Piper-only cleanup + assignment UI + `charSheet.voiceId` → the (C) post-pass last. <br><br>**Retain:** GM narrator voice; Piper offline (#41); native-voice resolution (picker UI goes, resolution stays for the fallback). <br><br>**⑦ MULTI-SPEAKER GOLDMINE (found 2026-07-20, DEFERRED by user):** the 30 English models actually contain **~2,000 distinct speaker voices** — 7 are multi-speaker: libritts=904, libritts_r=904, vctk=109, l2arctic=24, arctic=18, aru=12, semaine=4. **One `libritts_r` download (75MB) = 904 voices**, plausibly the whole cast (narrator + party + every NPC) from a single file — a strong candidate backbone for the feature. `voice_picker.html` (shipped 49db4bb) currently shows one row per MODEL, sampling speaker 0. **User call: keep to the 30 models for now**; a **speaker-browser** (page/sample/star individual speakers within a multi-speaker model — piper-samples has a rendered clip per speaker_N) is a deferred enhancement worth revisiting, since it's where 900+ of the ~2000 voices live and it changes the download math (1 file vs 25). <br><br>**⑧ CUSTOM SAMPLE TEXT — wish (user 2026-07-20):** the pre-rendered piper-samples all speak ONE fixed sentence (a weather/meteorology forecast), so every voice says the same thing — fine for a quick timbre check, useless for judging character/emotion, and especially limiting for **libritts** (auditioning 904 speakers on one repeated sentence is hopeless). Want: type your OWN text and hear a voice say it. **Tradeoff:** that needs LIVE Piper synthesis = the model DOWNLOADED (60–130MB) + the WASM runtime, vs the free pre-rendered clips — so it does NOT fit the "browse all 30 without downloading" picker, but it fits perfectly for (a) an ALREADY-downloaded voice, esp. the default **libritts_r** you'll have anyway, and (b) the in-game Voice Settings **▶ Test** button (make it take a custom line instead of fixed text). Natural pairing with the deferred speaker-browser (⑦): once libritts_r is downloaded, a custom-text + speaker-index UI is the real way to mine its 904 voices. <br><br>**ROW CLOSED 2026-07-27 (audit) — everything here is shipped or superseded:** the ④/(C) LLM speaker post-pass (v1.406, the AS-BUILT section above) was DELETED at v1.447, replaced by #96's authoring-time `[SAY:]` attribution (deterministic, no second call, no fuse); ⑦ the multi-speaker goldmine and ⑧ custom sample text shipped as the #95 arc (`speaker_browser.html` — 904 LibriTTS + 109 VCTK speakers, audition with your own text, ★ bench with cloud sync); ⑨ voice gender shipped as #95.7/#95.8 (structured mutable `g` on stars + gender-matched auto-cast with #96b pinning + per-voice overrides). Everything marked "retain" (narrator voice, `charSheet.voiceId`, Piper-only settings, native fallback) is live. Observed: *Message* was line-of-sight when first used, now its distance is limitless.** With no canonical entry, the GM re-improvises each spell's rules from its name every time, and compressed memory carries no correction. The bible must be **GM-authoritative**: inject the canonical entry into the prompt on cast/use (same re-inject-from-data pattern as the quest block, char sheet, and `[LOCATION_DESC:]`). **#8 merged in:** two consumers, one data source — ① GM prompt injection on cast/use (anti-drift), ② player-facing click-card/tooltip on spell & inventory names (old #8). <br>**Design converged 2026-07-07 (naming + schema):** <br>• **Nomenclature (locked): `<domain>_bible`, singular** — `spell_bible`, `item_bible`, `creature_bible`, `profession_bible`. Viewer page = `bible_study` (satellite HTML, not index.html; read-only; its render IS the in-game player card — one render, two hosts). Prefix names the domain so "where are the spells?" never happens; a viewer/injector iterates all `*_bible` registries uniformly. **`library` was rejected — collides with the existing Character Library** (`listCharLibrary`/`showCharLibrary`). *(Clarity above Brevity: the existing bare `library`/`bestiary` names are the anti-pattern we're correcting here.)* <br>• **Format: structured JS constant in its own file(s)** (`bible.js` or per-domain), loaded via `<script src>` (file://-safe, no build step) — NOT markdown (the engine reads it per-field; prose would defeat the tight-canon injection and force a runtime MD parser). Keyed by **base name** (parenthetical stripped) so existing `char.spells[].nm` strings and the `[SPELL_USED:]` base-name matcher keep working — pure lookup overlay, no refactor of how spells are stored. <br>• **Entry = one unified "capability" shape** (spells and abilities have **no intrinsic difference** — validated 2026-07-07). Cosmetic `kind` tag for modal grouping only. Two REAL structural fields: **`cost`/recharge** (`at-will` / `N-day` / `slot:tier` / `1-rest` / `passive` — unifies cantrips, abilities, racial 1/day, leveled spells; also the P10/R1 recast guard) and **`isMagical`** (bool — the one genuine spell-vs-ability divider: dispel/counterspell/antimagic/detect-magic susceptibility; **authoring rule: `true` if it would light up under Detect Magic / be targetable by Dispel Magic**). Drift-pinning fields: `range`, `targets`, `duration`, `effect`, optional `dice`/`save`/`concentration`/`tags`. GM injection uses a tight line built from these; the card renders them. <br>• **Static base + emergent overlay:** authored canon in `*_bible.js`; GM-invented spells/items get a **write-once `[SPELL_DEF:]`/`[ITEM_DEF:]` tag** (LOCATION_DESC pattern) filing a per-campaign overlay in worldState/memory, merged over the base at lookup. <br>• **No editor for v1** — hand-authored JS like `SPELLS`/`DEFAULT_RULES`; `bible_study` gives readability, editability stays hand-edit; a real editor waits until blueprint-bundled bibles create demand. <br>• **Bestiary reconciliation:** `creature_bible` must ABSORB the existing `worldState.bestiary` (blueprint `creatures` → BESTIARY block), not become a second monster home. <br>**MVP slice:** `spell_bible` only, base-name-keyed, injection on `[SPELL_USED:]` + player card (fixes the drift immediately) → then fold in abilities → `[*_DEF:]` write-once tag → other domains. | L | — | **IN PROGRESS.** ✅ v1.218 — `spell_bible.js` shipped (18-entry starter + `spellBibleLookup` w/ emergent-overlay hook; loaded, SW-precached, 8 tests) and the **anti-drift injection is LIVE**: `buildSpellBibleBlock()` re-injects canon for the player's known spells every turn (VOLATILE half, cache-safe; verified live — Message pinned to 120ft, Hunter's Mark exclusivity). Chose **preventive-every-turn (option A)** over reactive-on-`[SPELL_USED:]`. **✅ v1.219** — `[SPELL_DEF:]` write-once tag: GM canonizes an invented spell into the `worldState.spellBible` overlay (spellBibleLookup already prefers it), stripped by cleanTxt, documented. **✅ v1.220** — `ability_bible.js` (24 abilities, `kind:"ability"`; **separate file, later merged — see v1.222); `capabilityLookup()` resolves any capability (a Ranger's Hunter's Mark ABILITY resolves to its SPELL canon — no dup); `buildAbilityBibleBlock()` injects ability canon every turn. **✅ v1.222** — open question RESOLVED (user chose B): merged `spell_bible.js`+`ability_bible.js` → **one `capability_bible.js`** with `CAPABILITY_BIBLE` (kind-tagged rows), honoring "spells and abilities have no intrinsic difference — `kind` is cosmetic." `SPELL_BIBLE`/`ABILITY_BIBLE`→`CAPABILITY_BIBLE`, `spellBaseName`→`capBaseName`, overlay `worldState.spellBible`→`worldState.capabilityBible`, single `capabilityLookup`. Genuinely-different domains (item/creature/profession) stay separate `*_bible` files. **✅ v1.223** — `category` field: every entry tagged with a LIST of traditions (`arcane`/`divine`/`primal`/`necromantic`/`martial`; Turn Undead = `["divine","necromantic"]`), rendered as chips on the card; `capabilitiesByCategory(cat)` returns the matching menu (base + overlay) — the gate for limiting a rolled enemy caster (an enemy cleric → the "divine" list). `[SPELL_DEF:]` accepts `category=a,b`. **✅ v1.225** — FULL spell coverage: all 91 game spells (SPELLS + ARCH_SPELLS) authored into `CAPABILITY_BIBLE` (was an 18-spell starter; Necromancer went 0/23 → 23/23). 90 kind:spell + the `blink` name-collision (Sorcerer ability teleport vs Arcane Trickster ethereal spell — base-name keying holds one; ability wins, flagged). Coverage guard test added. **Remaining consumer:** wire `capabilitiesByCategory` into a GM prompt block / enemy-statblock so a rolled caster actually draws from its tradition (school axis deferred — tradition only, user call). **✅ v1.221** — player **click-card** (`showCapabilityCard`, clickable spell/ability names in the char sheet) + **`bible_study.html`** satellite viewer, both rendering via the shared pure `bibleCardHTML` (helpers.js) — one render, two hosts; verified live (Hunter's Mark card + 42-card viewer, magical/mundane badges, filter). **✅ v1.226** — **racial abilities/spells single-sourced into the bible.** `ANCS` entries now carry `racial_caps:[]` (bible-key references; bare string = passive/at-will, `{cap,use}` adds a recharge) instead of free-text defs + `racial_spells`; `confirmChar` gathers ancestry+subrace+lineage caps, dedupes/supersedes (Superior Darkvision > Darkvision), and resolves each via `capabilityLookup` → spells to `char.spells`, abilities to `char.abilities` (ds from bible) — so `buildSpell/AbilityBibleBlock` auto-inject their canon (fixes "change Faerie Fire in two places"; central lookup for Thaumaturgy). ~26 new racial entries authored (Darkvision/Superior Darkvision, Fey Ancestry, Gnomish Cunning, resistances, Draconic Breath, Camouflage, Relentless Endurance, Hellish Rebuke, Dancing Lights, …) + new `racial` category (drawn by no caster tradition). Coverage-guard test (every `racial_caps` key has a bible entry); `racial_spells` field removed; 3 tests added (237 total). **Remaining:** companion spell canon (their spells live on `charSheet.spells`) · full-coverage spell/ability authoring (starter sets only; first live-save authoring pass done 2026-07-11 via #48 ① — 29 entries from the t514 party sheets, bible at 169) · `item_bible` + `[ITEM_DEF:]` · `creature_bible` (absorb `worldState.bestiary`) · `profession_bible` · eventual editor. **Live GM-compliance is the open money test** — **✅ PASSED 2026-07-09** ([AUDIT_playtest_v1238.md](audits/AUDIT_playtest_v1238.md)): 40-turn fresh-catalog Arcane Trickster run — Message held 120ft under baited pressure twice, Mage Hand held 30ft, 8 unprompted canon holds; the t308/t160 drift classes are dead. Soft spot found: slot-expenditure awareness (F1 — the sheet omits used spells instead of naming them, api.js:141; ⛨ fix proposed). Hunter's Mark exclusivity (Ranger) is the one untested bound. |
| 11 | **Prompt caching (Anthropic `cache_control`)** — cache the stable prefix of the system prompt so it re-reads at ~0.1× input cost instead of full price every turn. **Do as its own focused task** (touches load-bearing prompt construction). **Why it's not a one-liner:** `buildSysPrompt` is rebuilt and *changes every turn* (turn counter, HP, gold, location, memory TOC, geography, quests), and currently **interleaves** stable content (rules, STATE TAGS block, STYLE, prose voice) with volatile content (char sheet, world, memory) — so a naive whole-prompt cache gets ~0 hits and pays the 1.25× write penalty for nothing. **Plan:** (1) reorder `buildSysPrompt` so ALL static content comes first (role decl, `getRulesBlock()`, tone, full STATE TAGS + companion-tag instructions, STYLE, prose voice — ~2–4k tokens), (2) drop one `cache_control:{type:"ephemeral"}` breakpoint at the end of that static block, (3) put ALL per-turn volatile state after it (char sheet, world, NPCs, memory, geography, quests, combat), (4) change `PROVIDERS.anthropic.buildBody` to send `system` as a two-block array (stable+cache_control, then volatile) instead of one string. **Thresholds:** min cacheable prefix 2048 tok (Sonnet 4.6) / **4096 (Opus 4.8)** — the static block must clear it or it silently won't cache. **Verify with a node render-diff** that the stable prefix is byte-identical turn-to-turn (any stray timestamp/turn-counter leak into the prefix kills caching) and check `usage.cache_read_input_tokens`>0. TTL 5min (warm during active play; one cold write after idle). **Anthropic-only** — lives in `PROVIDERS.anthropic.buildBody`; OpenAI caches automatically, Grok/Gemini differ. Directly serves #24 + the subscription cost model. | M | — | ✅ Done (v1.151) — `buildSysPrompt` returns `{stable, volatile}` (stable: rules → adult → role → tone+note → narrative DNA → MECHANICS/STATE TAGS/companion tags; volatile: identity → sheet → world/party → quests/skeleton → memory → geo → combat/history → REMINDER → **STYLE, kept last for voice fidelity**); Anthropic `buildBody` sends a two-block `system` array with `cache_control:ephemeral` on the stable block; other providers flatten via `sysJoin()`; `sysOverride` strings unchanged. 6 new engine tests incl. the byte-identical-stable invariant (70 total). **Measured (12-turn harness runs on the Runelords t54 save, Sonnet 4.6, telemetry #21):** stable block = 4,889 tok; total prompt/turn identical before vs after (12,160 vs 12,162 tok — clean A/B). Cold write turn 1, then **11/11 cache hits** (reads = exactly 11×4,889). Turn-bucket cost **$0.5056 → $0.3594 (−28.9%)**; input-only cost/turn −32.3%; all-in batch (turns+actions+summarize) $0.599 → $0.450 (−24.9%). Caveat: 5-min TTL — a player idling >5 min between turns pays a re-write (1.25×) instead of a read; worst case ≈ +0.4¢/turn over uncached, negligible. Prose voice spot-checked, held. Future headroom: 2nd breakpoint on slow-moving volatile chunks (skeleton/memory TOC) or message-history caching. |

| 15 | **Blueprint cloud library** — store campaign blueprints (full `.campaign` packages) in the cloud so they're available across your devices (a **PERSONAL** library — community *sharing* was scrapped 2026-07-08; see Architecture ▸ "Community blueprint sharing — scrapped"). A blueprint is the complete campaign authoring package: skeleton (3-act narrative spine + arcs), seeded NPCs with roles/knowledge, locations with canonical descriptions, custom rules, starting region, and blueprint name. It is NOT a save file — no player character, no combat state, no XP/gold. **Two save surfaces:** (1) upload a local `.campaign` file to the library ("☁ Save to library" alongside the file import on the Tone step); (2) **Export from active game** — File menu item that packages the current `worldState.skeleton`, `worldState.npcs[]` (minus the player PC), `memory.locations`, `customRules`, `worldState.world.region`, and `worldState.campName` into a portable blueprint (for your own reuse across devices, or to feed the curated-catalog pipeline #44). **Two load surfaces:** (1) cloud tab in a blueprint browser modal on the Tone step; (2) local `.campaign` file tab (existing flow). **Server:** new `blueprints` table mirroring the `characters` table pattern — `(user_id, slug, name, blueprint_data, updated_at)`. Three endpoints: `GET /api/blueprints`, `POST /api/blueprints`, `DELETE /api/blueprints/:slug`. **Storage adapter:** `listBlueprintLibrary(cb)`, `saveBlueprintToLibrary(bp, cb)`, `deleteBlueprintFromLibrary(slug, cb)`. **Export sanitization:** strip act/arc `status` back to `pending`/`active` on the first act (so it plays fresh); exclude `worldState.character` and all per-run state; exclude `worldState.transcript`. | L | Any | ⚠ Mostly done — **audit 2026-07-04 found far more built than this row admitted:** server `blueprints` table (WITH the `public` column) + all 3 endpoints live on Fly (verified 401 from deployed `/api/blueprints`); `storageAdapter.listBlueprintLibrary/saveBlueprintToLibrary/deleteBlueprintFromLibrary` shipped; game-side save + browse surfaces exist (ui.js library modal + save-to-library). **v1.174 added the Blueprint Designer surfaces: ☁ Publish (validate → upsert to personal library, same name republishes) + ☁ Library… (list with PUBLIC badge/date/act count, Open into editor, delete w/ confirm).** Auth rides in from the app's login via same-origin localStorage (`autoConnect`); disconnected state points at File ▸ Connect. Wire verified against the LIVE server (bogus-token 401 path end-to-end in preview). **Community browse: SCRAPPED (2026-07-08)** — see Architecture ▸ "Community blueprint sharing — scrapped". The server's `public` column stays dormant (no browse-public endpoint, never flips for user content). | 16 | **Mobile error reporting** — automatically send an email when a runtime error occurs on mobile (where the console is invisible). Client calls a webhook; webhook sends email to pmegow@gmail.com. Three implementation options: (A) **Google Apps Script** — free, no new accounts, ~20 min setup: create a GAS web app that accepts POST and calls `MailApp.sendEmail()`; (B) **EmailJS** — pure browser JS, 200 free emails/month, ~30 min; (C) **Fly.dev `/api/error` endpoint** — most control, requires server changes + email provider (Resend free tier). Recommendation: Option A. Client side is the same regardless: a debounced `reportError(context, message, detail)` function wired to `window.onerror`, the skeleton catch block, `callGM` error paths, and any other caught errors. Debounce to 1 per 30 s so a cascading failure doesn't flood the inbox. | S | Sonnet | ⚙ **Client shipped v1.352** — new `error-report.js` (loads right after globals.js; in the SW shell + engine-test loader): `reportError(ctx,msg,detail)` with 30s debounce (suppressed errors counted, count rides the next email), 10/session hard cap (boot-loop guard), reentrancy latch, 4KB detail cap; payload = version/campaign/turn/URL/UA/online/timestamp. Wired: `window.onerror` + `onunhandledrejection`, turn catch + re-roll catch + skeleton catch + actions catch (game.js), summarize catch (memory.js), and the **Piper narration-death crumb** (tts.js — the field-crash forensics now email themselves). 5 engine tests (suite 619 green); live-verified: 3 uncaught errors → exactly 1 POST, payload complete. ✅ **DONE end-to-end 2026-07-18 (v1.354):** GAS web app deployed (user), /exec URL wired into `ERROR_WEBHOOK_URL`, and BOTH channels verified against the real endpoint — one crash email + one user-report email (screenshot attached, quest+provider hints fired) landed in the inbox. GAS maintenance note: script edits go live via Deploy ▸ Manage deployments ▸ edit ▸ New version — a fresh "New deployment" mints a DIFFERENT URL and orphans the wired one. Caveat (accepted): the URL ships in the public repo/site, so it's spammable in principle; MailApp's 100/day quota bounds the blast radius, and the GAS app can be re-deployed to a fresh URL if abused. **#16b user-initiated reports shipped v1.353** ("not all bugs are crashes" — nonsense suggestions, hallucinations, drift): File ▸ ⚠ Report bug (game screen; via the buildFileMenus spec) → menu closes → DOM screenshot (vendored html-to-image `toSvg` + OUR OWN Image/canvas/JPEG encode — the lib's `toJpeg` awaits `img.decode()`+rAF and hangs in embedded Chromium, live-verified; 8s timeout, every failure → loud text-only fallback; iOS WebKit taints foreignObject canvases → text-only there too) → modal (thumbnail + textarea + Send/Cancel, send-failure shown inline, text preserved) → same webhook, `kind:"user-report"`, NO crash debounce (in-flight latch only). Auto-context: state digest + last 5 exchanges + newest RAW tagged GM response + on-screen suggestions + `ER_REPORT_HINTS` table (quest/render/provider/tts/combat/memory/sync mentions attach those settings; keys/tokens never gathered — engine-tested). 6 more suite tests (625 green); full flow live-verified incl. 500-failure path. 🔧 **v1.365 screenshot hardening** (user report 2026-07-18 — no field report had EVER carried a screenshot): ONE bad `<img>` in the captured subtree rejected the whole `toSvg` — a src-less img (the static `#car-portrait-img`) resolves its empty src to the PAGE URL, html-to-image embeds the returned text/html as the "image", and its error event kills the capture; reproduced + fix proven live in desktop Chromium. Class fix in `_bugCapture`: `_bugShotFilter` drops src-less imgs at clone time + `imagePlaceholder` (1×1 GIF) absorbs unfetchable srcs (CORS-blocked fal.media scene renders, 404s — the lib throws on 404 and falls back to the placeholder); instance fix: the car-mode img ships with a placeholder src. iOS Safari remains text-only by design (foreignObject canvas taint, documented in `_bugCapture`). SECOND leg found once the client leg was fixed (the first-ever screenshot payload exposed it): GAS was never granted the **Drive scope** — `storeScreenshot_` throws "You do not have permission to call DriveApp.createFolder", swallowed by doPost's try/catch into a response body nobody read (the AUTHORIZE_ME prompt-suppression lesson, Drive edition). Fixes: `AUTHORIZE_DRIVE` one-shot added to `dev/gas-error-webhook.gs` (deploy step 5b — ⚠ USER ACTION: run it in the GAS editor once + approve), and per no-silent-failures the client now READS the webhook response body — `_erPost` passes it through, `sendUserReport`/`_erSend` console-warn partial stores, the modal toasts "⚠ Report sent, but the server couldn't store the screenshot" instead of a clean ✓. |

Two facets of ONE mechanism: **(a) pacing** — complete an act in ~100 turns (all campaigns); **(b) blueprint fidelity** — when an AUTHORED adventure is loaded, follow its arcs rather than letting backstory personalization supplant them (the v1.224 Vyrindra drift: an emergent notation-seal subplot displaced the Skinsaw arcs). One symptom in the t308 audit F3 (308 turns still in Act 1 = a pacing failure AND a spine-drift at once). | S | — | ✅ **Done (v1.231)** — both nudges live in `buildSkeletonBlock` (api.js): ① a **blueprint-fidelity** line gated on `worldState.blueprintName` ("AUTHORED CAMPAIGN — the acts/arcs are the spine; COLOR the beats with backstory, never replace them"); ② an **act pacing budget** — `worldState.actStartTurn` (init at startGame, reset on `[ACT_COMPLETE:]`, migrated) drives a soft nudge once the ACTIVE act runs past `ACT_TURN_BUDGET`=100 turns (the scene-level "4+ turns" rule never caught an act-scale stall like t308). Both carry an **anti-over-rail guard** ("steer toward the ending; do NOT skip an active crisis or cut a scene mid-stakes") so agency + the won't-skip-a-crisis behavior survive. 4 engine tests (gated line, budget threshold, measured-from-actStartTurn, ACT_COMPLETE reset; 247 total). **Live-compliance (does the model obey) pending a post-fix playthrough** — the injection is deterministic + tested. **⛨ Per-ARC pacing follow-up (v1.296, 2026-07-15) — the t727 Runelords save:** the act-level budget alone could not catch a SINGLE arc metastasizing — "The Skinsaw Man" arc ran ~220 turns (Foxglove reached at t508, then spawned an emergent self-replicating "Skinsaw Network" of numbered conspirators that never converged; authored villain Ironbriar never appeared) while the act nudge just repeated "the act is long". Added `ARC_TURN_BUDGET`=50 (half the act budget) + a per-arc clock `arc.startTurn` (stamped at both init sites `stampSkeletonStatus`/`applyBlueprint`, both transition handlers `[ARC_COMPLETE:]`/`[ACT_COMPLETE:]`, and lazily backfilled at load for existing saves — at the CURRENT turn, since a long arc's true origin is unknowable and any earlier guess false-fires a young later arc). When exactly ONE arc is active and outlives the budget, `buildSkeletonBlock` fires a TARGETED "close THIS arc → `[ARC_COMPLETE:title]`" nudge that SUPERSEDES the generic act-turn line; skipped for parallel/multi-active acts (overstay unattributable) and unstamped arcs (fail-safe). Export (`buildBlueprintFromGame`) strips `startTurn` so blueprints stay clean. Drift-surface (`buildSkeletonBlock`/skeleton lifecycle) → Fable-tier, critically reviewed before code, volatile-half only (stable cache untouched), spot-checked on the real t727 save (arc nudge confirmed firing at t778). Soft-nudge-only per user pick (2026-07-15). 9 new engine tests (458 total). **⛨ Inverse arc/quest desync detector ADDED (v1.297, user-designed 2026-07-15 — initially declined, then requested):** `buildArcDriftNudge` (api.js, NOTE_BUILDERS registry) — the mirror of `buildArcQuestNudge`: fires when an arc is still `active` but its same-name quest has ALREADY completed+archived in `memory.quests` (the exact t727 shape: 'Skinsaw Man' quest closed, arc lingered, emergent 'Skinsaw Network' spawned) AND no LIVE quest still matches the arc title (legit-tracked arcs skipped). SOFT only — offers `[ARC_COMPLETE:]` OR "real work remains, that is fine — do NOT force it closed", re-anchoring the authored arc objective; never auto-closes (user's one worry = premature close). **RE-FIRES every `ARC_DRIFT_RECHECK`=50 turns** per pair (`worldState.arcDriftNudged[key]=lastTurn`) rather than latching once, so a "justify and forget" can't let the open arc go stale unwatched. Silent in combat without consuming the timer. 7 engine tests (465 total); spot-checked firing on the real t727 save (correct note, immediate re-call silent, re-fires at +50). **⛨ Live-compliance VERIFIED (Fable audit 2026-07-16, [AUDIT_ARC_NUDGES.md](audits/AUDIT_ARC_NUDGES.md)):** 7-trial playtest loop on the real t343 Runelords save (72 Sonnet GM turns, $6.20, corpora in `testRuns/arc_nudge_loop/`) — drift nudge 2/2 one-nudge-one-close with zero scene disruption; budget nudge converges 4/4 (t727 metastasis signature absent in every trial) and closes when the fiction's climax is reached; premature-close probes 2/2 SAFE (200-turn pressure vs a deliberately-stalling player → no railroading; pressure expressed diegetically, "steer don't teleport" observed in prose); one trial captured the v1.296→v1.297 composition firing organically (quest closed t348 → drift detector caught the desync in the wild → arc closed t349). Fable review also closed the todo_checkWithFable entry: split integrity verified, backfill choice signed off, guard wording validated empirically (no edit needed), parallel-act survivor clock recorded as a documented low-severity limitation. |

| 24 | **Sync hardening (client) — timeout, retry, visible failure state.** Born from the 2026-07-03 incident: a Fly host died, every sync POST from the phone hung, and the FIRST hang permanently wedged `_syncing=true` in storage-adapter.js — all later syncs (incl. the beforeunload/visibilitychange flush) early-returned as `_pendingSync`, so 20 turns accumulated locally with the UI showing "Connected" the whole time. Work: (a) **timeout the state POST** (~20s, AbortController) so a hung fetch always resolves and resets `_syncing`; (b) **retry** on next saveAll / `online` event / app-foreground — local is source of truth, so one successful POST after recovery uploads the complete current state, no replay needed; (c) **visible failure state** — track `lastAckedTurn` (set only on 2xx), and when `worldState.turn` pulls ahead show a red ☁ + "N turns unsynced" in the membar, toast after consecutive failures, and a dedicated "session expired — reconnect" toast on 401 (currently every failure is console.warn, invisible on mobile); (d) apply the same timeout to `syncPortrait` and the campaign-list GET (the latter's 60s timeout is why "Waking server up" flashes forever against a dead host). | S | — | ✅ Done (v1.152) — `_tFetch` (AbortController, 20s) wraps the state POST, portrait PUT, and campaign-list GET (old 60s picker timer replaced). `_syncOk`/`_onSyncFail` track `_lastAckTurn` + consecutive `_failCount`; `syncStatus()` exposed; ui.js `updateSyncBadge()` renders a red ☁ membar badge ("N turns unsynced" / "sync failing"), refreshed every turn AND on every sync result. Toasts: once at 3 consecutive failures, dedicated "Session expired — reconnect" on 401 (once per outage). Retry on `online` event + app-foreground (when unsynced>0); `load()` seeds `_lastAckTurn` from the server's turn so a device that's AHEAD shows the gap immediately. storage-adapter.js now loads in the headless test suite (72 assertions). **Fault-injection verified live in preview:** connection-refused ×3 → badge + single toast; bogus token vs real server → 401 toast; hanging fetch (dead-host clone) → aborted at 20s, `_syncing` reset, next sync attempted. The 2026-07-03 failure mode now self-heals in 20 seconds and is visible from turn one. |

| 27 | **RAG-based episodic memory** — retrieval over the verbatim transcript to fight summary-loss drift (details vanish in chapter compression) and saturation drift (rule collapse under an ever-growing `memoryTOC`, the 0/54 naming failure). Design discussion held 2026-07-03; full decisions in [RAG_MEMORY.md](DOC/RAG_MEMORY.md). Headlines: **augment don't replace** (structured memory stays authoritative for current truth; retrieval carries episodic texture ONLY — else you trade for retrieval-miss + stale-chunk drift); **entity-keyed retrieval, not vectors** (transcript entries tagged at write time from the tags `applyMuts` already parses; retrieval = scene ∪ input entities; embeddings are a maybe-never Phase 2); **read-side only** (summarize() untouched — the one irreversible failure is weakening write-side); **memoryTOC diet ships behind the same flag** (the real anti-drift win is prompt decongestion, and it pays for the excerpt tokens); **Dev Mode kill switch per campaign** (flag off must reproduce today's TOC byte-for-byte — engine test). Payoff is back-loaded: judge on a mature save, never a fresh harness run. | M | — | ✅ Phase 1 done (v1.154) — entity index written on every GM transcript entry (`logTranscript` 3rd param, raw tags + known-NPC name scan; lazy backfill for old entries), `ragRetrieve()` (memory.js) scores by scene∪input entity overlap, skips the last 10 turns, ≤3 excerpts / ~600 tok, oldest-first, turn-stamped, "current state overrides" framing; injected in `buildSysPrompt`'s VOLATILE half only; `memoryTOC` diet behind the same flag (lore → scene-relevant + recent 8 cap 12; chapter summaries dropped as duplicates of STORY SO FAR). Toggle: Dev Mode ▸ 🗂 Episodic memory… (`showRagModal`, all 3 menus) → per-campaign `worldState.ragMemory`, rides the sync blob. 7 engine tests (79 total) incl. flag-off byte-identity + stable-half invariance. **Live-verified on the Runelords t54 save: diet paid for the excerpts (volatile net −480 chars WITH a 1,360-char excerpt block); flag-off volatile byte-identical to baseline.** **✅ v1.230 — DEFAULTED ON** (validated on the t308 mature save, [AUDIT_t308.md](audits/AUDIT_t308.md): RAG is what carries a long campaign through NPC-key fragmentation + the cap-30 window — the exact "judge on a mature save then decide" trigger the design set). `ragEnabled()` now returns `ragMemory!==false` (on unless explicitly disabled → zero migration; every existing/new campaign gets it). Dev Mode toggle retained as an off-switch escape hatch; explicit-off still byte-identical to the pre-RAG prompt (engine-tested). Remaining: Phase 2 (vectors) only if entity retrieval misses in play. |

| 28 | **Summarize-tail retention — kill the amnesia cliff.** Finding from the t160 Runelords analysis (2026-07-04): with prose-voice responses at 1,300–3,100 chars, `SUMMARIZE_AT` (1200 tok, tuned in the 2-3-sentence-cap era) fires **every ~2 turns** in mature campaigns (chapter stamps 152/155/157/159), so the GM's verbatim window is ~2 turns deep and `summarize()` clears `sessionLog` to ZERO — object-level facts (the Mage-Hand pin grab, t146) evaporate within a few turns, and the GM *confabulates* when asked to recall them (t160's "correction" invented a false version of the grab — wrong place, wrong scene). **Fix: after a successful summarize, retain the most recent 2–3 exchanges in `sessionLog` instead of clearing it** — extraction unchanged (no RAG write-side violation), the cliff becomes a slope, scene continuity survives the boundary. Also halves the summarize cadence (retained tail re-counts toward the threshold — verify the loop can't thrash). Engine-testable. Side benefit: summarize firing every 2 turns is also a 2,000-token call every 2 turns — cadence relief is cost relief. | S | — | ✅ Done (v1.165) — `retainSessionTail()` (memory.js) keeps the newest ≤3 exchanges within a 1,600-token cap (newest pair always survives) instead of clearing; `worldState.sessKept` marks the already-extracted head so `sessionTokens()` counts only NEW tokens (retained tail can't re-trip the gate — no thrash) and the extraction prompt slices from the marker (nothing filed to memory twice — a stronger guarantee than the spec's "tail re-counts" sketch, which would have double-extracted). Cadence relief delivered via `SUMMARIZE_AT` 1200→2400 (safe now the boundary has no cliff): fires every ~5 mature exchanges instead of ~2. Degraded-failure path retains too. Stale markers fail safe (`sessKeptStart()`). 6 new engine tests (97 total) + full two-cycle boundary simulation against the t198 save: 3 exchanges retained, zero re-extraction, zero lost exchanges, RAG skipN follows the deeper window. |

| 29 | **futureEvents hygiene — stale "pending" pollution.** t160 save: `memory.futureEvents` pegged at the 30 cap, with long-resolved items still pending ("combat with six chanters," "investigate the altar," "far passage" — all completed turns earlier); the GM rarely emits `[FUTURE_EVENT_RESOLVED:]` on its own, so PENDING EVENTS injects finished business every turn — attention noise in an already-saturated prompt. Options (combine): ① prompt nudge — the STATE TAGS block tells the GM to resolve events it narrates as done; ② auto-expire — drop unresolved events older than ~40 turns (`setTurn` is already stored); ③ have `summarize()` also return a `resolvedEvents[]` list (it reads the whole session anyway). Start with ②, it's free and deterministic. **t198 escalation (2026-07-04): 7 of the 30 pending slots are "find Shalelu" variants (set t159–t189) while Shalelu joined the party at ~t189 — near-duplicate spam on file is half the problem; add dedupe in `fileFutureEvent` (token-overlap against existing pending) to the work list.** | S | — | ✅ Done (v1.166) — ②+③+dedupe shipped (① prompt nudge skipped: ③ covers it without spending stable-prompt attention). `fileFutureEvent` near-dup dedupe: ≥2 shared stemmed tokens (`feTokens`) AND ≥half the smaller fingerprint → refresh the existing entry's `setTurn` instead of filing a twin. `expireFutureEvents()` drops unresolved events older than `FUTURE_EXPIRE_TURNS`=40 (globals.js) at every summarize; unstamped entries grandfathered. Extraction prompt now lists pending events under "ANTICIPATED EVENTS"; the extractor echoes finished ones as EXACT text into `resolvedEvents[]` → `resolveFutureEvent` removes them. Filing refactored into sync `applySummaryExtract()` (testable without an API call); order expire→file→resolve so a same-window set-and-finished event nets out removed. Verified against the t198 save: 7 Shalelu variants → 2 genuinely-distinct phrasings, both Hemlock items survive, backlog decays 26→6 by t230, and a stubbed end-to-end summarize resolves an echoed item. 6 new engine tests (103 total). |
| 30 | **Saved renders reach the right place per platform, and come back after a reload.** *(Rewritten 2026-07-27 — the original ask was "renders saved on the phone should go to the Photos app; on the desktop to the Pictures folder; leave pointers so a 'clear cache and reload' can restore them; skip images that no longer exist." Two halves of that are **not reachable from a web page** and the row now says so instead of implying they are.)* <br><br>**What a browser genuinely cannot do:** write into the iOS **Photos app** or the Windows **Pictures** folder directly. The only routes are the OS **share sheet** (Web Share Level 2 — `navigator.share({files})`, the sole path to Photos) and a **user-chosen folder / download** on desktop. And Photos is **write-only** to a web page: an image shared to the phone can never be read back, so *the phone can save but can never restore*. That asymmetry is inherent, not a shortcoming of this build. <br><br>**Also uncovered while mapping:** scene renders were **never persisted at all** — no `worldState.renders`, no transcript record, and the fal.media URL is a temporary CDN link that expires — so "leave pointers" had nothing durable to point at; and `_campFolderHandle` was a **plain var**, so every reload silently dropped the chosen folder and the next save reverted to a download with no warning. | M | Any | ✅ **Done (v1.457, 2026-07-27).** **① Platform-correct saving:** one funnel `saveRenderImage(blob,filename,turn)` (ui-files.js) — share sheet → campaign folder → download. A **dismissed** share sheet counts as handled (`AbortError`), never falling through to a surprise download of a file the user just declined. **② The folder survives a reload:** the FSA directory handle is persisted in IndexedDB (`tnd_fs_v1`) and restored at boot. Permission deliberately is *not* re-requested at boot (no user gesture exists there) — a lapsed handle parks in `_campFolderPending` and is re-granted inside the next Save click, which is a gesture. Clearing the folder now also clears the persisted copy, or "cleared" would silently un-clear itself next reload. **③ Pointers + restore:** `worldState.renders` holds `{f,t,k}` POINTERS ONLY — never image bytes — capped at `RENDER_PTR_CAP`=60 from the front (it rides the sync blob, so it gets a bound like every other accumulator). `k` records where it went: `renders` (folder → **restorable**), `share` (Photos → never), `download` (path unknown). At boot, after the narrative rebuild, `restoreSavedRenders()` re-attaches each restorable image to the narration frame of its own turn — `addMsg` now stamps `data-turn` so that anchor is machine-readable instead of parsed out of display text. **A file deleted from disk is skipped silently** (the row's explicit requirement); so are pointers for turns not on screen. Idempotent. Blob URLs are revoked on load — no leak. **Verified:** 856 green (5 new pure-pointer tests: shape, same-file replacement, front-capping, junk tolerance, defaults) + **live in-browser** — IndexedDB round-trip; granted handle adopted; lapsed handle parked, NOT adopted, then re-granted on a gesture; all four save routes correct with the dismissed sheet producing zero downloads; restore attached the present file, skipped the deleted one, ignored the Photos-only and orphan pointers, and added nothing on a second run; zero console errors. **Untested:** a real iOS share sheet (needs a device) — the branch is feature-detected and falls back cleanly. <br><br>**v1.458 field fix (same day) — ROUTING ORDER WAS WRONG on desktop.** Field report: "works on the phone… on the desktop I've made the campaign folder, but hitting save brings up a 'share' UI." Root cause: v1.457 tried the share sheet FIRST, and **desktop Chrome implements `navigator.share` too** (the Windows share UI) — so a user who had deliberately configured a campaign folder got a share dialog instead of their folder. **A configured folder is an explicit instruction and now always wins.** The share sheet is reserved for a browser with NO folder picker at all (`window.showDirectoryPicker` absent = iOS Safari), where it is the only route to Photos; on a desktop with no folder chosen it is a plain download plus a hint to set one. Capability check, never UA sniffing. Also: the toast now names the WHOLE path (`Saved to Runelords/renders/Runelords_Ammut_t1234.jpg`) instead of a bare `renders/…` the user had to guess at, and the extension follows the blob's real MIME type (fal returns PNG for some models; a .jpg that is really a PNG confuses the OS and the restore path). Re-verified live for all three routes: desktop+folder → folder with **zero** share sheets; desktop without a folder → download; phone → share sheet. <br><br>✅ **FIELD-CONFIRMED BOTH PLATFORMS (user, 2026-07-27):** phone → share sheet → Photos ("works on the phone"), and desktop → campaign folder + **persistence across reload confirmed** ("render saving and persisting on the desktop confirmed"). That closes the row's last caveat — the iOS share sheet was the one branch a lab could not exercise, and the folder-survives-a-reload fix was the silent bug this work uncovered rather than something the original row asked for. Nothing outstanding. |

| 40 | **Core Memory — a permanent, always-injected memory tier for defining moments.** Fills the gap between the tiers that churn/compress (`knowledge[]`/`events[]` cap and evict FIFO — capped at 12/8 by audit E51/E50; `chapters` compress hard) and RAG (verbatim but *probabilistic* and read-side only). No current tier says "this moment is load-bearing — never forget it, keep it in front of the GM." A wedding, a betrayal, the night someone nearly died can age out. `firstEncounter` (write-once "when met", survives merges) and the player's `storyBeats[]` are half-steps; Core Memory is the coordinated generalization. **The design call that decides whether it works: what WRITES to it.** Do NOT lean on the GM's own judgment — every "emit a tag when you decide it matters" system (quest lifecycle, FUTURE_EVENT_RESOLVED) has been spotty. Anchor on **engine-detected triggers** (already visible in `applyMuts`): ① a party member's (or player's) HP crossing below ~10% of max — deterministic, captures near-death peaks, ~trivial hook; ② a companion joining/leaving; ③ a `[RELATIONSHIP:]` descriptor forming/changing to something weighty (Married, Betrayed, Sworn). Softer beats (a "first time", a moment the GM judges momentous) lean GM-authored via an OPTIONAL `[CORE_MEMORY:name\|text]` tag — enrichment on top, never the foundation. **v1 shape:** try a **shared party-level** `worldState.coreMemories[]` first (sidesteps "did this companion witness it?"), injected as a small always-present "DEFINING MOMENTS" block; cap **generously** (~25) not never — overflow is a signal the trigger threshold is too loose, not that we need infinite storage; keep each entry one sentence (it costs prompt tokens every turn). **Phase 2:** per-companion attribution ("Bram remembers *his* near-death"). **Design alongside the existing tiers** (lore/knowledge/events/chapters/RAG/storyBeats) so we don't add a 7th uncoordinated store — thematically adjacent to #10 (both are GM-authoritative persistent data). **User note (2026-07-06):** not sold on "party-shared" — build it that way FIRST to validate the mechanic, then revisit per-companion. | M | Fable | ⚙ **v1 SHIPPED (v1.243, 2026-07-10; pre-review presented + approved same day).** Engine-detected triggers ONLY, via a snapshot-diff (`coreMemorySnapshot`/`detectCoreMoments`, game.js) wrapped around `applyMuts` at the TURN call sites — deliberately ZERO parser contact (nothing implemented twice pre-cutover, invisible to the UA1 shadow diff, syncCharSheet audits excluded). Triggers: HP crossing below max(1, floor(10% maxHp)) for player + companions (crossing semantics = free hysteresis); companion join/leave + party-member death; weighty `[RELATIONSHIP:]` descriptors (`WEIGHTY_REL_RE`, globals.js). Party-shared `worldState.coreMemories[]` (rides the sync blob; `migrateWorldState` adds it), cap `CORE_MEMORY_CAP`=25 with oldest-near-death-first eviction to `memory.archive.coreMemories` + loud warn; one-sentence template entries, toast on file. Injected as the DEFINING MOMENTS block (volatile half, before REMINDER/STYLE); EMPTY list renders nothing — byte-identical prompt for existing saves (engine-tested both halves). Char sheet shows a read-only Defining Moments section (player sheet only). 13 engine tests (288 total). **Deferred by design:** the optional `[CORE_MEMORY:]` GM tag → post-cutover table entry (user-approved 2026-07-10); per-companion attribution → Phase 2. **Remaining validation:** defining-moments injection observed on a mature save. **All three TRIGGER classes live-validated on the fresh Playtest-2 run (2026-07-10): join t20, weighty bond t48, companion near-death t71 — ★ toasts + clean one-sentence entries.** UA14 resolved: archive is NOT a Core Memory source — its consumers are the story compiler #5 + future RAG phases. **✅ CLOSED (v1.307, 2026-07-16): the deferred `[CORE_MEMORY:subject\|text]` GM tag is BUILT** — one TAG_TABLE entry routing through the ONE write path (`fileCoreMemory`, game.js — witnessed-by-all fan-out, per-sheet cap+archive, toast; nothing re-implemented; `fileCoreMemory` now returns `filedAny` so the muts line never claims a deduped no-op). Subject via `resolveNpcName`; kind `"gm"`; text clamped to one-sentence length with a loud warn; the (turn,kind,who) dedupe doubles as one-GM-moment-per-subject-per-turn spam control.
