| 41 | **Re-evaluate TTS providers — hosted (Inworld / Cartesia) + local (Kokoro desktop; Piper / Kitten phone) — pricing is just better.** Inworld's voice/TTS pricing beats Cartesia's per-*character* billing, which is the documented TTS cost pain (see #12: shorter prose to cut the Cartesia bill; a `402` = character-quota exhausted). Swap surface is small and already abstracted: `tts.js` routes Cartesia ↔ native browser voice through one path (`speak()`, key in `KEY_K`, native fallback + red-error indicator per #19), so adding a provider is another adapter in the same shape, not a rewrite. **Three contenders, two categories:** ① **Cartesia** + ② **Inworld** are hosted APIs (keyed, per-character billing, ongoing cost, network latency) — the straight cost swap; ③ **Kokoro** is a different animal — open-weight (~82M param, Apache-licensed) local/self-hosted TTS at **$0/character marginal cost**. Kokoro trades the billing line for a hosting + latency + quality tradeoff and **does NOT fit the "route TTS through the T&D server as a keyed proxy under the subscription model" shape** — it'd be a self-host/on-device adapter, not a proxied-key one, so weigh it as an infra decision, not just a price line. **When building:** ① confirm CURRENT Inworld + Cartesia TTS pricing + free tiers (pricing moves — verify at build time); ② A/B voice quality/latency across all three on real narration (cost only wins if the voice holds up for car-audio, the primary use case — Kokoro especially must clear that bar to justify the self-host burden); ③ mirror the existing fallback discipline (key-missing / request-failed → native voice + reason indicator); ④ keep it provider-agnostic like the LLM `PROVIDERS` table so TTS providers become a swappable list, not a hardcode. Ties to #9 (per-character voices) and #12 (TTS cost); the hosted options fold into the server-side-keys move (Architecture: all TTS calls move behind the T&D server under the subscription model), Kokoro would sit outside it. **User note (2026-07-06):** "the pricing is just better" — the motivation is cost; scope is TTS. **(2026-07-13):** Kokoro added as the self-hosted/zero-marginal-cost third contender. **⚠ RESEARCH FINDING (2026-07-13) — Kokoro is a DESKTOP-local model, not a phone-local one; likely disqualified for the car-audio primary use case.** Speed is a non-issue (Kokoro-82M via WebGPU runs faster than real-time everywhere: ~6.5× RTX 4070, ~3.2× M3 Pro, ~2.4× M2 Air; the "brutal slow" demo-site test was choked shared infra, NOT the model). The blocker is **mobile reliability, not raw speed**: benchmarkers who tested it *explicitly avoid WebGPU on mobile* — mobile Safari/Chrome aggressively reclaim tab memory and WebGPU TTS "**fails ~30% of the time**" on phones (fatal for a long, continuous car session). Compounding: WebGPU mobile coverage is fragmented in 2026 (iOS Safari solid only from **iOS 26**; Android Chrome needs **v121+/Android 12+ with Qualcomm-ARM GPU**) AND **iOS WKWebView / Android WebView don't ship WebGPU by default** — so an installed PWA (Add-to-Home-Screen, exactly our car case) may not even expose it. Sustained thermal throttling is a secondary concern behind the memory-reclamation failure. **Consequence for #41:** the "local/$0-per-char" slot is real but Kokoro doesn't fill it *on a phone*. If phone-local TTS is genuinely wanted, the mobile-first models are **Piper** (75MB, CPU/WASM ~3–5× real-time, NO WebGPU dependency — the robust path, lower voice quality) or **Kitten** (24MB/15M params, purpose-built for mobile browsers, ~2–3× WebGPU / ~1.5–2× WASM). Recommend: keep Kokoro as the *desktop*-local option, treat Inworld/Cartesia as the phone/car path, and if local-on-phone matters, evaluate Piper/Kitten instead of Kokoro. Sources archived in chat 2026-07-13 (offlinetts.com browser-TTS showdown, quick-tts.com WebGPU benchmarks, web.dev WebGPU-in-major-browsers). **(2026-07-13, user call) Piper + Kitten are now explicit evaluation contenders — the local-on-phone candidates to actually try:** • **Piper** — 75MB, CPU/WASM only (no WebGPU fragility → the robust phone path), ~3–5× real-time even on WASM, established (Home Assistant / Raspberry Pi lineage); tradeoff is lower/robotic-er voice quality — must clear the car-audio quality bar. • **Kitten** — 24MB / 15M params, purpose-built for mobile browsers & tight memory, ~2–3× WebGPU / ~1.5–2× WASM; smallest footprint, quality vs Piper TBD. **Eval plan when building:** A/B both on real narration prose *on an actual phone* (not desktop — the whole point is mobile viability), scoring (a) does it stay real-time without stutter through a long session, (b) voice quality good enough for immersive narration, (c) reliability across iOS Safari + Android Chrome + installed-PWA context. Winner (if any) becomes the `$0/char` local-on-phone adapter alongside the hosted Inworld/Cartesia path; both slots coexist behind the provider-agnostic TTS table. **⚠ PIPER FINDING (2026-07-13, first hands-on) — raw Piper "completely ignores punctuation" (flat run-on delivery). Root cause + fix are known and on OUR side, do NOT disqualify Piper on this:** Piper synthesizes each input string as ONE utterance and does not self-insert pauses at sentence boundaries inside a block; the demo pages feed it a whole paragraph at once → run-on. The natural sentence pause in real Piper setups comes from **splitting into sentences + inserting silence** (what the CLI `--sentence-silence` does under the hood), NOT from the model reading the period. **Fix = a shared TTS text-prep pass (build once, helps EVERY provider — provider-agnostic, lives beside the adapter table, not welded to Piper):** ① **sentence-split** the cleaned GM prose, synth each sentence, concatenate with a configurable gap (~200ms mid-paragraph, longer at paragraph breaks); ② **punctuation normalization** — EXTEND the existing native-path pass (v1.76 already does em/en-dash → "..." for a spoken pause per #9) to ellipses/semicolons/dashes → clean pause cues; ③ **trim trailing punctuation** before synth (documented Piper gotcha: text ending in punctuation can emit a burst of static at the tail). So the honest eval note: judge Piper's *per-sentence voice quality* from the demo (that IS representative), but expect flat delivery from raw paragraph input — the sentence-split wrapper is the real integration and a solved problem. Sources archived in chat 2026-07-13 (rhasspy/piper issues #492 & #199, HA core #156603 static-tail, Mintplex-Labs piper-tts-web browser build). **✗ KITTEN — EVAL NO-GO (quality), 2026-07-13 hands-on:** user tested it, "sounds like a blown speaker" — audible distortion, not merely a plain/robotic tone. Expected failure mode at 15M params (footprint bought at the cost of quality); it was only ever the size long-shot. Not usable for immersive narration; **dropped from the local-on-phone race.** Leaves **Piper** (with the sentence-split wrapper) as the front-runner for the `$0/char` local-on-phone slot, Kokoro as desktop-only, and Inworld/Cartesia as the hosted path. Revisit Kitten only if a materially better small model supersedes it. **🔧 PIPER SPIKE BUILT (2026-07-13) — `piper_test.html` (satellite, NOT in SW shell; open directly like bible_study.html).** Auditions Piper's real voice on real Ammut prose WITH the sentence-split + punctuation-normalization wrapper the demo pages lack; A/B toggle for raw-vs-split; live per-sentence RTF + char/audio/synth metrics. Uses `@mintplex-labs/piper-tts-web` via esm.sh. **Harvest map (baked into the file as comments):** `normalizeForTTS()` + `splitSentences()` = ✅ lift VERBATIM into a shared provider-agnostic text-prep block in `tts.js` (helps Cartesia + native too); `loadPiper/ensureVoice/synth` = ✅ the Piper adapter core; Web-Audio scheduling = ◐ game already has it (`scheduleChunk` tts.js:214), written to mirror its shape. **VERIFIED WORKING:** library import, voice-model download+cache, sentence-split (6 units on the sample), Web-Audio wiring — all good. **⚠ BLOCKER surfaced = the real Piper integration cost, now concrete:** ONNX Runtime Web's WASM backend won't init in the sandboxed preview browser — first failed fetching ORT wasm from cdnjs (`ort-wasm-simd-threaded.jsep.mjs`), then under COOP/COEP cross-origin-isolation it failed on a `blob:` worker-module import. Root cause is env-specific (the preview Chromium has restricted network egress / worker policy); **the fix for a real deploy is to SELF-HOST the ORT wasm files + pin `ort.env.wasm.wasmPaths` (don't depend on cdnjs at runtime) and get the COOP/COEP combo right — exactly the "messy dependency plumbing" this row warned Piper would cost.** Could not verify audio in-sandbox (also can't judge voice quality by ear from here regardless — that's the user's call). **NEXT (user, ~1hr):** run `piper_test.html` in a REAL desktop Chrome served over http (`npx serve` in the repo folder → open the localhost URL; plain serve = ORT single-thread path, cdnjs reachable on a normal network) and LISTEN — does Piper's per-sentence voice hold up for narration? That's the gate before the ~½-day in-game integration. If yes → harvest the text-prep pass first (needed for every provider anyway), then the Piper adapter. **⛔ SPIKE OUTCOME — DEFINITIVE (2026-07-13, verified in the user's real Chrome, NOT just the sandbox; supersedes the "probably works on a real machine" guess above):** could NOT get CDN-loaded Piper to synthesize; the failure chain is fully characterized and it's an ARCHITECTURAL conflict, not a config tweak: ① the `@mintplex-labs/piper-tts-web` lib hardcodes `ort.env.wasm.wasmPaths` → `cdnjs/onnxruntime-web@1.18.0/`, but the `.jsep.mjs` WASM-glue file doesn't exist for 1.18 on any CDN (glue files began at 1.19) → *fixed* by pinning ORT **1.19.2** + locking wasmPaths to `jsdelivr@1.19.2` via a getter (verified the files 200 there). ② But 1.19.2 ships **ONLY a threaded jsep build** (no non-threaded single-file — `ort-wasm-simd.jsep.*` 404s), and the threaded binary needs `SharedArrayBuffer` even to instantiate → **requires cross-origin isolation** (COOP/COEP). Without COI it hangs; `numThreads=1` just 404s on the missing non-threaded file. ③ WITH COI on, ORT spawns its pthread **Worker from the cross-origin esm.sh module** → COEP blocks it: `SecurityError: Failed to construct 'Worker': script at esm.sh/onnxruntime-web… cannot be accessed from origin`. **Conclusion: Piper-in-browser needs the ORT runtime + worker + wasm all SAME-ORIGIN (self-hosted) AND served with COI headers. CDN-loading ORT (esm.sh/jsdelivr) is fundamentally incompatible with the threaded-build + cross-origin-worker + COEP combination.** Same-origin self-hosting of a bundled lib+ORT effectively needs a **BUILD/bundler step — which collides head-on with the project's core "no build step, no npm dependencies" tenet.** So the real #41 decision if Piper stays in: (a) accept a build/bundle step for the TTS layer (violates a core principle — user call), (b) find a pre-bundled same-origin ORT distribution or a Piper wrapper that inlines a non-threaded WASM (no worker, no COI), or (c) an older non-threaded ORT path. **What DID work & harvests regardless:** the text-prep (`normalizeForTTS`/`splitSentences`) ran correctly (split the sample into 6 units) — still lift it into `tts.js` for every provider. **To judge Piper's VOICE QUALITY now (the actual gate, decoupled from integration):** paste a few Ammut sentences into a hosted Piper demo (e.g. offlinetts.com/app/piper) and listen per-sentence — no engine work needed. Spike file `piper_test.html` retained as the documented scaffold + harvestable text-prep; it will synth once ORT is self-hosted same-origin. **🎧 VOICE VERDICT (2026-07-13, user judged on rhasspy piper-samples + demo.html):** Piper voice quality = **"passable — worth exploring"**; needs a proper LONG-TERM listen (many turns of real narration) to judge fully. **Piper stays a LIVE contender** (not eliminated like Kitten). Run-on/punctuation = confirmed non-issue (our sentence-split wrapper, split-logic already verified; only the gapped-audio-by-ear proof is pending a working synth). **NEXT STEP to enable the long-term listen = get the spike actually producing audio = solve the same-origin ORT problem WITHOUT a build step.** Promising no-build path: **vendor onnxruntime-web (its `.mjs` + worker + `.wasm`) AND the piper lib as local files, wire the bare `onnxruntime-web` specifier to the same-origin copy via a native `<script type="importmap">`** (import maps are a browser feature, no bundler → keeps the zero-build tenet), served over http with COOP/COEP. If import-map vendoring can't force the lib's internal ORT import same-origin, the fork from the SPIKE OUTCOME note stands (accept a scoped TTS build step, or find a Piper wrapper that inlines a non-threaded WASM). This is the concrete task when Piper work resumes. **✅ SOLVED (2026-07-13, spike now SYNTHESIZES) — the no-build vendoring path WORKS; NO build step, NO cross-origin isolation needed.** The winning recipe (all proven live in `piper_test.html`): ① vendor `@diffusionstudio/vits-web@1.0.3` (`vits-web.js` + `piper-DeOu3H9E.js`) AND `onnxruntime-web@1.18.0` (ESM `ort.wasm.min.js` + the **non-threaded** `ort-wasm-simd.wasm`/`ort-wasm.wasm`) as LOCAL same-origin files (~20MB total, mostly the 2 wasm binaries); ② a native `<script type="importmap">` redirects the bare `onnxruntime-web` specifier (vits-web loads it via `await import("onnxruntime-web")` — bare, so import-map-redirectable) to the local copy → **ORT is same-origin, killing the cross-origin-worker wall**; ③ import the same local ORT in the page and LOCK `ort.env.wasm.numThreads=1` + `wasmPaths="/…/ort/"` via ignoring setters (vits-web sets `numThreads=navigator.hardwareConcurrency`→32 and `wasmPaths`→cdnjs/1.18; both must be overridden). numThreads=1 selects the non-threaded `ort-wasm-simd.wasm` → **no worker, no SharedArrayBuffer, no COI** → runs on a plain http server. **Live metrics (Lessac, real Ammut paragraph, 692 chars → 6 sentences):** 38.1s audio synth'd in 18.9s = **2.0× real-time overall**, **2.5–4.5× per sentence** after a one-time ~9s WASM-compile on the first call. Phonemizer (`@diffusionstudio/piper-wasm` jsep) + voice model (HuggingFace) still load cross-origin — fine on a plain (non-COEP) server. **So the earlier "needs a build step" fork is RESOLVED — vendoring + import map keeps the zero-build tenet.** For the real in-game integration this is the shape: vendor ORT+vits same-origin (into the repo/app shell), single-thread, locked local wasmPaths; the sentence-split text-prep still harvests into `tts.js` first. Spike vendored files currently in session scratchpad (throwaway) served by a local static server; **user is now unblocked for the LONG-TERM voice-quality listen.** **⚠ TEXT-PREP FIX (2026-07-13, found in first real use):** single-threaded Piper scales badly on long utterances — a ~500-char run-on sentence (commas, no period) synth'd as ONE unit hangs 30s+. Fixed in the harvestable `splitSentences`: added `MAX_UNIT=220` cap + `packLongUnit()` that sub-splits an over-long sentence on clause boundaries (`,;:`) then word-wraps if needed. Verified: the 521-char hang → 3 units, completes ~3–4.5× real-time. **This cap is MANDATORY for the in-game text-prep** (GM prose routinely has long sentences) — harvest it with the splitter. **✅✅ PIPER VALIDATED — GO (2026-07-13, user heard it working on a proper localhost tab):** verdict = *"Is it the greatest VO? No. Passable, especially considering the cost? Cache-Control` detach; ③ v1.300 Piper adapter (ORT locks, OPFS voice download w/ loud progress, epoch-guarded synth→decode→schedule through the existing AudioContext timeline — Fable added the post-loop stale check); ④ v1.301 `TTS_PROVIDERS` table (native/cartesia/piper — the provider-agnostic table this row always wanted), `ENGINE_K` selection with legacy inference (no existing user's audio changes), 8-voice `PIPER_VOICES` stable, `worldState.piperVoice` two-tier proseAuthor pattern, prewarm-on-enable; ⑤ v1.302 Phase-5 preview verification + stale-indicator fix. **Machine-verified live (sandbox preview, localhost):** vendor files 200 same-origin, NON-threaded `ort-wasm-simd.wasm` selected (locks working — no worker/COI), Lessac model downloaded to OPFS w/ progress + survives reload, full public `speak()` path played ~4s audio and completed cleanly, skip-mid-synth discarded the stale item with zero overlap/errors, `tnd-piper-v1` cache holds exactly the 4 vendor files (separate from the versioned shell cache), engine choice persists across reload. 17 new engine tests (482 green). **Remaining = the human half of Phase 5: long-term LISTEN on real narration (the actual gate), Car Mode live, cross-device on the deployed origin, post-deploy `curl -I` on `/vendor/piper/*` (immutable must win), and a real-deploy confirmation that `tnd-piper-v1` survives a CACHE bump.** Hosted tier (Inworld/Cartesia pricing) still open — unchanged. **✅ MACHINE HALF CLOSED (Sonnet-run + Fable-verified, 2026-07-16):** ① deployed-origin checks PASS — all 5 vendor files 200 on production, byte-identical to repo, `Cache-Control: public, max-age=31536000, immutable` winning (the `! Cache-Control` detach working), correct JS/WASM content-types, shell no-cache, deployed APP_VERSION current; side-catch: `/sw.js` served a duplicated `no-cache, no-cache` token (overlapping `/*.js` rule appends without a detach) — fixed in `_headers` same day. ② `tnd-piper-v1` cache-bump survival: strong local evidence — the piper cache held exactly the 4 vendor files alongside the versioned shell cache through multiple CACHE bumps (k) in the sandbox SW; the real-deploy confirmation remains a formality on the next deploy. ③ Piper synthesis re-verified in the #2 pre-flight (same-origin loads, OPFS voice download to 100%, clean stop). ④ **Hosted-tier pricing RESOLVED (verified on official pages, Fable spot-checked Inworld):** Inworld Realtime TTS-2 = $25/1M chars on-demand with a self-serve ladder ($20/$17.50/$15/$12.50 → "$5/1M" enterprise), ~70 min free ≈ **$3.00/hr of active play** vs Cartesia Sonic-3.5 at $37–50/1M ≈ **$4.49–6.00/hr** — Inworld beats Cartesia 35–45% and takes the paid-quality slot recommendation when the hosted adapter gets built (OpenAI tts-1 ~$15/1M and Google Chirp 3 ~$30/1M could not be verified on primary pages this pass — recheck if either becomes a real contender). **Still human:** the long-term LISTEN (the actual gate) and cross-device on the deployed origin. **🎧 First phone listen (2026-07-16, via Car Mode): "voice is ok, nothing to write home about — we can tweak it."** First tweak shipped (v1.310): **tiered pause gaps** — Piper renders no punctuation pause itself, so `splitSentences` gains a Piper-only `commaSplit` mode (breaks at every `, ; :`, tags each unit's `end` type) and scheduling maps each type to its own independently tunable constant: `PAUSE_COMMA`=0.15 / `PAUSE_COMMA_CLAUSE`=0.22 / `PAUSE_FULLSTOP`=0.44 (doubled from 0.22 by user call) / `PAUSE_PARAGRAPH`=0.88 (tts.js, one block). Vendored file now carries a T&D session-cache patch (one session per voice, release on switch) AND v1.323 caches the PHONEMIZER instance (upstream also spawned a fresh Emscripten module + espeak-ng-data FS per sentence — the second faucet, why v1.322 got further but still died; callMain reuse verified live, loud per-call fallback if a future build cannot re-run main; run-tests tripwire guards BOTH patch markers against re-vendoring). **⚠ v1.324 — THE DELIVERY TRAP + proof (the wasted-tries lesson):** /vendor/piper/* is cache-first from the PERMANENT tnd-piper-v1 SW cache (+immutable header), so the v1.322/v1.323 vendored patches NEVER RELIABLY REACHED installed clients — including the verification sandbox itself (the SW re-registers on load and re-served the stale module; instrumentation caught it: 43 phonemizer wasm instantiations per 43 units on the stale runtime). Delivery now rides `?tnd=`+PIPER_RUNTIME_REV in PIPER_LIB_PATH (query-strict cache.match → new URL misses → fresh fetch; bump the rev with every vendored patch). Side effect: synth much faster after unit 1 (no per-sentence model re-parse). Phone re-test CONFIRMED at v1.324. **v1.327 — the phone-silence class (native worked, Piper+Cartesia silent, desktop fine):** diagnosed from the engine split — both broken engines schedule on the shared AudioContext, native does not. Two iOS facts: a persisted voice-ON flag means a reload never runs toggle(), so the ctx is born OUTSIDE a gesture at first narration (iOS: suspended, resume denied); and iOS has a third state "interrupted" that every suspended-only check missed — even a real Test tap scheduled onto a stopped clock. Fixed: _resumeCtx handles both states everywhere; one-tap document-level unlock armed whenever voice-on persists across a load; visibility-return resume; synth entries REQUIRE state===running or refuse LOUDLY (toast + native fallback for the line — no more silent scheduling); Voice Settings shows a live "Audio:" state line (phone-readable diagnosis). Refusal path proven in preview with a suspended ctx whose resume() lies (the iOS behavior). **v1.328 — the second layer (phone screenshot: Audio RUNNING + r2 loaded + still silent):** a running ctx discarded at the OUTPUT stage = iOS device-level muting — bare WebAudio plays in the "ambient" session category, which the physical ringer/silent switch mutes while speechSynthesis ignores it (the exact native-works split). Fix: primeAudioSession (the Car Mode playback-category primer that exists for exactly this) now runs for ALL WebAudio narration — wired at toggle-on and both synth entries (idempotent). Mute-switch-immune + consistent BT routing. Verified: primer coexists with Piper playback in preview. **v1.328 CONFIRMED WORKING on the phone after an app kill+relaunch** — the kill also cleared a WEDGED pipeline (stranded _playing latch from a native fallback whose iOS completion events never fired; Test taps queued silently behind the phantom). **v1.329 makes that class self-healing:** Test pre-empts via stop() (clears any wedge), per-unit native stall watchdog (forced advance + loud warn — _playing can never strand silently), paused-engine resume() kick, and the Audio diag line shows pipeline state (speaking/idle +N queued); still open: long listen + cross-device; hosted recommendation: Inworld. **v1.334 (Fable piper-audit 2026-07-17, findings #3+#4):** ① primer now survives a voice off/on cycle — _closeCtx left a stale _primerSrc from the closed ctx, so primeAudioSession short-circuited and the v1.328 playback-category claim silently vanished after any 🔇→🔊 (fix: _closeCtx clears it + primeAudioSession verifies the primer is on the CURRENT ctx); ② user pause honored by all anti-wedge machinery — tap-unlock and visibility-return no longer auto-resume a user-suspended ctx (audio restarted by itself while the button showed ▶), the native stall watchdog defers while _paused (keyed on user intent ONLY, so the iOS engine-paused wedge still force-advances), and skip/stop clears the pending watchdog (a live one resurrected the cancelled chain up to ~30s later). **v1.335 (findings #1+#2, vits-web r3):** ① download integrity — upstream S() saved whatever fetch returned, so an HF 404/rate-limit page could be cached to OPFS as the .onnx forever (stored() reports it "downloaded", every predict fails, nothing re-fetches); non-OK now THROWS before anything is written; ② phonemizer .wasm/.data (~18MB) vendored to /vendor/piper/phonemize/ — tndLocate pointed at jsdelivr, which the SW never caches (cross-origin), silently breaking the offline claim; ③ hang rider: the fallback phonemizer promise had no reject path — a load failure hung predict() forever. Tripwire (run-tests.js) extended: r3 markers + on-disk phonemize assets + PIPER_RUNTIME_REV↔TND_VITS_PATCH rev parity (the delivery-trap class is now test-enforced). **v1.336 (finding #5, vits-web r4):** delivery rev extended to ALL permanently-cached piper files — the ?tnd= query previously covered only vits-web.js itself; piper-DeOu3H9E.js (relative import drops the parent's query) + the phonemize wasm/data now ride `TND_DEP_REV` (r1), ort.wasm.min.js rides its own ?tnd=ort-r1 on the import-map entry, and the ORT .wasm binaries (wasmPaths prefix — can't take a query) are documented RENAME-on-change. Tripwire enforces the dep-rev queries in both vits-web.js and index.html. Known residual: stale ?tnd= revs accumulate in the never-purged PIPER_CACHE (a few hundred KB per rev — cosmetic). **v1.337 (finding #6, found DURING live verify; vits-web r5):** upstream `I()` fired the OPFS write (`p()`) without awaiting it — download() resolved while the ~78MB commit was in flight, so the FIRST predict after a voice download read the not-yet-committed (empty) OPFS file → ORT "No graph was found in the protobuf" → whole item failed to native. Timing-dependent (fast connection = reliable repro on desktop; explains any first-download flakiness on phones). Fixed by awaiting the write. **Live-verified end-to-end on localhost:** fresh OPFS → download → immediate first synth OK, warm synth + playback + clean drain OK, runtime line r5, phonemizer served same-origin (zero jsdelivr requests in the network log). **v1.338 (audit round 2, finding #7):** Piper's comma-split treated the [,;:] inside "1,000 gold" / "3:30" as clause boundaries — spoke "one … zero zero zero gold" on every formatted number. `mergeDigitClauses` (tts.js) re-joins digit-tight junctions in BOTH splitSentences commaSplit and packLongUnit; test-first (4 new engine tests, 3 red pre-fix). **v1.339 (findings #8–#11):** ⑧ boot prewarm — a reload with voice-ON never ran toggle(), so the ~9s WASM compile landed inside the first narration of every returning session; loadSettings now prewarms, download-GATED (engine+voice warm only if the voice is already on disk — never a surprise 60MB pull); ⑨ `_piperSerial` mutex — voice ensure/download and every predict (incl. prewarm's) run one-at-a-time on the shared single-threaded wasm session (epoch discards stale results but never prevented overlap; two racing ensures each started a 60MB download); ⑩ WebAudio stall watchdog (`_armCtxWatch`) — the v1.329 native watchdog's missing twin: a mid-read iOS ctx interruption with no hide/show (BT route change, screen on) froze narration silently with _playing latched; now resume-attempted + ONE loud toast + tap unlock armed; ⑪ `_piperVoiceComplete` — stored() only proves an .onnx file EXISTS; now both files must exist non-empty (catches the 0-byte relic a tab kill leaves mid-first-download, and the missing .json that made "downloaded" voices need the network at narration time); any gap re-downloads both. **v1.340 (findings #12–#16, vits-web r6):** ⑫ `navigator.storage.persist()` requested once per session at voice-ensure (OPFS eviction witnessed live — a 78MB voice vanished between desktop sessions; denial logged, non-fatal); ⑬ download stall watchdog in vendored S() — no data/response for 30s aborts LOUDLY (a stalled fetch used to hang ensure forever, and the #9 mutex would have blocked all later Piper ops); ⑭ pagehide/beforeunload mark the crash crumb done — deliberately closing the app mid-read no longer false-alarms "narration died" at next boot (real crashes fire neither event); ⑮ PIPER_CACHE GC — caching a new ?tnd= rev deletes superseded same-path entries; ⑯ truthful per-voice sizes (HEAD-checked: mediums 60MB, LibriTTS R 75MB, Ryan high 115MB) in blurbs, modal info line, and the download toast. **v1.341 (final deep-dive, finding #17 — the last high-severity):** a device where AudioContext can't be created (`_ensureCtx()` null — no WebAudio, or the 22050Hz constructor throws) DROPPED every Piper/Cartesia line silently (`_drain()` skip — no speech, no toast, voice showing ON); both paths now fall back to native with a console warn, same idiom as their other failure branches. Deep-dive residuals filed as low/medium, NOT fixed (see audit table in the 2026-07-17 session): #18 `_escVal` doesn't escape `'` (apostrophe in a voice-bank name corrupts the single-quoted attributes of the settings modal), #19 story replay button queues instead of pre-empting (Test got stop() in v1.329, replay didn't), #20 Cartesia never got Piper's v1.320 scheduling backpressure (bounded ~20MB/response), #21 mic button outside Car Mode doesn't pause narration (dictation can transcribe the narrator), #22 `_lastSpokenText` not persisted (Car Mode ⏮ dead after reload) **⚠ FIELD CRASH (2026-07-17, live car session, v1.345/r7) — iOS tab kill returned as a CROSS-TURN class: 3 full turns fine, killed at unit 9/50 of turn 4 (crumb toast, no pagehide = jetsam).** v1.320–323 each removed a per-sentence accumulator; the one left standing was the v1.322 forever-cached ORT session, whose wasm arena + per-shape plan cache grow with every distinct sentence length (wasm memory never shrinks). **Fixed v1.346 (vits-web r8):** `tndRecycleSession()` export + `_piperMaybeRecycle` in tts.js — between narrations, once ≥`PIPER_RECYCLE_AFTER`(30) synths accumulate on the session, release + background warm-rebuild from OPFS (phonemizer deliberately NOT recycled — that would reintroduce the v1.323 leak class). Crumb now carries cumulative synth count + session uptime (`pc`/`up`) and the boot toast prints them, so the NEXT death (if any) discriminates on-phone: similar cumulative count = ratchet (lower threshold / mid-read recycle / worker isolation next); low count on a giant read = per-read peak. Not iOS-verified from the desk (jetsam unreproducible on Windows) — **next real drive is the verification run**. **Follow-up (same day):** piper_test.html v0.2 gained **soak mode** (memory-slope harness: N silent synths, recycle-every-N for A/B against r8; on iOS the soak button doubles as an on-demand jetsam repro with a crash crumb — the definitive instrument; on Chrome the settled verdict catches JS-side leaks only, since performance.memory can't see wasm memory — live-verified: 100-synth recycle run false-read +128MB/100 from transient model-buffer churn, settled to 21MB idle), rev-locked to the shipped runtime by vendor-check ⑤b. Live bring-up also functionally verified r8's recycle path: 5 mid-soak release+rebuild cycles, zero errors, synth continued seamlessly. Class-level lesson is now a standing audit dimension (Fable_UberAudit.md ▸ "monotonic resources", pointer in CLAUDE.md). **GEMINI TTS FEASIBILITY ✅ 2026-08-16 (owner-commissioned, research only, no code): [DOC/Research/DOC_gemini_tts_feasibility.html](DOC/Research/DOC_gemini_tts_feasibility.html) — TLDR: the arithmetic works. Moving the DEFAULT from sonnet-5 to gemini-3.7-flash-low frees $2.14/50 turns; premium Gemini voice on that same run costs $1.21 → the VOICED experience lands $0.93 cheaper than today's silent default.** Verified at ai.google.dev (not blog aggregators): three preview models — `gemini-3.1-flash-tts-preview` ($1/1M text in, $20/1M audio out), `gemini-2.5-flash-preview-tts` ($0.50/$10), `gemini-2.5-pro-preview-tts` ($1/$20); billing is by DURATION at **25 audio tokens/sec**, not per character (unlike the Cartesia pain in #12) — but characters drive duration, so prose length still multiplies the bill one step removed. Costed against our OWN measured chars/turn from the committed corpora: gpt-5.6-sol $0.58 · gemini-low $1.21 · sonnet-5 $1.58 · opus-5 $2.57 per 50 turns at the streaming rate. **Two facts decide it:** ① streaming exists ONLY on 3.1+, and Car Mode needs it — so the cheap $10/1M tier is effectively unavailable for the primary use case; ② all three models are PREVIEW with restrictive rate limits and no stability promise (this project has already been bitten by retired Gemini IDs 404ing). **Architecture is smaller than expected:** this likely needs NO client change and NO fourth `TTS_PROVIDERS` entry — the existing `server` tier already proxies to the tnd-tts Fly app, so swapping THAT backend keeps the client ladder byte-identical, inherits the whole degradation story free, keeps Piper as the $0 offline floor, and touches zero client drift surface (server-repo change, `tts/`, flyctl). The 2-speaker cap is a non-issue (our queue already synthesizes per-unit with a per-unit voiceId); real work is a curated voice bank mapping the 30 prebuilt voices into #174 casting — data, not architecture. **Genuine product win:** natural-language style control (`[whispers]`, per-speaker emotional direction) pairs directly with the field-validated #96 `[SAY:]` attribution layer — Piper can only render a flat read. **Blocking-ish:** on a player's own key this repeats exactly the objection that killed LiveKit; it only makes sense server-proxied under the BYOK-elimination ruling. **PROBE RUN ✅ same day ($0.06, owner-ordered): the estimates were 45% LIGHT and the published conversion rate is wrong.** Real corpus turn (t10, 631 speakable chars, narration + dialogue) on `gemini-3.1-flash-tts-preview` with a `[SAY:]`-style direction. Measured: **13.27 chars/sec** speech (assumed 15) and **32.0 audio tokens/sec** — NOT the 25 tok/sec published in Google's own worked example and every pricing aggregator, which is what the first model used. Keep the combined ratio instead, it bypasses both: **2.412 audio tokens per character**. Corrected 50-turn TTS cost: gpt-5.6-sol $0.85 · gemini-low **$1.75** · sonnet-5 $2.29 · opus-5 $3.72. **The swap is still favorable but thinner: gemini-low + Gemini voice = $2.25 vs today's sonnet-5 + silent Piper $2.64 → $0.39 cheaper (not the estimated $0.93)** — treat it as "voice for free", not "voice plus savings". **Car Mode ANSWERED: time-to-first-audio 840 ms streaming** (vs the current server chain's 10.17s cold worst case); the same clip non-streaming took 31s, so streaming is mandatory not preferred, and streaming outruns realtime (52s audio delivered in 21s) so playback starts at 840ms and never starves. Two voice samples (Algenib/Gravelly, Gacrux/Mature) delivered to the owner for the listening call — whether it changes register for Sylvie's whispered line is the capability Piper structurally cannot match. **MULTI-ACTOR PROBE ✅ same day (owner question: "can we have multiple actors the way we do with Piper?"): YES, and it costs nothing extra.** The advertised `multi_speaker_voice_config` caps at **2 speakers** — useless for a party scene — so that is NOT the path; the path is the one we ALREADY use for Piper (per-unit synthesis via the existing `voices` map), and the stored `sp` speaker map from the #96 `[SAY:]` layer already carries exactly the needed data. **No new architecture.** Probed on the real Runelords **t1774** (5 speakers — Ammut/Morwen/Frizwick/Daeris/Wyla Ashvane — plus narrator, 1,433 speakable chars); the real `splitSentences` comma-split produced 42 units matching stored `sp.n=42` exactly. Results: **42 units → 17 calls** by grouping CONSECUTIVE same-speaker units (halves round trips AND improves prosody — a narration run is spoken as one breath, not 8 fragments); 6 distinct voices, no cap hit; 94.1s audio; **$0.061 total**; per-call latency ~2.5s median → 17 calls ≈ 42s synthesis for 94s audio, still ~2.2× faster than realtime so pipelining stays ahead of playback. **Economically multi-actor is FREE** — audio bills by DURATION, which doesn't change because six voices spoke it; the only delta is the per-call style direction (689 input tokens across 17 calls = $0.0007, rounding error). **Operationally the constraint is RATE LIMITS, not cost: 3 of 17 calls FAILED at concurrency 3** and only cleared on sequential retry with backoff — the predicted preview-tier limit, now observed. Manageable (playback is self-pacing so synthesis need only stay 1-2 units ahead, and the ladder already degrades to Piper) but a naive fire-all-units implementation WILL fail in the field; Piper never had this constraint because it runs locally. ⚠ **Unverified:** the voice bank is documented by CHARACTERISTIC ("Warm"/"Clear"/"Gravelly"), never by gender — the gender-matched cast is assigned from commonly-reported voice genders and needs a human ear before it becomes a shipped mapping. Demo delivered to owner (98s stitched scene). **OWNER VERDICT on the demo 2026-08-16: voice quality "head and shoulders above Piper, it's not even close" — but the actors "were WAY over acting"; wants subdued.** Root cause is OURS, not the bank's: Google documents natural-language prompting as the ONLY control surface (no cloning, no fine-tune, no pitch/rate/tone parameters — verified), and the demo's directions were character-actor framing ("Speak as Ammut, a hard northern warrior"), which asks the model to PERFORM. Two levers: damping language that describes the READING not the character (audiobook/understated/no theatrical emphasis/let the prose carry it), and low-affect voices (Schedar Even · Charon & Rasalgethi Informative · Iapetus Clear · Algieba Smooth · Umbriel Easy-going · Achernar Soft · Vindemiatrix Gentle) vs the performative end (Fenrir Excitable · Puck Upbeat · Sadachbia Lively · Pulcherrima Forward — the demo narrator, picked because "snappier" was requested, which compounded it). **Effective pool is therefore voice × direction, much larger than 30 names.** ⛨ **DESIGN IMPLICATION for #174 casting: a cast entry can NOT be just a `voiceId` like Piper's — it must carry a DIRECTION STRING alongside the voice; that pair IS the character's voice.** Per-scene/per-mood direction override is the natural extension (tense scene read differently from a tavern scene without recasting). **Escape hatch if 30 proves too few:** Google Cloud TTS **Chirp 3 Instant Custom Voice** clones from ~10s of audio — but it is a SEPARATE API from the Gemini one costed here, access is allow-listed (sales contact) for safety, and it carries consent obligations; don't design around it until prompt+bank is proven insufficient. **FREE-TIER / QUOTA PROBE ✅ 2026-08-16 — and it CORRECTS the multi-actor claim above.** ① **The free tier is moot for this project**: the key reports `serviceTier:"standard"`, i.e. billing-linked/paid, not free. Free-tier RPM/TPM/RPD are **not published in the docs at all** — Google routes them to the per-account AI Studio dashboard (login-gated, unreadable from here); tiers are spend-set (Free → T1 billing linked → T2 $100+3d → T3 $1000+30d). ② **CORRECTION:** the 3/17 scene failures were attributed to "preview-tier rate limits" — that was an over-reading, the retry loop had discarded the status codes so the cause was never established. Probing produced BOTH modes and they are different problems: **429 RESOURCE_EXHAUSTED** ("You exceeded your current quota…") = a real quota ceiling, reached only after minutes of sustained bursting; **503 UNAVAILABLE** ("high demand") = Google-side capacity shedding, unrelated to our usage and the same thing that paced the 50-turn GM run. ③ **Concurrency is NOT the constraint**: a worst-case **8 simultaneous** TTS requests returned **8/8 × HTTP 200** (2.8–7.3s each); 3 paced sequential calls returned 3/3 at ~3s. The ceiling behaves like a rolling per-minute quota, not a parallelism cap. ④ **Real play is far gentler than any probe** — a cast turn is ~17 calls but plays for ~90s and the next turn waits on the player, so sustained rate is ~10-odd calls/min, an order of magnitude under what tripped the quota. Net: bursty probing found a real ceiling, normal play should not approach it, and the ladder covers the case where it does. **FIRST PASS SHIPPED ✅ v1.646 — the `gemini` TTS tier is live.** New `TTS_PROVIDERS.gemini`, **opt-in and default OFF**: it is the only tier that spends the player's money, so the measured cost ("about $1.75 per 50 turns") is printed on the toggle itself rather than buried in a doc, and enabling without a key on file is refused with a toast. Ladder becomes `gemini → server → piper → native` — the paid tier sits ABOVE the free ones so a failure degrades toward free, never toward spend; Piper remains the $0 offline floor, untouched. `_speakGemini` is deliberately `_speakServer`'s twin (same epoch guards after every await, same backpressure, same whole-remainder hand-off down the ladder) with three measurement-forced differences: ① consecutive same-voice units are GROUPED into one request (42→17 on the real t1774 scene — the difference between viable and not, and it reads better because a narration run becomes one breath instead of eight fragments), ② the body is base64 headerless PCM, not WAV, so it needs its own `_pcm16ToAudioBuffer` rather than the RIFF parser, ③ 429 is treated as backpressure (one retry) before degrading. **No re-casting needed:** `_geminiVoiceFor` derives a stable, gender-matched Gemini voice from the Piper cast id the player already has, reading gender from the star bench's own `g` field — so turning the tier off restores the exact previous cast. Settings modal gains the toggle, a narrator picker (subdued voices in their own optgroup) and an editable delivery direction, defaulting to the audiobook-understated wording that answers the over-acting note. **Live-verified through the shipped `TTS.speak()` path, which caught a real defect:** unattributed narration was resolving through the item's PIPER id, so a scene set to Schedar came back in Umbriel — fixed, and pinned by a test whose sabotage reproduces that exact symptom. The same live run exercised the failure path for real (11×200, 2×429 → degraded to Piper, 60s retry window), and a follow-up read confirmed the fix (Schedar → Callirrhoe → Schedar, no degrade). **Known limit, stated honestly:** a dialogue-dense scene paces to roughly 11 calls/min once playback is driving the backpressure, which is close enough to the quota that a long scene can half-degrade mid-read; the real remedy is the server-side proxy (the recommended architecture anyway), not client tuning. Tests: 12 new assertions, two sabotage-proven (default-OFF and paid-above-free), plus the SERVER TTS CONTRACT ladder clause **rewritten stricter** — it now pins native-is-floor, server-above-piper and paid-above-free as invariants instead of one literal string. ⚠ Bank voice genders remain best-effort: Google documents characteristic only, never gender. **▶ TEST BUTTON ✅ v1.647 (owner: "sample text, but no clear way to play it")** — audition button beside the narrator picker, laid out like Piper's. Deliberately NOT routed through `testVoice()`, which walks the Piper/server ladder and would audition the wrong engine entirely; `testGeminiVoice()` pushes a `gemini` queue item directly, so it works regardless of tier selection and clears any open degrade window (a Test press is an explicit "try it now", not a silent no-op for 60s after an earlier failure). **Both inputs are read from the DOM, not the store** — it speaks the voice currently SELECTED and the direction currently TYPED, because tuning delivery is an edit-listen-edit loop and forcing a save between attempts would make it unusable. Needed a `forceVoice` param threaded through `_geminiGroupUnits`/`_speakGemini`: without it the audition would speak the saved narrator and every voice in the dropdown would sound identical. Sample line carries narration AND a quoted line on purpose — quoted speech is where the over-acting shows. Live-verified: selecting Gacrux with an unsaved "read this flatly" direction sent exactly that voice and that text (saved narrator Schedar correctly ignored). +2 assertions. **STREAMING INDICATOR ✅ v1.648 (owner: "we need an indicator that the streaming is happening")** — the ▶ Test button pulses while the request is in flight, reusing the existing `@keyframes pulse` (label swaps to "▶ …", accent-tinted via the new `.tts-testing` class). **Driven by the real milestone, never a timer:** `_auditionPhase("playing")` fires the moment the FIRST buffer source is scheduled, so the pulse cannot keep running through a failure and lie about what is happening; "idle" fires on completion, hand-off, native fallback, empty-units and `stop()` alike, so no path leaves it stuck. Live-verified over 109 samples: pulsed through the ~4s silent fetch window, stopped exactly when audio was scheduled, label restored, never resumed. **One trap worth remembering:** `stop()` now signals idle to cancel a previous audition, so `testGeminiVoice` must arm its callback AFTER its own `stop()` — arming first has stop() immediately clear the brand-new callback and the button never pulses at all. That ordering plus the stop()-clears-pulse clause are pinned as GEMINI TTS CONTRACT source checks (the audition path needs a real AudioContext, so no headless test can reach it), both sabotage-proven. +1 assertion. **#41b THE SYNTHESIS CONVEYOR ✅ v1.653 (2026-08-17, owner field report: "stops mid paragraph… on a voice change; slow to start").** TLDR: the v1.646 loop was strictly sequential, so every narrator→character handoff paid a full non-streaming round-trip IN the seam, and the flat 25s timeout sat BELOW a full 900-char group's ~27s expected synthesis (measured 30ms/char at ~2.5× realtime) — the biggest groups were guaranteed mid-read degrades. Sequentialism rested on a RETRACTED probe reading ("429 at concurrency ≥4" — corrected: 8 simultaneous requests, 8/8 HTTP 200; the ceiling is a rolling per-minute quota). Now: `_geminiConveyor` (promise-free state machine, sync-drivable by the headless tests) runs up to `GEMINI_TTS_PREFETCH`=3 groups' synthesis concurrently and releases results strictly in order — the pump condition `started−taken<depth` bounds in-flight AND landed-unplayed together (memory + quota burst + skip-waste all hang off that one bound); skip/failure aborts in-flight controllers (billing bounded to depth); the remainder hand-off and epoch-guard discipline are unchanged. `GEMINI_TTS_FAST_START_CH`=220 caps the FIRST group so the cold open lands in ~2–3s instead of ~27s; `_geminiTimeoutMs` scales the deadline with group length (+cold-path allowance on call 1). 5 failing-first tests + `dev/sabotage-gemini-tts.js` 5 clauses; the stale "stays SEQUENTIAL" section header re-baselined with the retraction cited. **Owner ear-check owed:** seam continuity + cold open on the live device. Streaming (840ms first-audio) remains the deliberate later step if the cold open still bothers in Car Mode — each conveyor slot becomes a stream, nothing is thrown away. **#41c QUOTA-AWARE RETRIES ✅ v1.654 (same day — owner's first conveyor listen caught it):** an 11-group turn filled the rolling per-minute token quota at group 7/11 (429 → the 1.2s blip-retry gave up → mid-read degrade, "timeout/abort after 14115ms"). A quota window is not a blip: 429s now retry on `GEMINI_TTS_429_BACKOFF_MS`=[5s,15s,30s], honoring Google's own `RetryInfo.retryDelay` from the 429 body when present (`_geminiRetryDelayMs`) — the conveyor's scheduled runway is what affords the waiting. 503/transients keep the old single quick retry. Skip-abort hardened for the long waits: the deadline timer identifies itself (`timedOut`), so an external abort mid-wait reads as "aborted by skip" and the loop stops minting controllers (a 30s wait must not outlive the skip and bill unheard audio). +2 tests, +2 sabotage clauses (5→7... schedule-flattened and parser-gutted both caught). Bycatch noted from the same capture, NOT this row: the server tier reported "availability re-check failed" (tnd-tts cold machine?) and the piper-host FetchEvent redirect warning — both pre-existing. **#41d HINT SANITY CAP ✅ v1.655 (owner's SECOND capture, same day):** Google's RetryInfo quoted **1986s (33 min)** — a deeper quota bucket, not a rolling-minute window; honoring it verbatim parked a live read on an absurd wait while another group hung to its deadline and degraded anyway. `_geminiQuotaWaitMs` (pure): a hint over `GEMINI_TTS_429_HINT_CAP_MS`=45s refuses the wait and degrades IMMEDIATELY with Google's number in the reason, and the degrade window now honors the quoted delay (capped `GEMINI_TTS_DEGRADE_CAP_MS`=10min) instead of the flat 60s — a closed quota stops getting a probe request burned against it every minute. All retry sleeps are now skip-interruptible (`_geminiWait` races the abort signal; #41c's check only ran AFTER the full wait — a 30s sleep outlived its skip). +2 tests, +2 sabotage clauses (9/9). **Open question for the owner:** the 33-min quota on a paid key suggests the TTS preview model's own RPD/TPD ceiling — if these long-turn reads keep closing the quota, the mitigations are voice-tier restraint on long turns or the server tier carrying more (see the availability bycatch). **#41e PRIME THE PUMP ✅ v1.656 (owner report + owner-proposed shape):** a SHORT opening group (one quick quote) with a voice change on the next sentence started and immediately hung — group 2's synthesis wasn't done when group 1's ~3s ran out. The first schedule now HOLDS conditionally (`_geminiPrimeReady`, pure): released the moment group 2 lands, or when the contiguous landed prefix carries `GEMINI_TTS_MIN_LEAD_SEC`=8s alone, or at the `GEMINI_TTS_PRIME_MAX_MS`=8s hold cap (a monster group 2 delays the open boundedly, never forever); a failed opener skips the hold straight to hand-off; single-group reads never hold. Conveyor gains non-consuming `peek(ix)`. +1 test (all five release conditions + the hang shape verbatim), +2 sabotage clauses (11/11). **#41f MODEL LADDER ✅ v1.659 (owner call 2026-08-17 — "set up 2.5 as a fallback. I really don't see a downside"):** TLDR — when 3.1's quota closes mid-read, the read now continues on `gemini-2.5-flash-preview-tts` with the identical voices instead of dropping to Piper. Gemini free-tier quota buckets are PER-MODEL and Google documents ONE shared 30-voice list across its TTS models, so `GEMINI_TTS_MODELS` = [3.1-flash-tts-preview, 2.5-flash-preview-tts]: a closed-quota 429 (over-cap hint, or the patient backoff exhausted) closes ONE model's bucket (`_geminiModelClose` — honors Google's hint, clamped 60s–10min, never shrinks an open window) and the SAME group falls to the next rung — no Piper hand-off, no cast change (narrator pref + the #174 NPC stable picks survive untouched). Later fetches skip closed buckets (no probe burned against a quota Google already said is closed); only an all-closed ladder degrades the engine, its window sized to the EARLIEST bucket reopen instead of the flat 60s; ▶ Test and the opt-in toggle clear the memo (the try-it-now contract); a backup-rung success toasts once per session, and only on success. 2.5 is also half the paid price ($10 vs $20 per 1M audio tokens) — but can never stream, so streaming (the deliberate later step) stays rung-0-only. +5 engine tests + a 4-assertion scripted-fetch battery (dev/tests-41f-gemini-model-ladder.js, registered in run-standalone-suites) + 3 sabotage clauses (14/14). **FIELD-VALIDATED same day (owner listen, 2026-08-17):** 3.1's quota closed live, the fall toast fired, and the read continued on 2.5 — the per-model-bucket assumption is confirmed in the wild. **Ear verdict (owner, same listen):** 2.5 is acceptable — pace slower, delivery flatter, "3.1 is the easy victor," but 2.5 sounds better than Piper. The ladder order matches the ear exactly (3.1 > 2.5 > Piper); 2.5 stays a quota-relief rung, never a cost-saving primary. |

| 60 | **Reliable consumable drain — the durable fix #50a's doc line couldn't deliver (open design problem for a fresh Fable pass).** Recurring symptom: consumables narrated as spent never leave the sheet. Live evidence — the t582 Runelords save (`Rise_of_the_Runelords_Ammut_t582.tnd`): blasting charges stuck at **x4** since t455 (~127 turns) through repeated detonations; at t569 a charge is wedged into the stairs and at t570 another is thrown and detonates ("the blast comes up through the floor like a fist"), yet the sheet never moves off the exact count the player named at t531. **Proven NOT an engine bug (2026-07-13):** loaded the real engine against the actual t582 inventory — `Blasting charge x4` drains correctly to x3/x2/x1 for every normal tag form (`Blasting charge`, plural `Blasting charges`, lowercase); the only no-op is a parenthetical qualifier (`Blasting charge (thrown)`). Count pristine at x4 ⇒ **zero `[ITEM_LOST:]` ever emitted** = model under-emission, the #50a class recurring. **Why the obvious fix is already spent:** the stable prompt rule (tag_table.js:47) LITERALLY names "a charge detonated" as the trigger and was ignored anyway — this is a model-adherence ceiling during fast action scenes, not a missing instruction, so strengthening the nag won't carry it. **Design space explored with the user (all rejected here, recorded so the Fable pass doesn't re-tread):** (a) per-turn auto consumption-check that reads the GM narration and emits the tags — **rejected: a 2nd model mutating authoritative inventory 500×/campaign unattended is the drift surface firing constantly; false-positives remove items that were reached-for-not-used**; (b) reading PLAYER INPUT instead of narration — rejected: input is intent, not outcome (a thrown charge may fizzle); (c) periodic auto-run of the existing Sync inventory audit (#50a option 1) — same drift risk, just less often. **Cost context (t582 telemetry): main GM turn ≈4.15¢ dominates (88%); a suggestion-sized side call ≈0.26¢/turn (~5%)** — so cost is not the blocker, correctness/drift-containment is. **The unsolved question for the Fable pass:** a mechanism that closes the ghost-consumable gap WITHOUT a hallucination-prone second model writing state every turn — candidates to weigh: a deterministic player-driven "use/spend item" sheet affordance (no model in the loop for player-initiated consumption); a tighter, cheaper detector scoped to only items whose names appear in THIS turn's narration (bounded blast radius); or a confidence-gated correction that only ever DECREMENTS a consumable the sheet lists and the narration explicitly spends. ⛨ drift surface (inventory write path + tag docs) — design first, present before building. See the #50 row for the full inventory history and the shipped #50a/b/d work. **✅ DESIGN RESOLVED + SHIPPED (Fable, v1.308, 2026-07-16): the house pattern — engine detects, GM decides.** `detectGhostConsumables` (game.js, the #40/#46/#61 post-pass site — ZERO parser contact): an inventory entry is a consumable candidate if counted (" xN", N≥2 — the t582 form) OR base-name matches `CONSUMABLE_RE` (globals.js; ammo deliberately excluded from the lexicon, the xN path carries stacks); flagged when its **HEAD NOUN** appears in the turn's player action or GM narration with no ITEM_LOST/COMPANION_ITEM_LOST in the same response (head-noun matching is load-bearing: the t582 narration said "a charge is wedged", never "Blasting charge" — full-name matching misses the real case; the looseness is priced in below). Queued (bounded 6, deduped) → `buildConsumableNudge` (api.js, 9th NOTE_BUILDERS entry): one item per turn, asks the GM to check its OWN narration and emit the battle-tested tag "or leave the sheet alone — do NOT invent a consumption" (the false-positive escape, the C-scenario-validated soft-nudge shape); cooldown latch `CONSUMABLE_NUDGE_COOLDOWN`=6 written on fire (an ignored nudge means "not spent" — re-nagging would railroad a false decrement); combat-silent WITHOUT consuming. **Pre-review (confidently resolved, recorded per the amended clause):** companions included (same class, same tags); no spend-verb gate (soft wording + cooldown bound the loose match instead); the player-driven "use item" sheet affordance deferred as complementary UX (doesn't fix the observed class — the player didn't notice the ghost either). Never auto-decrements — the only write path remains the sole parser; stable half untouched (notes ride the message); strip/doc hashes untouched (no new tag). 10 engine tests (524 total) incl. the t582 regression fixture; live spot-check on v1.308: t582 scenario queues → note renders → latch → re-fire silent. **Live validation pending:** a real session where a consumable is spent untagged — expect the nudge next turn and a correct single decrement. **#60b (v1.384) — the shipped check was FEEDING ON ITSELF; root-caused on the t881 corpus.** TLDR: the check's "not consumed" answer had nowhere to go but the story text, and the story text is what the check reads, so it kept re-triggering on its own denials. | 83 | **Long-press → tooltip on mobile (remap the native copy callout).** TLDR: on touch there is no hover, so the desktop `title` tooltips (spell #8, inventory #82) are invisible on a phone, and a long-press instead pops the browser's text-selection/copy menu — remap that gesture to show the description instead. Mechanism (feasibility confirmed 2026-07-20): suppress the native callout per-element with CSS `-webkit-touch-callout:none` + `user-select:none`, then a custom long-press detector — `touchstart` starts a ~500ms timer, cancelled by `touchmove`/`touchend`/`touchcancel`; on fire, show a custom positioned tooltip element with the description and set a flag so the follow-up `click` is suppressed (a long-press must NOT also fire the tap action, e.g. spell cast-append). Native callout killed by `.has-tip{-webkit-touch-callout:none;user-select:none}` (index.html). Capture-phase `click` swallow so a fired long-press never ALSO runs the tap action (spell cast-append). **No-data fallback (user call):** `spellTip`/`itemTip` return `"no description available for: <name>"` when there is no bible entry — every inventory item shows it today (no #81 yet), and an uncovered spell shows it (rare — most are in the bible). Three findings settled it. **(a) LiveKit does no STT itself** — transcription lives in the Agents framework, which needs an always-on server worker plus server-signed JWTs, so none of it is reachable from a static, no-build, no-npm, ES5.1 client; the whole benefit list is conditional on an architecture we don't have. **(b) Every gap it was raised to close is already closed in-repo** — the iPhone Safari path by the `MediaRecorder → whisper-1` fallback in stt.js (v1.342), fantasy proper nouns by `sttCorrectNames` (v1.330), and multiplayer by hot-seat on ONE device (#1 — there is no networking to attach voice to yet). The research lanes were briefed off a stale CLAUDE.md and spent most of their effort solving solved problems; **the CLAUDE.md `stt.js`/`tts.js` rows were corrected in the same commit as this row.** **(c) Cost inverts who pays** — today the player spends their own key; LiveKit bills the *developer* ~$0.60/player-hour in agent-session fees dominated by connection time rather than speech, reopening exactly the ownership decision #9 already settled on on-device Piper for $0. One genuine capability gap survives — coordinated turn-taking / barge-in for Car Mode — and LiveKit's own issue tracker has that closed *not planned*, so adopting it wouldn't fix it either. Re-open triggers listed in §8 of the findings doc (the honest one: if #1 ever grows real cross-device networking, the transport question is worth re-asking — but as transport, not as STT). | S (investigation) | Sonnet | 🚫 **Closed — investigated and DECLINED** (2026-07-21, user call) — see [DOC/Research/liveKit_findings.html](DOC/Research/liveKit_findings.html) |

That helps the symptom; it does not give subtasks. <br><br>**Design questions for the rework:** does a task become its own section (heading + prose + a small status table) rather than a table row, which would remove the one-physical-line constraint entirely? Does the history (the shipped-version log that makes #9 huge) belong in the task at all, or should it move to a changelog and leave the task describing only what is LEFT? That last one is probably the biggest single win and the least work. <br><br>**Constraint worth respecting:** the current format's one virtue is that it is greppable and machine-checkable, and the linter has caught real corruption from at least two writers (viewer exports, hand/model edits). | 87 | **The Piper phonemizer's reuse patch fails open into the v1.323 leak, permanently and silently.** TLDR: `tndPhonemize` (vendor/piper/vits/vits-web.js) caches ONE Emscripten phonemizer module and re-drives it via `callMain`; if any call throws it sets `tndPhon.broken = true` and falls back to building a FRESH 16MB module per synth — which is exactly the v1.323 leak the cache exists to prevent. **Nothing ever resets the latch**, so one bad sentence downgrades the rest of the page's life, and the only complaint is a `console.warn` a phone cannot show. <br><br> **Found in a lab, not the field (2026-07-22, while making ORT memory observable for B9):** the new wasm probe counted **27 phonemizer modules over a 100-synth soak** where the design allows one, with `memory access out of bounds` in the console. ⚠ **The TRIGGER is unproven on real narration** — it fired on long synthetic word-salad sentences from the soak generator, so it may be an artifact of that input. The latch, the missing reset and the silent downgrade are plain in the code regardless of what trips them. <br><br> **The field answer is already instrumented:** v1.416 puts the module count on the narration-death crumb as `pn`, so the next real crumb says whether this happens in play. **Decide on that evidence before coding.** Remedies, cheapest first: (1) reset the latch after N synths or on the next read so one fault is not permanent; (2) make the downgrade LOUD (toast + `reportError`), since a silent fallback into a known leak class violates the no-silent-failures policy; (3) find and fix what makes `callMain` fault on re-entry. ⚠ Any `vendor/piper/*` edit MUST bump `PIPER_RUNTIME_REV` (r9 today) or the permanent SW cache eats the patch. | S–M | Fable | 🚫 **CLOSED 2026-07-27 (user ruling): parked pending field evidence — no `pn`>1 crumb in 5 days of watching, and #90 (v1.435, shipped 3 days into the watch) moved connected-play synthesis to the server, shrinking the local phonemizer's trigger surface to offline reads and ladder degrades.** The lab trigger was synthetic word-salad input, always suspect. The watch outlives the row: the `pn` counter rides every narration-death crumb permanently (v1.416), so a real fault emails its own evidence in and reopens this with a case attached. Mechanism stays recorded in DOC/BUGS.md ▸ B9 (2026-07-22 entry). |

| 89 | **On a sleep, the clock should roll forward to the start of the next Day.** TLDR: the campaign clock (#73) is accurate but a night's sleep just adds its stated duration (e.g. `[TIME_ADVANCE:8h]`), so mornings land at odd offsets — the user wants a sleep to advance "however many minutes it takes to start the next Day" instead. Consequence accepted with eyes open: Days are dawn-to-dawn adventuring days, and the future C5 calendar inherits that (fine — day-counted deadlines are boundary-offset-invariant; C4's eventual time-of-day mapping = `(clock % 1440)/60 + 6`). ✅ Fork ② RATIFIED (user 2026-07-23): GM-narrated sleeps too — a sleep-marked advance (`[TIME_ADVANCE:sleep]`, one tag_table entry, engine-resolved) alongside the Rest button. ✅ Fork ③ RATIFIED (user 2026-07-23): overnight sleep ONLY — and the distinguisher IS the marker: the GM is instructed to emit `sleep` solely for a full overnight rest (naps/short rests keep duration advances like `[TIME_ADVANCE:1h]`), and the Rest button is an overnight rest by definition, so no heuristic ever guesses. Jump-safety (C3 threshold firing) already handles schedules crossed by the roll. ⚠ Clock write path = drift surface (tag_table/clock.js/buildClockBlock) — Fable-tier; the #73 subsystem is also still pending its Fable review (todo_checkWithFable #3), so this could ride that pass. | S | Fable | ✅ **Done (v1.433, 2026-07-23, built by Fable).** Better shape found during the build: the GM already HAD the overnight marker — `[REST:long]`, doc'd as "a night's sleep" — so NO new tag; the roll (`clockSleepRoll`, clock.js: next multiple of 1440, at-boundary sleeps a full day) lives in ONE site, `restSpells()`, which both the Rest button and the `[REST:long]` handler call — double-roll impossible by construction. Same-response `[TIME_ADVANCE:]` tags are ABSORBED loudly when `[REST:long]` is present (the 28h-overshoot guard; an under-advance is bounded and self-correcting, an over-advance is not). TTS sized independently) · **D3** degrade toast once per session · **D4** Kokoro benchmark-gated (→ #91) · **D5** 3GB volume. **Server half** (server repo `tts/`, deploy `cd tts && flyctl deploy --ha=false`): Hono app, `POST /api/tts` {text, voiceId, rate} → audio/wav; warm `piper` daemon per (voice, rate) — spawn-per-request pays ~1-3s model load, so daemons persist with LRU cap 3 + 10min idle kill; the `--json-input` stdin protocol's stdout path-print is the completion signal, FIFO-aligned even across timeouts (a timed-out entry stays queued so later responses can't shift); 2 timeout strikes kill+respawn the daemon; voices download HF→volume on first use behind an allowlist mirroring `PIPER_VOICES` (the endpoint can't be used to fill the volume with arbitrary models); auth = proxy `/auth/me` with a 10min memo (one auth round trip per read, not per unit — known coarseness: a logged-out token works ≤10min, the gate protects CPU, not revocation latency); same per-IP rate limiting as the game server; `TTS_TEST_SECRET` ops lever (DEV_LOGIN_SECRET pattern) for deploy smoke tests — set, verify, UNSET. **Client half** (tts.js, off drift surface): `TTS_PROVIDERS.server` + `TTS_LADDER = server → piper → native`; `getEngine()` RESOLVES (connected+healthy → "server", else "piper" — offline devices byte-identical to #9 behavior, the 3 constant-piper engine tests pass unchanged); `_speakServer` = the `_speakPiper` unit loop with predict() → fetch (same splitter/speaker-maps/manual-WAV-decode/scheduler/backpressure/shared-epoch); ANY unit failure hands the WHOLE remainder down the ladder via the queue (the governor's handoff pattern — one timeout, never a per-unit stall crawl) + 60s retry memo; `prewarmServer()` health probe wakes the auto-stopped machine off the critical path; the governor never meters the server tier (no client work to budget) and keeps guarding the local tier forever; `storageAdapter.authHeader()` exposes the assembled header, never the raw token; Voice Settings shows a server-tier status line (off/active/degraded-with-reason). **Tests:** 810 green (+5 server-tier engine tests: resolution, D1 no-token, degrade window + backdated retry, D3 single toast, enqueue shape) + a 4-point SERVER TTS source contract in run-tests.js (zero-wasm, no governor contact, remainder handoff, ladder order). **v1.436 field fix (same day, from the user's first phone session):** the 🔋 governor latched because Fly parked the machine after ~2min idle and real turns idle longer — so nearly every read's first unit paid the measured 10.17s cold chain (machine boot + auth proxy cold-starting the GAME server too + daemon spawn + model load), 170ms past the 10s unit timeout → whole read degraded to local Piper → budget spent. Four-part close: ① fly.toml `auto_stop_machines = "suspend"` (RAM-intact sub-second resume — warm daemons + auth memo survive); ② `TTS.prewarmServer()` on the send gesture (game.js, beside the B10 recoverAudio tap) so the machine wakes while the GM is thinking; ③ first-unit timeout 15s (`SERVER_TTS_TIMEOUT_FIRST_MS`; units 2+ stay 10s); ④ diagnosability — ▶ Test auditions via the server tier when it's up (a local Test booted wasm + spent governor budget for nothing; no eviction prompt either, nothing downloads), and a connected page reading below the server tier now stamps a `tts-server-skip` crumb + console.info (silent-local reads were climbing the budget with no signal — the no-silent-failures gap). Contracts ⑤⑥ pin the Test routing + the send-tap prewarm. |
| 91 | **Server-TTS M2 — Kokoro-82M behind the same endpoint (quality upgrade, benchmark-gated).** TLDR: with M1 (server Piper, #90) live, the same `POST /api/tts` can serve Kokoro-82M (Apache-2.0) — markedly better than Piper mediums, ~24-26 English voice packs, voice BLENDING for per-character voices. **📄 SPEC'D 2026-07-24: [DOC/Research/DOC_kokoro.html](DOC/Research/DOC_kokoro.html)** — decisions K1-K6: **K1** runtime = in-process `kokoro-js` in a worker_thread (no daemon protocol, quant ladder fp32→q4; python sidecar only as fallback) · **K2** the voice id IS the engine selector (`kk_` prefix routes; allowlist discipline as M1; blend syntax `kk_mix:` reserved day one, mixer UI deferred to M2.5) · **K3** the real client work: tier-aware voice resolution (`voiceTier`/`narratorVoice(tier)`/`characterVoiceId(char,tier)`) so a server-only `kk_` id can never reach local `predict()`, the ladder handoff converts to the piper narrator, and the snap guard can't eat a kk_ campaign pin — the one drift-adjacent surface (speaker-map resolution), annotate for /fable-review · **K4** the D4 benchmark concretized: `tts/bench-kokoro.mjs` on the LIVE box via fly ssh, 30 real-length units × q8/fp16/fp32, pass = sustained median RTF ≥1× at an ear-checked quant, with the piper daemon as the comparison row · **K5** box sizing decided BY the benchmark (stay 1GB w/ piper LRU 3→2 / 2GB ≈ +$5/mo / performance-1x needs explicit ratification) · **K6** curated en-only catalog via a voice_picker pass, gender-tagged from day one (the #9⑨ lesson). Milestones M2.0 benchmark → M2.1 server → M2.2 client → M2.3 field validation (default flip = user call) → M2.5 blending. **D4 (ratified): ships only after the benchmark passes.** <br><br> ⛔ **M2.0 RAN 2026-07-24 — D4 GATE FAILED, Kokoro does NOT ship.** Measured on the live box: at 1GB (the shipped config) the process **OOM-died before its first synthesis** — Kokoro needs ~275MB RSS and the box had ~119MB free with the session's three warm Piper daemons resident. Scaled temporarily to 2GB (K5's candidate tier) it ran, and the SPEED is the disqualifier: **RTF ≈ 2.1 at full burst** (D4 bar is ≤1.0), then a step-change collapse to **RTF 22.65 → 24.67 → 33.68** — one 7-second line took **3m56s**. Mechanism (evidence-backed): ~48s of continuous 100% CPU exhausts the `shared-cpu-1x` **burst balance**, then the machine throttles to baseline; it is a step, not a ramp, and loadavg stayed busy throughout, so it is not memory/GC. Piper never trips it at RTF 0.16. **RAM was a red herring; CPU is the wall** — no RAM tier fixes it, and even the best-case number is **13× Piper's cost per second of audio**. Box restored to 1GB, volume reclaimed, `tts/bench-kokoro.mjs` kept for re-runs. **K1/K2/K3/K6 remain valid and rebuildable** the day the CPU math changes (a faster quantization, a cheaper build, or ratified dedicated-CPU spend). Full record + the confirming burst test: [DOC/Research/DOC_kokoro.html](DOC/Research/DOC_kokoro.html). <br><br> ➡ **The benchmark surfaced a better route — see #94:** Piper's own **"high" voices are already in the shipped catalog** (`en_US-libritts-high` is ALREADY on the server volume, plus lessac/ryan/cori-high) — same engine, same daemons, zero architecture change. A high model costs more CPU than a medium (unmeasured) but the headroom is enormous: it could be **6× slower than medium and still clear the D4 bar**, where Kokoro needed to get 2× *faster*. Next step is one cheap measurement: point the same harness at the piper daemon, medium vs high, same 30-unit corpus. | M | Any (K3 → Fable review) | 🚫 **CLOSED 2026-07-27 (user ruling): hardware-gated, all avenues exhausted.** The D4 benchmark failed on CPU physics, not code — RTF ≈2.1 best-case then burst-throttle collapse on shared-cpu-1x; no quant/RAM tier fixes it. Investigation complete, K1-K6 remain rebuildable in DOC/Research/DOC_kokoro.html if dedicated-CPU spend is ever ratified. The quality-upgrade thread lives on as #94 (Piper high voices) |

Protocol = whole-list LWW with rev-marker change detection (`tnd_speaker_stars_rev_v1` per origin), no tombstones: **speaker_browser is the bench's sole editor** (debounced 1.5s push on every star/label/remove/import, loud starioNote on push failure; boot pull adopts on rev change or seeds an empty cloud); **the game is a reader** (`storageAdapter.syncSpeakerStars` on autoConnect + fresh login — adopt/seed only, never edits). `speakerStarsPlan` is the pure decision core: never adopts a rev>0 row whose value isn't an array (corrupt cloud can't blank a device), corrupt local JSON = "no bench" (can't seed garbage, can't block adopt). Verified: 7 plan engine tests (835 green) + contract pins for all wiring call sites; LIVE round trip against a local server through the REAL page and the REAL adapter (curl-seeded bench → fresh-device boot adopted 2 stars rev 3 → in-page unstar pushed rev 4 → node-run `syncSpeakerStars` adopted rev 4); server failure matrix curl-verified (401/400/404/413/rev increments). Deployed: Fly game server + v1.444 client. Mechanism: `DEFAULT_SPEAKER_STARS` served by `starsList()` (tts.js) and `loadStars()` (speaker_browser.html) ONLY when `tnd_speaker_stars_v1` was NEVER WRITTEN on that origin — a stored `"[]"` means deliberately cleared and STAYS empty; corrupt stores still yield `[]` (never mask corruption with defaults). The store stays unwritten until the first real edit, so a cloud adopt is unhindered; a connected new user's browser boot seeds their cloud with the defaults (rev 0 + non-empty local = the existing #95.5 seed path, unchanged). The cast is DUPLICATED in tts.js + the self-contained satellite by necessity — the **DEFAULT BENCH CONTRACT** (run-tests.js) asserts the two copies byte-identical + shape-valid (sabotage-verified: one drifted label fails the build). Verified: 838 green (determinism/decline/assignment-wins/label-fallback + contract g-cases + gendered-defaults pin); live: 52 ⚥ selects, a flip persists g without disturbing neighbors, zero console errors. | 103 | **Bible editor: a save refused for a good reason read as a crash, and merged additions never cleared.** TLDR: the user's Save died with a raw DOMException ("state cached in an interface object ... changed since it was read from disk") and the header kept reporting 4 capability additions that were already shipped. Two separate defects, both self-inflicted by a normal day's work. | S | Any | ✅ **SHIPPED v1.481 (2026-07-28).** **① The save was CORRECT to refuse.** The FSA API blocks `createWritable()` when the file changed on disk after the handle read it — here because the session's own commits rewrote class_bible.js while the editor tab held an older view. The browser was refusing to let a stale editor clobber a newer file, which is exactly right; the raw wording just explained none of it, and the old handler then fell back to a download as if the write had merely failed. Now that case is detected (`InvalidStateError` / the message probe) and stated in the user's terms — what happened, why the browser refused, and the only correct recovery — with NO misleading download fallback. **② `↻ Reload from disk`** (user request): re-reads the open file through the existing handle, confirming first if the tab has unsaved edits. It is the named recovery for ①, and the general answer to a file that moved underneath a long editor session. Explains itself when there is no handle rather than throwing. **③ `⌫ Clear merged (N)`**: additions whose name now RESOLVES in the shipped bible are dead weight — the define-form flow exists only to author MISSING capabilities — but nothing ever removed them, so the count reported 4 forever (3 had shipped at v1.468). The button appears only when N>0, names them in its tooltip, confirms, and **keeps additions not yet in the bible**. Deliberately NOT an auto-prune: silently deleting authored data is the worse failure. 894 green. Live-verified against the user's exact four staged entries restored through a real draft: 4 → 1 (fog bank/flash/leap cleared, poisoner kept), tooltip correct, button hides, **the cleanup survives a page reload** (the debounced draft write was checked explicitly — an unpersisted clear would have reproduced the original "it's still there" complaint), no-handle reload explains itself, zero console errors. <br><br>**v1.485 — THE SAVE ACTUALLY WORKS NOW (user: "I have not yet been able to save from the bible editor. It's always blocked somehow").** v1.481 EXPLAINED the refusal but gave no way THROUGH it, and this session then rewrote class_bible.js on ~10 commits, re-staling the handle within minutes of every recovery — explaining a wall repeatedly is not fixing it. **`saveBible` now re-reads the file through the handle BEFORE writing.** Two jobs in one read: it refreshes the cached snapshot the browser was rejecting (so the common case just works), and it compares the file's mtime against the one observed at load, so a real disk change is detected UP FRONT where it can be explained and **chosen about** (overwrite with your version · cancel and reload) instead of surfacing as an opaque `InvalidStateError` after the fact. mtime is captured on every load path (open/reconnect/reload) and re-baselined after a successful write. **`diskChangedSinceLoad` is extracted between markers and pinned by a new SAVE STALENESS CONTRACT** — and writing that test CHANGED THE CODE: the comparison is `!==`, not `>`, because a sync client or `git checkout` can restore an **older** timestamp and `>` would sail past it and clobber. Three clauses, all sabotage-proven. **Also fixed a silent-data-loss path found while writing the recovery advice:** `openBible` replaced the tab's contents with NO guard, so opening a file with unsaved edits destroyed them wordlessly (the same class the reconnect path already guarded) — it now confirms and points at Download copy first; the contract pins the guard. If a save still fails after the re-read the handle is genuinely beyond use (the file was REPLACED rather than edited in place), and the message now says so and names the one thing that always works: re-open the file, minting a fresh handle inside the user's own click. 895 green. ⚠ **HONEST LIMIT:** the FSA round-trip cannot be driven from the harness (the picker is an OS dialog needing a real user gesture) — the logic is pinned and the mechanism sound, but **end-to-end confirmation is the user's next save**. |

**Tier** (which model the task *needs* — how smart, where Effort is how long): **Fable** = architecture / high judgment / expensive-to-get-wrong · **Opus** = substantial builds with real design decisions · **Sonnet** = well-specified builds · **Any** = mechanical work or real-device/play testing · — = done/merged (needs nothing)
